# Sri Chaitanya-chandramrita

*"The Nectar of the Moon Chaitanya"*

*Prabodhananda Sarasvati*

*Translated from the Sanskrit*

*Sanskrit text, transliteration, word-for-word translation and the "Gaudiya-bhashya" of Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati (translated from the Bengali)*

---

## Translator's note

The *Sri Chaitanya-chandramrita* is a Sanskrit collection of a little over a hundred verses glorifying Sri
Chaitanya Mahaprabhu. Its author is Prabodhananda Sarasvati, a contemporary of Mahaprabhu who later lived in
Vrindavana (tradition regards him as the uncle and teacher of Gopala Bhatta Gosvami). The book is divided
into twelve sections: praise, obeisance, benediction, the glory of Gaura's devotees, censure of the
non-devotees, humility, fidelity to the object of worship, instruction to the world, the supremacy of
Chaitanya, the glory of His descent, the beauty and dance of Gaura, and finally lament over His
disappearance.

**Source.** The Sanskrit text is taken from the edition *Sri Chaitanya-chandramritam tatha Sri
Sangita-madhavam*, Mathura, 1951 (ed. Baba Krishnadasa, with a Hindi translation by Gosvami Krishna
Chaitanya; eGangotri scan at archive.org). The verse numbering follows that edition (142 verses and one
verse in brackets without a number). Poorly recognized passages have been checked against the Bengali
edition with the translation of Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati (which has 143 verses: one verse stands in a
different place, so from the seventh section on its numbers are one higher). **Only Prabodhananda's
Sanskrit text has been translated.** The Hindi and Bengali translations and commentaries of the editions
helped to make out corrupt passages but are not reproduced.

**Verification.** After the first translation the Sanskrit text was restored line by line from both
editions (checking the metre and relying on Bhaktisiddhanta's word-for-word analysis), and the translation
of every verse was compared afresh with it; places where the translation departed from Bhaktisiddhanta's
interpretation in meaning were corrected according to him. The English translation was made from the
Sanskrit, in agreement with the Russian translation (same numbering and notes).

---

**Full version.** For each verse are given: the Sanskrit text in Devanagari, restored from the 1951 edition
and Bhaktisiddhanta's Bengali edition (where they differ, Bhaktisiddhanta's reading is preferred; this is our
own corrected text, not a reprint of any single edition); an IAST transliteration; a word-for-word
translation — the complete translation of Bhaktisiddhanta's *anvaya* (word-for-word analysis) in his word
order (words he supplies as implied are in square brackets); and the translation.

After each verse follows a translation of the "Gaudiya-bhashya" of Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati (1874–1937) —
his Bengali translation and commentary from the Gaudiya Math edition (Calcutta, 1st ed.); his preface to that
edition is given at the beginning of the book. For each verse the summary heading and the continuous
commentary (*anuvada*) are translated; glosses from the word-for-word analysis that are not in the continuous
text appear in the *anvaya* above. The Bengali text is known from an OCR copy; illegible places are marked
"[…]". The verse numbers follow the 1951 edition; in the Bengali edition verse 17 is repeated once more as
No. 75, so from No. 75 on its numbers are one higher. The commentary and the *anvaya* were translated into
English from the Bengali, in agreement with the Russian translation.

---

## Contents

- Preface by Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati
- Part One — Praise. Obeisance. Benediction. The glory of Sri Chaitanya's devotees (verses 1–28)
- Part Two — Censure of the non-devotees. Humility (verses 29–56)
- Part Three — Fidelity to the object of worship. Instruction to the world (verses 57–98)
- Part Four — The supremacy of Sri Chaitanya. The glory of His descent. The beauty and dance of Gaura. Lament (verses 99–142)

---

# Preface by Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati

*"Sri Sri Chaitanya-chandramritam" and "Sri Sri Navadvipa-shatakam," composed by the crest-jewel of the tridandi-gosvamis, the wandering acharya, the foremost of Gaura's associates, Srila Prabodhananda Sarasvati Gosvamipada — with the "Gaudiya-bhashya" and Bengali translation of Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati, which refute all false conclusions. Published by Sri Atindranatha Devasharma Bandyopadhyaya, Gaudiya Math (1, Ultadingi Junction Road, Calcutta). First edition; offering — one and a half rupees. Printed by Ananta Vasudeva Brahmachari, B.A., at the Gaudiya Printing Works (243/2, Upper Circular Road, Calcutta).*

## About the author

At the beginning of the year 1433 of the Shaka era Sri Gaurasundara, under the pretext of a pilgrimage, distributed mercy to the devotees in South India. Setting out from Nilachala in the province of Utkala, He first came to the confluence of the Godavari with the sea and then visited many holy places of the present Madras Presidency. On the bright *ekadashi* of the month of […] Sri Mahaprabhu arrived at […]. Seeing that […] had come, the Lord […], following the rule of the *dashanami* sannyasis, resolved to spend four months at the holy place of Sri Ranganatha. Vaishnavas […] lived there. The adherence of the *sampradaya* Vaishnavas of the South to righteous conduct […]. In the villages of the South where genuine Vaishnavas [live], the *smarta* brahmanas do not find it convenient to live. […] was at that time a holy place served exclusively by Sri Vaishnavas. Therefore Sri Mahaprabhu spent four months among righteous Vaishnavas who had taken shelter of devotion to Vishnu, and, taking *darshana* of Sri Ranganatha and preaching the topics of Krishna, He instructed the living beings. At that time three brothers — Tirumalla, Venkata and Gopala-guru — who had come from the province of Mysore, lived in Srirangam. In fact they were natives of Andhra, or the Northern province. Sri Mahaprabhu, greatly pleased with this brahmana family, spent four months in their house. The son of the middle brother, Venkata, then in his boyhood (*pauganda*), is the famous Sri Gopala Bhatta, one of the six Gosvamis.

The Vaishnavas of the Sri-sampradaya love the worship of Sri Lakshmi-Narayana. By the inner mercy of Sri Mahaprabhu this Bhatta family became expert in attaining Krishna-rasa. Although we do not know much about Sri Tirumalla, it can be understood that Sri Chaitanya was his life as well.

Sri Venkata's conversation with Sri Mahaprabhu is described in the *Sri Chaitanya-charitamrita* (Madhya-lila, ninth chapter). Sri Prabodhananda's attachment to Sri Chaitanya was incomparable. Through the influence of Sri Prabodhananda's good instruction […]'s son Sri Gopala Bhatta attained the position of acharya of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas. Among the servants of Sri Chaitanya the place of Sri Prabodhananda is very high. Sri Kavi-karnapura in his *Sri Gaura-ganoddesha-dipika* has proclaimed Sri Prabodhananda Sarasvati to be Tungavidya in the pastimes of Sri Krishna.[^00-1] In the *Sri Hari-bhakti-vilasa* […] it is written that Sri Gopala Bhatta, the disciple of Srila Prabodhananda, who is dear to the Lord, composed the *Sri Hari-bhakti-vilasa* to please Sri Rupa, Sri Sanatana and Sri Raghunatha Dasa.[^00-2] In the *Sri Bhakti-ratnakara* it is written:

> Some say: great are the virtues of Prabodhananda; […] he became renowned as "Sarasvati."

> The complete Brahman […] Bhagavan — is dear to him; apart from Him, [Prabodhananda knows] nothing else even in a dream.

> Supreme renunciation, love, […] enchanting; a great poet, matchless in song, instrumental music and dance.

> Hearing his words, everyone's joy increases; boundless is the great glory of Prabodhananda.

After Sri Mahaprabhu returned to Nilachala, within a few years Sri Prabodhananda Sarasvati, having entered deeply into the worship hidden in the heart of Sri Krishna-Chaitanya, leaving […] for the sake of the desired *bhajana* […], without delay, under the shelter of Sri Gaura's feet, settled in Kamyavana in the district of Mathura. In Sri Gopala Bhatta too the desire to live in Vraja gradually awoke; later he also followed in the footsteps of his paternal uncle.

Many people ask: if Sri Prabodhananda Sarasvati was so dear to Sri Gauranga, why did […] Kaviraja Gosvami not record his life and glories for the pleasure of the readers who are devotees of Sri Gaura? […] the writing of the *Sri Bhakti-ratnakara* seems quite sufficient. Its author, Sri Ghanashyama Sri Narahari Chakravarti, says:

> These accounts of Sri Gopala Bhatta some describe in part, others do not describe at all.

> Whoever, without understanding the heart of this, […] — in his heart the seed of offence takes root.

> The supreme *rasikas*, the earlier poets, though able to describe it, did not describe it.

> They left a description only here and there; […] the poets for this reason.

> Sri Gopala Bhatta, pleased, gave the order […], forbidding them to describe what concerned himself.

> Why did he forbid it — who can understand that? He always regards himself as most lowly.

> Kaviraja could not transgress his order.

Some say that in the words written by Sri Prabodhananda support for the doctrine of *svakiya* is seen, […], seeing the superiority of *parakiya-bhajana*, […] says little about Srila Sarasvati Gosvami Prabhu. Be that as it may, all devotees who follow […], impartial like Sri Narahari Dasa, are fortunate; so let them, like him, leaving aside […], ever relish Sri Prabodhananda's pure following of Gaura and the sweetness of *parakiya* service to Sri Vrindavaneshvari.

Sri Prabodhananda's moods are supremely clear; in his language […] and sweetness are present at the same time. All Vaishnavas who have taken shelter at the feet of Sri Chaitanya obtain incomparable joy by daily reading Sri Prabodhananda's *Sri Vrindavana-shataka*. The *Sri Navadvipa-shataka* composed by him, like the *Sri Vrindavana-shataka*, also […]. Sri Prabodhananda's poem *Sri Radha-rasa-sudhanidhi* is truly incomparable in the world. Although an ordinary reader fond of poetry may not experience […] in reading this book, it is supremely dear to devotees whose hearts have been softened by Hari-rasa. Therefore how highly [it] is valued depends on differences of taste, on the reader's *sukriti*, and […] the transcendental moods rooted in the *rasa* of Vraja […]. He also has a book called *Viveka-shataka*; it is mentioned in the work of Professor Aufrecht, and the late Ramadasa Sen Mahashaya of Baharampur has seen this book.

The book *Sri Chaitanya-chandramrita* has been published […]. Even the opponents of Sri Gaura, on reading it, will feel the purity of their hearts. And it goes without saying that the followers of Sri Gaura, reading it, will be immersed in supreme bliss in an indescribable ocean of happiness. Those for whom the Lord of Goloka Himself became the object of service for four months, and to whom He granted […] — they possess an inexhaustible treasury of […], and the little circle of living beings certainly cherishes the hope of obtaining some part of it.

Some try to identify Prabodhananda, the foremost of Vaishnavas, with Prakashananda, the *mayavadi* of Kashi; but we can in no way believe their words. For about the *mayavadi* sannyasi of Kashi named Prakashananda it is written thus in the *Sri Chaitanya-bhagavata*, […], third chapter:

> Thus in Navadvipa […] Vishvambhara floats in the bliss of *bhakti* together with all His followers.

> One day, hearing a verse about the mood of the Boar, He went roaring of His own accord to the house of Murari.

> Pleased with Gupta's words, the Lord, the Boar, angry with the Vedas, says in reply:

> "As if I had no hands, no feet, no face, no eyes — so the Vedas [describe] Me, […].

> That fellow Prakashananda who teaches in Kashi — that fellow cuts My body to pieces.

> He explains that the Vedas do not accept My form; leprosy has struck his whole body, and still he does not understand.

> […] My body, which is supremely pure, whose deeds Aja (Brahma), Bhava (Shiva) and the others sing,

> by touching which piety and purity themselves become pure — that fellow calls it 'false.' What audacity!"

This event took place between the years 1425 and 1430 of the Shaka era. Sri Mahaprabhu, auspiciously arriving at Srirangam in 1433 Shaka, saw Sri Prabodhananda-pada among the three brothers. They were then […] Vaishnavas of the Sri-sampradaya, that is, adherents of *vishishta-advaita*, servants of the eternal form of Sri Narayana; while Prakashananda was then the foremost of the servants of the *mayavada* founded by Shankara. To try to make these two persons "one," or to equate them, is sheer madness.

In the twentieth chapter of the *Sri Chaitanya-bhagavata* this too is said about Prakashananda:

> As He was speaking, the Lord was filled with the mood of Ishvara; gnashing His teeth, He says emphatically:

> "The sannyasi Prakashananda lives in Kashi; that fellow thoroughly cuts My […] to pieces.

> He teaches Vedanta and does not accept My form; […] I have struck his body with leprosy, and still he does not understand.

> The body in which countless […] rest — that fellow calls it 'false.' What audacity!

> I tell you the truth, Murari: you are My servant. Whoever does not accept My body […] goes to ruin.

> True are My pastimes and deeds, true is My abode; calling them 'false,' he cuts Me to pieces.

> The glory by hearing which ignorance is destroyed today — those pastimes the sinful teacher calls 'false.'

> Whoever disregards Him whose glory is so holy will never know […] My descent."

Sri Prakashananda was the […] of the *ekadandi* sannyasis of Shankara's *sampradaya* of that time; while Sri Prabodhananda was a Ramanujiya *tridandi-jiyar-svami*, who had come from the land of Mysore and lived in Ranga-kshetra. Prakashananda was a *mayavadi* living in Kashi, while Prabodhananda was a […] living in Kamyavana. One was a resident of Aryavarta, the other a Vaishnava of the South; one was a *nirvishesha-vadi* (an advocate of the impersonal), the other […] a *savishesha-vadi* (an advocate of the personal), and later an adherent of the doctrine of *achintya-bhedabheda*. One, an opponent of Vishnu [and the Vaishnavas], became a devotee only after being delivered; the other is an eternally perfect associate of Gaura and the guru of the Vaishnava acharya Sri Gopala Bhatta Gosvami. To call and […] the most worshipful paternal uncle and guru of Sri Gopala Bhatta Gosvami not the crest-jewel of eternally perfect devotees but a hater of Vishnu and the Vaishnavas, a *mayavadi* and [formerly] a conditioned soul is a terrible offence against a Vaishnava, leading to hell.

In the *Sri Chaitanya-charitamrita*, in the twenty-fifth chapter of the Madhya-lila and in the seventh chapter of the Adi-lila, only the *mayavadi* Prakashananda is spoken of. It is incomprehensible how a person who from 1425 to 1430 Shaka was a *mayavadi* could in 1433 Shaka be in the South […] a Ramanujiya, and then in 1435 Shaka […] become a *mayavadi* again. Therefore the attempt to identify Prakashananda with Sri Prabodhananda shows only extreme ignorance. […] Such a tendency to uproot traditions is no small sorrow. Because Sri Prabodhananda Sarasvati, moved by his […] and humility, forbade through Sri Gopala Bhatta the discussion of his personal life […], and […] Kaviraja Gosvami did not transgress his order — this calamity has now arisen. Had Srila Prabodhananda known that after his disappearance, in order to count him among the offenders against Vishnu and the Vaishnavas, this terrible whirlpool of error would arise in the future, he […] would not have forbidden Sri Kaviraja Gosvami in that way. The readers of the *Bhakti-ratnakara* will understand this. About Srila Prabodhananda it is written thus in the *Bhakti-ratnakara*:

> […], Venkata and Prabodhananda — the treasure of the life of the three brothers is Gaurachandra.

> These three brothers, worshippers of Lakshmi-Narayana, by the Lord's […] became intoxicated with the *rasa* of Radha-Krishna.

> Tirumalla, Venkata, Prabodhananda — all three reflect: "How shall we live without the Lord?

> Who will joke with us? Who will take us along to bathe in the Kaveri?"

> After four months the Lord took His leave; the three brothers weep aloud.

> The Lord, […] the three brothers, spoke to them many words of consolation.

> Some say: great are the virtues of Prabodhananda, […] he became renowned as the sannyasi "Sarasvati."

> […] Sri Krishna-Chaitanya Bhagavan — is dear to him; apart from Him, [he knows] nothing else even in a dream.

In the catalogue of the esteemed Professor Aufrecht a book called *Sri Sangita-madhava* is listed among the works of Srila Prabodhananda Sarasvati. We have obtained this book. It has been published in full in the journal *Sri Sajjana-toshani*, from issue […] to issue 12 of its 18th year.

The householder Vaishnavas of the "Sri" *sampradaya*, when leaving home, never take *ekadanda* sannyasa; they all take *tridanda* sannyasa and are known as Ramanujiya *svamis*. Some, having studied Srila Prabodhananda's *Sri Chaitanya-chandramrita*, conclude that he was a "*brahma*-sannyasi," but in the absence of definite evidence, accepting this raises many difficulties.

*[On the following leaves of the edition there is an illustration, "Sri Gauranga in the mood of Sri Radha," with the caption "Radha-bhava-dyuti-suvalitam naumi krishna-svarupam" ("I bow to Krishna in His own form, adorned with the mood and lustre of Radha"), provided by courtesy of […] Bhakti-visharada Mahashaya, and alphabetical indexes of the verses of the "Chaitanya-chandramrita" and the "Navadvipa-shataka."]*

## Contents

1. Indication of the subject (*vastu-nirdesha*) — verses 1–7
2. Obeisance (*namaskara*) — verses 8–12
3. Benediction (*ashir-vada*) — verses 13–17
4. The glory of Sri Chaitanya's devotees — verses 18–30
5. Censure of those not devoted to Sri Chaitanya — verses 31–45
6. Censure in the form of humility (*dainya*) — verses 46–56
7. Fidelity to the object of worship — verses 57–79
8. Instruction to the world (*loka-shiksha*) — verses 80–99
9. The supremacy of Sri Chaitanya — verses 100–109
10. The glory of the descent — verses 110–130
11. The beauty, jubilation, dance and so on of Sri Gaura — verses 131–136
12. Lament (*shochaka*) — verses 137–143

[^00-1]: "She who in Vraja was Tungavidya, learned in all the *shastras*, is now the ascetic Prabodhananda, a Sarasvati in singing of Gaura" (*Sri Gaura-ganoddesha-dipika*, 163).

[^00-2]: "[…] Gopala Bhatta, the disciple of Prabodhananda, who is dear to the Lord, compiles the *Bhakti-vilasa* to please Raghunatha Dasa, and also Rupa and Sanatana" (*Sri Hari-bhakti-vilasa*, 1st *vilasa*, verse 2).

# Part One

*Praise. Obeisance. Benediction. The glory of Sri Chaitanya's devotees (verses 1–28)*

## Praise (stuti)

### Verse 1

स्तुमस्तं चैतन्याकृतिमतिविमर्यादपरमा-
द्भुतौदार्यं वर्यं व्रजपतिकुमारं रसयितुम् ।
विशुद्धस्वप्रेमोन्मदमधुरपीयूषलहरीं
प्रदातुं चान्येभ्यः परपदनवद्वीपप्रकटम् ॥ १ ॥

*stumastaṁ caitanyākṛtimativimaryādaparamā-*
*dbhutaudāryaṁ varyaṁ vrajapatikumāraṁ rasayitum |*
*viśuddhasvapremonmadamadhurapīyūṣalaharīṁ*
*pradātuṁ cānyebhyaḥ parapadanavadvīpaprakaṭam || 1 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* vrajapati-kumaram — [to give a taste] to Himself, non-different from the son of the lord of Vraja; vishuddha-sva-prema-unmada-madhura-piyusha-laharim — the sweet nectar waves in the form of joy and other feelings, rising from His own pure *prema*; rasayitum — to give a taste; anyebhyah ca — and to others; pradatum — to bestow; [yah — He who]; para-pada-navadvipa-prakatam — appeared in the supreme abode called Navadvipa, the seat of the ninefold *bhakti*; tam — Him; varyam — the best of all *avataras*; ati-vimaryada-parama-adbhuta-audaryam — the embodiment of boundless and supremely wonderful mercy; caitanya-akritim — the person named Sri Krishna Chaitanya; [vayam — we, the Gaudiya *sampradaya*]; stumah — praise.

**1.** We praise the best of all — Him who has the form of Chaitanya, whose generosity is boundless and supremely wonderful; who appeared in the supreme abode of Navadvipa to give the son of the lord of Vraja — Himself — a taste of the nectar waves of His own pure, maddening, sweet love, and to distribute them to others.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Praise of Gaurahari, who, to taste His own sweetness and to distribute prema, accepted the mood and lustre of the ashraya (Radha) and appeared in Navadvipa.*

>> To give Himself, non-different from the son of the lord of Vraja, a taste of the nectar waves of sweet feelings such as joy and the rest, rising from the ocean of His own most pure *prema*, and to distribute them to others, He appeared in the supreme abode called "Sri Navadvipa," the seat of the ninefold *bhakti*. That person named Sri Krishna Chaitanya — the best of all *avataras*, the embodiment of boundless and supremely wonderful mercy — we praise.

### Verse 2

धर्मास्पृष्टः सततपरमाविष्ट एवात्यधर्मे
दृष्टिं प्राप्तो न हि खलु सतां सृष्टिषु क्वापि नो सन् ।
यद्दत्तश्रीहरिरससुधास्वादमत्तः प्रनृत्य-
त्युच्चैर्गायत्यथ विलुठति स्तौमि तं कञ्चिदीशम् ॥ २ ॥

*dharmāspṛṣṭaḥ satataparamāviṣṭa evātyadharme*
*dṛṣṭiṁ prāpto na hi khalu satāṁ sṛṣṭiṣu kvāpi no san |*
*yaddattaśrīharirasasudhāsvādamattaḥ pranṛtya-*
*tyuccairgāyatyatha viluṭhati staumi taṁ kañcidīśam || 2 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* [yah janah — the man who]; dharma-asprishtah — untouched by *dharma*, that is, in whom there is not a drop of piety and the like; ati-adharme — in great sin; satata-parama-avishtah eva — constantly, wholly immersed; na hi khalu satam drishtim praptah — of whom it is known that he has not received the merciful glance of the saints; samsrishtishu kvapi ca no san — and has not stayed in any place connected [with the saints]; [tadrisha janah — even such a man]; yat-datta-shri-hari-rasa-sudha-asvada-mattah [san] — intoxicated by tasting the nectar of the loving *rasa* bestowed by Him: "Sri" means Srimati Radhika, the source of all beauty, and "Hari" [Sri Krishna]; intoxicated by tasting the nectar of Their *prema*; pranrityati — dances exceedingly; uccaih gayati — sings aloud; atha viluthati ca — and rolls on the ground; tam kancit isham — that powerful, indescribable person; staumi — I praise.

**2.** A man never touched by *dharma*, always sunk in lawlessness, who has never come into the sight of the saints and has never been anywhere among them — intoxicated by the taste of the nectar of Sri-Hari-rasa, the *rasa* of the love of Radha and Krishna, which this Lord has bestowed, he dances, sings aloud and rolls on the ground. I praise this indescribable Lord.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Praise of Gaurahari, who by the causeless shower of His mercy bestows the intoxication of prema even on one who has abandoned dharma.*

>> One whom *dharma* has never touched, that is, in whom there is not a trace of piety; who is always sunk in great sin; who has never received the merciful glance of the saints and has never stayed in any holy place established by the righteous and inaccessible to sin — even such a great sinner, intoxicated by the taste of the nectar of the loving *rasa* of Radha and Krishna which He bestowed, He makes dance, sing aloud and roll on the ground. That powerful, indescribable person I praise.

### Verse 3

यन्नाप्तं कर्मनिष्ठैर्न च समधिगतं यत्तपोध्यानयोगै-
र्वैराग्यैस्त्यागतत्त्वस्तुतिभिरपि न यत्तर्कितञ्चापि कैश्चित् ।
गोविन्दप्रेमभाजामपि न च कलितं यद्रहस्यं स्वयं त-
न्नाम्नैव प्रादुरासीदवतरति परे यत्र तं नौमि गौरम् ॥ ३ ॥

*yannāptaṁ karmaniṣṭhairna ca samadhigataṁ yattapodhyānayogai-*
*rvairāgyaistyāgatattvastutibhirapi na yattarkitañcāpi kaiścit |*
*govindapremabhājāmapi na ca kalitaṁ yadrahasyaṁ svayaṁ ta-*
*nnāmnaiva prādurāsīdavatarati pare yatra taṁ naumi gauram || 3 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* karma-nishthaih janaih — by people attached to *karma*, that is, [the *karmis*]; yat — that which; na aptam — is not obtained; tapo-dhyana-yogaih ca — and by austerity, meditation, yoga and the like; yat na samadhigatam — that which no one can realize; kaishcit api [janaih] vairagyaih tyaga-tattva-stutibhih api yat na tarkitam ca — that which no one can perceive through renunciation, abandonment of *karma*, [knowledge of the truth], [the reciting of hymns of praise] and the like; govinda-prema-bhajam api [jananam] — even of people full of love for Sri Krishna; na ca yat kalitam — that which is not obtained; tat rahasyam — that secret *prema*; yatra pare avatarati [sati] — when the Supreme, Sri Gauranga, descends; namna eva — through *nama-sankirtana* alone; svayam pradur asit — manifested of its own accord; tam gauram — that Gaurasundara; naumi — I praise.

**3.** That secret which those devoted to ritual did not attain, which was not won by austerity, meditation and yoga, which no one even guessed at in spite of renunciation, abandonment of the world, knowledge of the truth and hymns of praise, which even those who love Govinda did not know — that revealed itself of its own accord through the Holy Name alone, when the Supreme descended. I bow to that Gaura.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Praise of Gaurahari, the giver of the secret prema unattainable even by the devotees of Krishna.*

>> That which people devoted to ritual action (*karma*) cannot obtain; that which no one can realize by the power of austerity, meditation and the eightfold yoga; that which no one is able to perceive even through renunciation, abandonment of works, knowledge of the truth, the reciting of hymns of praise and the like; more than that, that which is unattainable even by devotees dedicated to the loving service of Sri Govinda (that is, unattainable even by the devotees of the Nimbarka *sampradaya*, who are dedicated to loving service in the *svakiya* relationship and lack skill in understanding *parakiya-rasa*) — that secret *prema*, upon His appearance, revealed itself of its own accord through *nama-kirtana* alone. That Gaurasundara I praise.

### Verse 4

दृष्टः स्पृष्टः कीर्तितः संस्मृतो वा
दूरस्थैरप्यानतो वादृतो वा ।
प्रेम्णः सारं दातुमीशो य एकः
श्रीचैतन्यं नौमि देवं दयालुम् ॥ ४ ॥

*dṛṣṭaḥ spṛṣṭaḥ kīrtitaḥ saṁsmṛto vā*
*dūrasthairapyānato vādṛto vā |*
*premṇaḥ sāraṁ dātumīśo ya ekaḥ*
*śrīcaitanyaṁ naumi devaṁ dayālum || 4 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* yah — He who; drishtah sprishtah kirtitah [san] — becoming the object of sight, touch and glorification; dura-sthaih api [janaih] samsmritah anatah adritah va — or becoming the object of remembrance, obeisance and great honour of people who are far away; premnah saram datum ekah ishah — alone is able to give the essence of *prema*; [tam] dayalum — that merciful; devam shri-caitanyam — Sri Chaitanyadeva; naumi — I praise.

**4.** I bow to the merciful Lord, Sri Chaitanya, who alone is able to give the essence of love to anyone who has seen Him, touched Him, glorified Him or remembered Him, or even from afar has bowed to Him or honoured Him.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Praise of Sri Chaitanya, who bestows the highest prema merely by being seen, touched and so on.*

>> He who, becoming the object of sight, touch or glorification, or the object of remembrance, obeisance or honour even of those who are far away, alone is able to give the essence of *prema* (the *rasa* of separation, *vipralambha*) — that merciful Lord, Sri Chaitanya, I praise.

### Verse 5

कैवल्यं नरकायते त्रिदशपूराकाशपुष्पायते
दुर्दान्तेन्द्रियकालसर्पपटली प्रोत्खातदंष्ट्रायते ।
विश्वं पूर्णसुखायते विधिमहेन्द्रादिश्च कीटायते
यत्कारुण्यकटाक्षवैभववतां तं गौरमेव स्तुमः ॥ ५ ॥

*kaivalyaṁ narakāyate tridaśapūrākāśapuṣpāyate*
*durdāntendriyakālasarpapaṭalī protkhātadaṁṣṭrāyate |*
*viśvaṁ pūrṇasukhāyate vidhimahendrādiśca kīṭāyate*
*yatkāruṇyakaṭākṣavaibhavavatāṁ taṁ gaurameva stumaḥ || 5 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* yat-karunya-kataksha-vaibhavavatam — for those whose wealth is His mercy, that is, His merciful glance; kaivalyam — liberation in the form of merging into Ishvara; narakayate — seems like hell; tridasha-puh — the city of the *tridashas*: "*tridasha*" is three times ten plus three, that is, the number thirty-three; among [the gods] the chief are the twelve Adityas, the eleven Rudras, the eight Vasus and the two Ashvini-kumaras — these thirty-three gods are the *tridashas*, and their city is heaven (*svarga*); akasha-pushpayate — seems as unreal as a flower in the sky; durdanta-indriya-kala-sarpa-patali — the host of senses, which are like serpents: "*durdanta*" — untamable, "*kala*" — extremely wrathful; protkhata-damshtrayate — behaves like snakes whose poison fangs have been pulled out; vishvam purna-sukhayate — the universe is felt as an abode of complete happiness, that is, everywhere the presence of Bhagavan is seen; vidhi-mahendra-adih ca — and Brahma, Indra and the other gods; kitayate — seem like worms, that is, the positions of the lordship of Brahma, the lordship of Indra and the rest seem as insignificant as that of a worm; tam — that; gauram eva — Gaurasundara alone; [vayam — we]; stumah — praise.

**5.** For those who possess the wealth of His merciful glance, *kaivalya* (liberation) becomes like hell, the city of the gods like a flower in the sky, the pack of untamed senses — those serpents of death — like snakes whose fangs have been pulled out; the whole world becomes full of happiness, and Brahma, Indra and the rest become like worms. Gaura alone we praise.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Praise of Gaurahari, the master of the devotees who possess incomparable wealth and scornfully reject liberation and the like.*

>> For Gaura's devotees, rich with the treasure of Gaurasundara's merciful glance, *kaivalya*, or merging into Ishvara, attainable by yogis, is like hell; Amarapuri (the city of the gods) — the desired or already obtained fruit of those who perform their *dharma* with selfish desires — is as unreal as a flower in the sky; the hosts of untamed senses, like black serpents of death, are like snakes whose poison fangs have been pulled out; the whole world appears as an abode of complete happiness, that is, […]; and the position of Brahma, the lord of the gods and others appears as the position of a worm. That Sri Gaurasundara we praise.

### Verse 6

माद्यन्तः परिपीय यस्य चरणाम्भोजस्रवत्प्रोज्ज्वल-
प्रेमानन्दमयामृताद्भुतरसान् सर्वे सुपर्वेडिताः ।
ब्रह्मादींश्च हसन्ति नातिबहु मन्यन्ते महावैष्णवान्
धिक्कुर्वन्ति च ब्रह्मयोगविदुषस्तं गौरचन्द्रं नुमः ॥ ६ ॥

*mādyantaḥ paripīya yasya caraṇāmbhojasravatprojjvala-*
*premānandamayāmṛtādbhutarasān sarve suparveḍitāḥ |*
*brahmādīṁśca hasanti nātibahu manyante mahāvaiṣṇavān*
*dhikkurvanti ca brahmayogaviduṣastaṁ gauracandraṁ numaḥ || 6 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* suparva-iditah — honoured even by the gods; sarve — all, that is, all of Gaura's devotees; yasya — whose, that is, Gaurachandra's; carana-ambhoja-sravat-projjvala-prema-ananda-maya-amrita-adbhuta-rasan — the wondrous nectar *rasa*, full of *prema* and bliss, supremely radiant, flowing from His lotus feet; paripiya — having drunk; madyantah [santah] — intoxicated by drinking; brahma-adin — at Brahma and the others; hasanti — laugh; maha-vaishnavan ca — and the great Vaishnavas, the *mahabhagavatas*, the devotees of Vishnu; atibahu na manyante — do not honour very much; brahma-yoga-vidushah — the knowers of Brahman, who worship on the principle "I am He" (*ahamgraha-upasaka*), and the yogis of the eightfold yoga; dhik kurvanti — censure; tam gaurachandram — that Gaurachandra; [vayam — we]; stumah — praise.

**6.** His devotees, worshipped even by the gods, having drunk and become intoxicated with the wondrous nectar juices of radiant love and bliss that flow from His lotus feet, laugh at Brahma and the others, do not think much even of great Vaishnavas, and censure the knowers of Brahman and of yoga. We praise that Gaurachandra.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Praise of Gaurahari, the master of the devotees who laugh at the good fortune of Brahma and the others and censure the endeavours of the knowers of undifferentiated knowledge and of the yogis.*

>> Gaura's devotees, honoured by all the gods, having drunk to the full the wondrous nectar *rasa* full of supremely radiant *prema* and bliss which flows from His lotus feet, absorbed in the madness of love, look even at Brahma and the others and lament: "Alas, alas! They are deprived of the honey of Gaurasundara's lotus feet!"; they do not greatly honour even great Vaishnavas who lack devotion to Gaura, and they censure the knowers of Brahman and the followers of the eightfold yoga for their [futile endeavours]. That Sri Gaurasundara we praise.

### Verse 7

रक्षोदैत्यकुलं हतं कियदिदं योगादिवर्त्मक्रिया-
मार्गो वा प्रकटीकृतः कियदिदं सृष्ट्यादिकं वा कियत् ।
मेदिन्युद्धरणादिकं कियदिदं प्रेमोज्ज्वलाया महा-
भक्तेर्वर्त्मकरीं परं भगवतश्चैतन्यमूर्तिं स्तुमः ॥ ७ ॥

*rakṣodaityakulaṁ hataṁ kiyadidaṁ yogādivartmakriyā-*
*mārgo vā prakaṭīkṛtaḥ kiyadidaṁ sṛṣṭyādikaṁ vā kiyat |*
*medinyuddharaṇādikaṁ kiyadidaṁ premojjvalāyā mahā-*
*bhaktervartmakarīṁ paraṁ bhagavataścaitanyamūrtiṁ stumaḥ || 7 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* [rama-nrisimha-adibhih — by the *avataras* Rama, Nrisimha and the others]; rakshah-daitya-kulam — the race of Rakshasas and Daityas; hatam — was destroyed; idam kiyat — what is so important in this deed?; [kapiladeva-adibhih — by Kapiladeva and the others]; yoga-adi-vartma-kriya-margah — the path of action on the ways of yoga and the like; va prakatikritah — was revealed; idam kiyat — what is so great in this?; [guna-avatara-brahma-adina kritam — done by the *guna-avataras*, Brahma and the others]; srishti-adikam — the deeds of creation, maintenance, destruction and so on; va kiyat — what is so excellent in this too?; [shri-varaha-adina kritam — done by Sri Varaha and other *avataras*]; medini-uddharana-adikam — the lifting of the earth and other deeds; idam kiyat — what is there in this either?; [vayam tu — we, however]; bhagavatah — to Bhagavan; prema-ujjvalayah maha-bhakteh — of the highest (*para*) *bhakti*, radiant with *prema*; vartma-karim — showing the path; caitanya-murtim — the form of Chaitanya; param stumah — praise exclusively.

**7.** What is so great in the destruction of the race of Rakshasas and Daityas? What is so great in revealing the path of yoga and other practices? What is so great in the creation of the world and the rest, in the rescue of the earth and so on? We praise only the form of Chaitanya, who has revealed the path of the highest *bhakti* to Bhagavan, radiant with *prema*.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Praise of Sri Chaitanya, who revealed the elevated, radiant bhakti never given before.*

>> Is the destruction of the race of Rakshasas and the race of Daityas in the *avataras* Rama, Nrisimha and the others such a beneficial and great deed? Is the showing of the path of action of Sankhya, yoga and so on in the *avataras* Kapila and the others so important? How great is the significance of the pastimes of creation, maintenance and destruction of the *guna-avataras*, Brahma and the others? Is the feat of rescuing the earth sunk in the waters of the deluge in the *avatara* Varaha and the others so beneficial? (We do not attach great importance to all this: beside Gaurasundara's gift of *prema* it is all insignificant.) We praise the form of Sri Chaitanya, the best of all *avataras*, who shows the path of the highest *bhakti* to the Lord, radiant with *prema*.

## Obeisance (namaskara)

### Verse 8

नमश्चैतन्यचन्द्राय कोटिचन्द्राननत्विषे ।
प्रेमानन्दाब्धिचन्द्राय चारुचन्द्रांशुहासिने ॥ ८ ॥

*namaścaitanyacandrāya koṭicandrānanatviṣe |*
*premānandābdhicandrāya cārucandrāṁśuhāsine || 8 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* koti-candra-anana-tvishe — to Him whose radiance of face is more beautiful even than millions of moons; prema-ananda-abdhi-candraya — to Him who is like the moon in the ocean of bliss born of *prema*; caru-candra-amshu-hasine — to Him whose smile is like beautiful moonbeams; caitanya-candraya — to Chaitanya-chandra; namah — obeisance.

**8.** Obeisance to Chaitanya-chandra, the Moon, whose face shines like millions of moons — the moon over the ocean of love and bliss, whose smile is like beautiful moonbeams.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Obeisance to Gaurachandra, the moon over the ocean of prema and bliss.*

>> To Him whose radiance of face is more beautiful than the splendour of millions upon millions of full moons, who is the moon over the ocean of *prema* and bliss, whose sweet smile on His lotus face is more charming than the moonbeams — to that Chaitanya-chandra we offer obeisance.

### Verse 9

यस्यैव पादाम्बुजभक्तिलभ्यः
प्रेमाभिधानः परमः पुमर्थः ।
तस्मै जगन्मङ्गलमङ्गलाय
चैतन्यचन्द्राय नमो नमस्ते ॥ ९ ॥

*yasyaiva pādāmbujabhaktilabhyaḥ*
*premābhidhānaḥ paramaḥ pumarthaḥ |*
*tasmai jaganmaṅgalamaṅgalāya*
*caitanyacandrāya namo namaste || 9 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* prema-abhidhanah paramah puma-arthah — the highest goal of man, called *prema*; yasya eva — only by whose; pada-ambuja-bhakti-labhyah — is attainable by devotion to the lotus feet; [bhavati — is]; tasmai — to Him; jagat-mangala-mangalaya — to Him who is the good of the good of the world: the good of the world is the worship (*bhajana*) of Sri Krishna, and still more auspicious is the supreme *prema* obtained from Him; that is, to Him who is the good of the good of the world; caitanya-candraya te — to You, Chaitanya-chandra; namah namah — obeisance again and again.

**9.** By devotion to His lotus feet alone is attained the highest goal of man, called love (*prema*). To Him, the good of all the good of the world, to Chaitanya-chandra — obeisance, again obeisance!

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Obeisance to Chaitanya-chandra, the good of all the good of the world.*

>> Only from exclusive devotion to Your lotus feet is the highest goal of man — *prema* — obtained. You are that Chaitanya-chandra, the good even of the good of the world; to You we bow again and again.

### Verse 10

उच्चैरास्फालयन्तं करचरणमहो हेमदण्डप्रकाण्डौ
बाहू प्रोद्धृत्य सत्ताण्डवतरलतनुं पुण्डरीकायताक्षम् ।
विश्वस्यामङ्गलघ्नं किमपि हरिहरीत्युन्मदानन्दनादै-
र्वन्दे तं देवचूडामणिमतुलरसाविष्टचैतन्यचन्द्रम् ॥ १० ॥

*uccairāsphālayantaṁ karacaraṇamaho hemadaṇḍaprakāṇḍau*
*bāhū proddhṛtya sattāṇḍavataralatanuṁ puṇḍarīkāyatākṣam |*
*viśvasyāmaṅgalaghnaṁ kimapi hariharītyunmadānandanādai-*
*rvande taṁ devacūḍāmaṇimatularasāviṣṭacaitanyacandram || 10 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* hema-danda-prakandau bahu — huge arms like golden pillars; proddhritya — raising; aho — ah!; kara-caranam — hands and feet; uccaih asphalayantam — Him who, absorbed in the dance, swings them high; sat-tandava-tarala-tanum — Him whose body has become mobile through a beautiful vigorous dance: "*sat*" — sweet, "*tandavena*" — by a vigorous dance, "*tarala*" — the body has become mobile; that is, Him whose body sways in a beautiful vigorous dance; pundarika-ayata-aksham — Him whose eyes are wide as lotuses; kimapi hari hari iti unmada-ananda-nadaih — with indescribable sounds of the bliss of *prema* arising from the words "Hari, Hari"; vishvasya — of the universe; amangala-ghnam — Him who destroys the inauspicious; deva-cudamanim — the crown-ornament of the gods; tam — that; atula-rasa-avishta-caitanya-candram — Chaitanya-chandra, absorbed in incomparable *rasa*; [aham — I]; vande — worship.

**10.** He vigorously swings His arms and legs — oh wonder! — raising His arms, which are like pillars of gold; His body sways in a beautiful dance, His eyes are long like lotuses; with indescribable cries of "Hari! Hari!", full of ecstatic bliss, He destroys all that is inauspicious in the universe. I bow to that Chaitanya-chandra, the crest-jewel of the gods, absorbed in incomparable *rasa*.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Glorification of Gaurahari, who dances wildly to the loud nama-kirtana.*

>> Oh! He who in the mood of Radha is immersed in the most exalted secret *rasa* directed towards Krishna; who, absorbed in the dance, throws up His huge arms, like golden pillars, and swings His arms and legs this way and that; whose beautiful body sways in the wondrous *tandava* dance; who with the cries "Hari! Hari!" — indescribable sounds of loving bliss, full of feelings such as joy and pride — destroys everything inauspicious in the whole world — that Sri Chaitanya-chandra with eyes as wide as lotus petals, the crown of all *avataras*, I glorify.

### Verse 11

आनन्दलीलामयविग्रहाय
हेमाभदिव्यच्छविसुन्दराय ।
तस्मै महाप्रेमरसप्रदाय
चैतन्यचन्द्राय नमो नमस्ते ॥ ११ ॥

*ānandalīlāmayavigrahāya*
*hemābhadivyacchavisundarāya |*
*tasmai mahāpremarasapradāya*
*caitanyacandrāya namo namaste || 11 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* ananda-lila-maya-vigrahaya — to Him whose form is made of pastimes of bliss; hema-abha-divya-cchavi-sundaraya — beautiful with a divine radiance like gold; maha-prema-rasa-pradaya — the giver of the *rasa* of the most exalted radiant *prema*, never given before; tasmai caitanya-candraya te — to Him, to You, Chaitanya-chandra; namo namah — obeisance again and again.

**11.** To Him whose body is made of pastimes of bliss, who is beautiful with a golden divine lustre, who bestows the *rasa* of great love — to Chaitanya-chandra — obeisance, again obeisance!

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *The name, form, qualities and pastimes of Gauranga; obeisance to Gauranga, who shines like molten gold, the embodiment of separation (vipralambha), the giver of the secret prema.*

>> To that Sri Chaitanya-chandra — the form made of the *rasa* of pastimes of bliss, beautiful with a radiance like gold, the giver of the *rasa* of the elevated, radiant *prema* never given before — I bow again and again.

### Verse 12

प्रवाहैरश्रूणां नवजलदकोटी इव दृशौ
दधानं प्रेमर्द्ध्या परमपदकोटीप्रहसनम् ।
वमन्तं माधुर्यैरमृतनिधिकोटीरिव तनु-
च्छटाभिस्तं वन्दे हरिमहह सन्न्यासकपटम् ॥ १२ ॥

*pravāhairaśrūṇāṁ navajaladakoṭī iva dṛśau*
*dadhānaṁ premarddhyā paramapadakoṭīprahasanam |*
*vamantaṁ mādhuryairamṛtanidhikoṭīriva tanu-*
*cchaṭābhistaṁ vande harimahaha sannyāsakapaṭam || 12 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* ahaha — oh!; ashrunam pravahaih — with streams of tears; nava-jalada-koti iva drishau dadhanam — Him whose eyes are like millions of new clouds; prema-ardhya — by the wealth of *prema*; parama-pada-koti-prahasanam — Him who laughs at millions of supreme abodes, that is, at millions of Vaikunthas; tanu-cchatabhih madhuryaih va — by the radiance of His body, or by the sweetness of the beauty and charm of His form; amrita-nidhi-kotih iva vamantam — Him who seems to pour forth millions of oceans of nectar; tam sannyasa-kapatam — Him who under a pretext accepted the dress of a sannyasi, that is, enacts the role of a sannyasi in order to spread the example of an acharya; harim — Gaurahari; vande — I worship.

**12.** With streams of tears His eyes are like millions of new rain-clouds; by the wealth of His love He laughs at millions of supreme abodes; by the lustre of His body He seems to pour forth the sweetness of millions of oceans of nectar. I bow to that Hari, who has assumed the guise of a *sannyasi*.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Glorification of Gaurahari, who [laughs at] Vaikuntha and enacts the pastime of sannyasa.*

>> Oh! He whose eyes, in streams of tears, are like millions of new rain clouds; whose wealth of *prema* makes even millions of supreme abodes, that is, Vaikunthas, appear insignificant, like objects of ridicule; whose bodily beauty and sweetness seem to pour forth millions upon millions of oceans of nectar; who (so that people, seeing Him in the dress of a sannyasi and bowing to Him, might obtain *bhakti* to Krishna by the fruit of unknown piety, *ajnata-sukriti*) under a pretext accepted the dress of a sannyasi — that Sri Gaurahari I glorify.

## Benediction (ashir-vada)

### Verse 13

सिंहस्कन्धं मधुरमधुरस्मेरगण्डस्थलान्तं
दुर्विज्ञेयोज्ज्वलरसमयाश्चर्यनानाविकारम् ।
बिभ्रत्कान्तिं विकचकनकाम्भोजगर्भाभिरामा-
मेकीभूतं वपुरवतु वो राधया माधवस्य ॥ १३ ॥

*siṁhaskandhaṁ madhuramadhurasmeragaṇḍasthalāntaṁ*
*durvijñeyojjvalarasamayāścaryanānāvikāram |*
*bibhratkāntiṁ vikacakanakāmbhojagarbhābhirāmā-*
*mekībhūtaṁ vapuravatu vo rādhayā mādhavasya || 13 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* simha-skandham — Him whose shoulders are raised like a lion's; madhura-madhura-smera-ganda-sthala-antam — Him at the edges of whose cheeks a sweet, sweet smile blossoms; durvijneya-ujjvala-rasa-maya-ashcarya-nana-vikaram — "*durvijneya*" — hard to comprehend even for the worshippers of Bhagavan, "*ujjvala-rasamaya*" — full of *shringara-rasa*, "*ashcarya-nana-vikaram*" — possessing wondrous transformations of feeling, such as the form of a tortoise, caused by tasting *rasa*; that is, possessing most wondrous transformations, such as the form of a tortoise, caused by tasting the most exalted *ujjvala-rasa*, hard to comprehend even for the worshippers of Bhagavan; vikaca-kanaka-ambhoja-garbha-abhiramam kantim — a lustre as charming as the filaments of a full-blown golden lotus; [yah — He who]; bibhrat — bears; radhayah madhavasya ca — of Radha and Madhava; ekibhutam vapuh — the united body; vah — you; avatu — may protect.

**13.** With the shoulders of a lion, with cheeks smiling ever so sweetly, with many wondrous transformations born of the inconceivable radiant *rasa*, with a beauty as charming as the heart of a blossoming golden lotus — may the body of Madhava, become one with Radha, protect you.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Gaurasundara — the body in which Radha and Krishna are united.*

>> He whose neck is raised like the neck of a lion; whose cheeks are lit at their edges by the sweetest gentle smile; who (incomprehensibly even to the *bhagavatas* who worship Sri Krishna, the embodiment of the *rasa* of union, *sambhoga*) from the excess of the *rasa* of separation (*vipralambha*) displays wondrous transformations of feeling, such as the form of a tortoise; whose bodily lustre is more charming than the filaments of a full-blown golden lotus — may that Sri Gaurasundara, the body in which Radha and Madhava are united, protect you.

### Verse 13a

वन्दे श्रीकृष्णचैतन्यं गौरं कृष्णमपि स्वयम् ।
यो राधाभावसंलुब्धः स्वभावं नितरां जहौ ॥

*vande śrīkṛṣṇacaitanyaṁ gauraṁ kṛṣṇamapi svayam |*
*yo rādhābhāvasaṁlubdhaḥ svabhāvaṁ nitarāṁ jahau ||*

*Word for word:* vande — I bow to; shri-krishna-caitanyam — Sri Krishna Chaitanya; gauram — Gaura, the golden; krishnam — Krishna; api — though; svayam — Himself; yah — who; radha-bhava-samlubdhah — longing for the moods of Radha; svabhavam — His own nature; nitaram — entirely; jahau — gave up.

(I bow to Sri Krishna Chaitanya, Gaura, who is Krishna Himself, but who, longing for the moods of Radha, entirely gave up His own nature.)[^01-1]

### Verse 14

दृष्ट्वा माद्यति नूतनाम्बुदचयं संवीक्ष्य बर्हं भवे-
दत्यन्तं विकलो विलोक्य वलितां गुञ्जावलीं वेपते ।
दृष्टे श्यामकिशोरकेऽपि चकितं धत्ते चमत्कारिता-
मित्थं गौरतनुः प्रचारितनिजप्रेमा हरिः पातु वः ॥ १४ ॥

*dṛṣṭvā mādyati nūtanāmbudacayaṁ saṁvīkṣya barhaṁ bhave-*
*datyantaṁ vikalo vilokya valitāṁ guñjāvalīṁ vepate |*
*dṛṣṭe śyāmakiśorake'pi cakitaṁ dhatte camatkāritā-*
*mitthaṁ gauratanuḥ pracāritanijapremā hariḥ pātu vaḥ || 14 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* [yah — He who]; nutana-ambuda-cayam — a mass of new clouds; drishtva — seeing; madyati — becomes intoxicated; [yah ca — and who]; barham — a peacock feather; samvikshya — seeing; atyantam vikalah bhavet — becomes extremely agitated; valitam — coiled in a ring like a bracelet; gunja-avalim — a string of *gunja* berries; vilokya — seeing; vepate — trembles; shyama-kishorake drishte api — even on seeing a dark youth; cakitam — starting; camatkaritam — wondrousness; dhatte — assumes; ittham — thus; pracarita-nija-prema — He who has spread His own *prema*; gaura-tanuh harih — Gauranga Hari; vah — you; patu — may protect.

**14.** Seeing a mass of new clouds, He becomes intoxicated; seeing a peacock feather, He becomes utterly overwhelmed; at the sight of a string of *gunja* berries, He trembles; and when He sees a dark youth, He is filled with wonder and awe. Thus Hari in the form of Gaura spreads His own love. May He protect you!

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Krishna, imbued with the mood of Radha, who preaches the longing to seek Krishna.*

>> He who, at the sight of a garland of new clouds that awaken the remembrance of Krishna, becomes frenzied; who at the sight of a peacock feather becomes extremely agitated; whose body trembles at the sight of a string of *gunja* berries coiled like a bracelet; who at the sight of a dark youth starts and assumes a wondrously beautiful appearance, and who has thus spread His own *prema* everywhere — may that Gauranga-Hari protect you.

### Verse 15

कृपासिन्धुः सन्ध्यारुणरुचिविचित्राम्बरधरो-
ज्ज्वलः पूर्णः प्रेमामृतमयमहाज्योतिरमलः ।
शचीगर्भक्षीराम्बुधिभव उदाराद्भुतकलः
कलानाथः श्रीमानुदयतु तव स्वान्तनभसि ॥ १५ ॥

*kṛpāsindhuḥ sandhyāruṇarucivicitrāmbaradharo-*
*jjvalaḥ pūrṇaḥ premāmṛtamayamahājyotiramalaḥ |*
*śacīgarbhakṣīrāmbudhibhava udārādbhutakalaḥ*
*kalānāthaḥ śrīmānudayatu tava svāntanabhasi || 15 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* kripa-sindhuh — the ocean of mercy; sandhya-aruna-ruci-vicitra-ambara-dharah — wearing wondrous garments of a red colour, like the sun at evening twilight; ujjvalah purnah prema-amrita-maya-maha-jyotih — radiant, undivided, full of the nectar of *prema*, possessing the lustre of the *sattvika* and other *bhavas*; amalah — pure; shaci-garbha-kshira-ambudhi-bhavah — born from the milk ocean, the womb of Shachi; udara-adbhuta-kalah — possessing the beautiful and wondrous sixty-four arts of *rasa*, beginning with refined skill (*vaidagdhya*); shriman — the body united with Srimati Radhika, who is full of supreme beauty, or: full of supreme beauty; kalanathah — the moon, or in another sense: Svayam Bhagavan, the source even of the partial *avataras* such as Matsya; tava — in your (here: in your, plural); svanta-nabhasi — in the sky of the heart; udayatu — may He rise.

**15.** The ocean of mercy, in a wondrous garment the colour of the red dawn, radiant and full — a pure great light made of the nectar of love — born in the milk ocean of Shachi's womb — the beautiful lord of the digits of the moon, with generous and wondrous rays — may He rise in the sky of your heart!

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Gaurachandra, who appeared from the ocean of Shachi's womb.*

>> He who is the ocean of mercy, who wears a lovely garment of a red colour like [the sun] at evening twilight — that radiant, complete one, made of the nectar of *prema*, illumined by the lustre of the *sattvika-bhavas* and the rest, spotless, charming and endowed with the wondrous sixty-four arts of *rasa*, beginning with *vaidagdhya* (refinement), born from the milk ocean of Shachi's womb, the most beautiful Sri Chaitanya-chandra — may He rise in the sky of your hearts.

### Verse 16

बध्नन् प्रेमभरप्रकम्पितकरो ग्रन्थीन् कटीडोरकैः
सङ्ख्यातुं निजलोकमङ्गलहरेकृष्णेति नाम्नां जपन् ।
अश्रुस्नातमुखः स्वमेव हि जगन्नाथं दिदृक्षुर्गता-
यातैर्गौरतनुर्विलोचनमुदं तन्वन् हरिः पातु वः ॥ १६ ॥

*badhnan premabharaprakampitakaro granthīn kaṭīḍorakaiḥ*
*saṅkhyātuṁ nijalokamaṅgalaharekṛṣṇeti nāmnāṁ japan |*
*aśrusnātamukhaḥ svameva hi jagannāthaṁ didṛkṣurgatā-*
*yātairgauratanurvilocanamudaṁ tanvan hariḥ pātu vaḥ || 16 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* nija-loka-mangala-hare-krishna-iti namnam — of His own names "Hare Krishna," which are the good of the world; sankhyatum — to keep count of the repetitions; kati-dorakaih — with the waist-cord; granthin — knots; badhnan — tying; japan [ca] — and chanting; prema-bhara-prakampita-karah — He whose hands tremble from the excess of *prema*; ashru-snata-mukhah — His face bathed in tears of *prema*; svam eva hi jagannatham — Jagannatha, non-different from Himself; didrikshuh — wishing to see; gata-ayataih — by going to and fro; vilocana-mudam — the joy of the eyes; tanvan — spreading; gaura-tanuh harih — Gauranga Sri Hari; vah — you; patu — may protect.

**16.** To count His own names, "Hare Krishna," the blessing of the whole world, He chants them and ties knots in the cord at His waist with a hand that trembles from the excess of love; His face is bathed in tears; He walks to and fro, longing to see Himself — Jagannatha.[^01-2] May Hari in the form of Gaura, the delight of the eyes, protect you!

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Gaurahari, who loudly chants the maha-mantra.*

>> He who, chanting the *maha-mantra* Hare Krishna, which brings blessing to the whole world, and tying knots on His waist-cord to keep count of the names — while His hands tremble [from the excess of *prema*] — who, longing to see Sri Jagannathadeva, non-different from Himself, His face bathed in tears, walks to and fro and thus increases the joy of people's eyes — may that Gauranga, Sri Hari, protect you.

### Verse 17

अन्तर्ध्वान्तचयं समस्तजगतामुन्मूलयन्ती हठात्
प्रेमानन्दरसाम्बुधिं निरवधि प्रोद्वेलयन्ती बलात् ।
विश्वं शीतलयन्त्यतीव विकलं तापत्रयेणानिशं
युष्माकं हृदये चकास्तु सततं चैतन्यचन्द्रच्छटा ॥ १७ ॥

*antardhvāntacayaṁ samastajagatāmunmūlayantī haṭhāt*
*premānandarasāmbudhiṁ niravadhi prodvelayantī balāt |*
*viśvaṁ śītalayantyatīva vikalaṁ tāpatrayeṇāniśaṁ*
*yuṣmākaṁ hṛdaye cakāstu satataṁ caitanyacandracchaṭā || 17 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* samasta-jagatam — of the whole world; antar-dhvanta-cayam — the mass of the darkness of ignorance abiding in the heart; hathat — suddenly; unmulayanti — uprooting; balat — by force; prema-ananda-rasa-ambudhim — the ocean of the *rasa* of the bliss of *prema*; niravadhi — incessantly; prodvelayanti — increasing exceedingly; tapa-trayena — by the three miseries; anisham — incessantly; vikalam — overwhelmed; vishvam — the universe; ativa — exceedingly; shitalayanti — refreshing by sprinkling the nectar of *prema*; caitanya-candra-cchata — the bodily radiance of Chaitanya-chandra; yushmakam — your; hridaye — in the hearts; satatam — always; cakastu — may it shine.

**17.** It suddenly uproots the darkness accumulated within all the worlds, forcefully makes the ocean of the *rasa* of love and bliss overflow without end, and cools the universe, tormented day and night by the threefold miseries — may the radiance of Chaitanya-chandra ever shine in your hearts![^01-3]

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Gaurachandra, who destroys the inner darkness, makes the ocean of His prema swell and refreshes the universe.*

>> That moonlight of the body of Sri Chaitanya-chandra which suddenly uproots the masses of the darkness of ignorance in the hearts of all living beings; which with mighty force incessantly makes the ocean of the *rasa* of *prema* and bliss swell and increase; which, sprinkling the nectar of *prema*, deeply refreshes the world of living beings, incessantly tormented by the three kinds of misery — may it ever shine in your hearts.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya (Bengali ed., No. 75 — the verse repeated).** *The bodily radiance of Gaurachandra, which makes the ocean of love swell and destroys impurity*

>> That moonlight of the Chaitanya-moon which suddenly uproots the masses of the darkness of ignorance in the hearts of living beings, incessantly makes the ocean of the *rasa* of the bliss of love swell by force, and every moment cools the world scorched by the three kinds of misery — *adhyatmika* and the rest — may that radiance of the Chaitanya-moon shine even for a moment in our hearts.

## The glory of Sri Chaitanya's devotees

### Verse 18

भ्रान्तं यत्र मुनीश्वरैरपि पुरा यस्मिन् क्षमामण्डले
कस्यापि प्रविवेश नैव धिषणा यद्वेद नो वा शुकः ।
यन्न क्वापि कृपामयेन च निजेऽप्युद्घाटितं शौरिणा
तस्मिन्नुज्ज्वलभक्तिवर्त्मनि सुखं खेलन्ति गौरप्रियाः ॥ १८ ॥

*bhrāntaṁ yatra munīśvarairapi purā yasmin kṣamāmaṇḍale*
*kasyāpi praviveśa naiva dhiṣaṇā yadveda no vā śukaḥ |*
*yanna kvāpi kṛpāmayena ca nije'pyudghāṭitaṁ śauriṇā*
*tasminnujjvalabhaktivartmani sukhaṁ khelanti gaurapriyāḥ || 18 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* pura — in ancient times; muni-ishvaraih api — even by the lords of sages; yatra [bhakti-marge] — on which [path of *bhakti*]; bhrantam — went astray; kshama-mandale — on the whole earth; kasyapi — no one's; dhishana — intelligence; yasmin — into which [path of *bhakti*]; na pravivesha eva — did not enter at all; shukah va — even Shuka; yat — which [path]; na veda — did not know; kripamayena shaurina — by the merciful Sri Krishna; kvapi — ever; nije api jane — even to His own devotees; na udghatitam — was not revealed; gaura-priyah — the devotees dear to Gaura; tasmin ujjvala-bhakti-vartmani — on that radiant path of *bhakti*; sukham khelanti — joyfully play.

**18.** On that path where once even the lords of sages went astray, into which no one's mind on the whole earth has penetrated, which even Shuka did not know, which the merciful Shauri (Krishna) never revealed anywhere even to His own intimates — on that radiant path of *bhakti* the beloved devotees of Gaura joyfully play.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Gaura's devotees, who play in the rasa of the prema never given before.*

>> On that path of the *bhakti* of sweet *rasa* on which in ancient times even the best of sages and *rishis* went astray, into which no one's intelligence in the whole circle of the earth could penetrate, of which even Shukadeva did not know — more than that, which the all-merciful Sri Krishna Himself did not reveal even to His own devotees — on that elevated, radiant path of *bhakti* the dear devotees of Sri Gaura now play in supreme bliss.

### Verse 19

तावद्ब्रह्मकथा विमुक्तिपदवी तावन्न तिक्तीभवे-
त्तावच्चापि विशृङ्खलत्वमयते नो लोकवेदस्थितिः ।
तावच्छास्त्रविदां मिथः कलकलो नानाबहिर्वर्त्मसु
श्रीचैतन्यपदाम्बुजप्रियजनो यावन्न दृग्गोचरः ॥ १९ ॥

*tāvadbrahmakathā vimuktipadavī tāvanna tiktībhave-*
*ttāvaccāpi viśṛṅkhalatvamayate no lokavedasthitiḥ |*
*tāvacchāstravidāṁ mithaḥ kalakalo nānābahirvartmasu*
*śrīcaitanyapadāmbujapriyajano yāvanna dṛggocaraḥ || 19 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* yavat — as long as; shri-caitanya-pada-ambuja-priya-janah — one absorbed in drinking the nectar of Sri Chaitanya's lotus feet, that is, an intimate of Gaura; na drik-gocarah [bhavet] — does not become visible; tavat — only so long; brahma-katha — discussion of the impersonal Brahman; tavat — so long; vimukti-padavi — the path of liberation; na tiktibhavet — does not seem bitter; tavat ca — and so long; loka-veda-sthitih api — even the order of worldly and Vedic duties; no vishrinkhalatvam ayate — does not fall into disorder, that is, the respective boundaries are not transgressed; in other words, having learned, in association with Gaura's people, of the supremely wondrous *bhakti* radiant with *prema*, a person gives up the mind fixed in the world and in the Vedas; tavat eva — only so long, that is, only until one sees the beauty of the radiance of the toenails of the lotus feet of Gaura's people; nana-bahih-vartmasu — about the many false external paths born of mental speculation; shastra-vidam — of the knowers of the *shastras*, that is, of those who consider themselves *panditas*; mithah — among themselves; kalakalah [bhavet] — there are disputes and quarrels.

**19.** Only so long does talk of Brahman, the path of liberation, not taste bitter; only so long does adherence to the order of the world and the Vedas not slacken; only so long does the noisy dispute of the learned about the many external paths go on — as long as one has not seen one who loves the lotus feet of Sri Chaitanya.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *As long as there has been no association with a devotee of Gaura, the soul retains a taste for Brahman, liberation and speculative argument.*

>> As long as an intimate associate of Sri Chaitanya — a bee drinking the nectar of His lotus feet — has not come within one's sight, only so long do discussions of the impersonal Brahman and the paths of liberation, such as merging into Ishvara, not taste bitter; only so long do worldly and Vedic norms not fall into disorder, that is, "the mind fixed in the world and in the Vedas" is not given up; only so long are disputes and quarrels about the various paths turned away from the Lord inevitable, that is, […].

### Verse 20

क्व तावद्वैराग्यं क्व च विषयवार्तासु नरके-
ष्विवोद्वेगः क्वासौ विनयभरमापूर्यलहरी ।
क्व तावत्तेजोऽलौकिकमथ महाभक्तिपदवी
क्व सा वा सम्भाव्या यदवकलितं गौरगतिषु ॥ २० ॥

*kva tāvadvairāgyaṁ kva ca viṣayavārtāsu narake-*
*ṣvivodvegaḥ kvāsau vinayabharamāpūryalaharī |*
*kva tāvattejo'laukikamatha mahābhaktipadavī*
*kva sā vā sambhāvyā yadavakalitaṁ gauragatiṣu || 20 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* gaura-gatishu — among those whose "*gati*," that is, refuge or goal, is Gaurahari; yat avakalitam — that [renunciation and the rest] which is seen, that is, felt; tavat vairagyam — such renunciation, that is, *yukta-vairagya*, or attachment to Bhagavan; kva — where?; kva ca — and where; tavan — such; vishaya-vartasu — towards talk of sense objects; narakeshu iva — as towards hell; udvegah — alarm; kva ca — and where; asau vinaya-bhara-apurya-lahari — this overflowing wave of abundant humility; tavat alaukikam tejah va — such unearthly power; kva — where?; atha sa maha-bhakti-padavi va — and this wondrous path of *bhakti*, full of *mahabhava*; kva sambhavya — where is it possible?

**20.** Where else is such renunciation, where such aversion to talk of sense objects as to hell, where this wave overflowing with humility, where that unearthly power, where that path of great *bhakti* — all that is seen in those whose refuge is Gaura?

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Only Gaura's devotees are the mine of all virtues.*

>> In those whose only refuge is Gaurahari, causeless renunciation, or attachment to Bhagavan, is seen — where else is such renunciation to be found? Where else is there such fear, as of falling into hell, at worldly talk or village gossip? Where else is there such a wave of the perfect beauty of humility and meekness? Where else is there such unearthly power? And where else is such a wondrous path of *bhakti*, full of *mahabhava*, possible?

### Verse 21

सकृन्नयनगोचरीकृततदश्रुधाराकुल-
प्रफुल्लकमलेक्षणप्रणयकातरश्रीमुखः ।
न गौरचरणं जिहासति कदापि लोकोत्तर-
स्फुरन्मधुरिमार्णवं नवनवानुरागोन्मदः ॥ २१ ॥

*sakṛnnayanagocarīkṛtatadaśrudhārākula-*
*praphullakamalekṣaṇapraṇayakātaraśrīmukhaḥ |*
*na gauracaraṇaṁ jihāsati kadāpi lokottara-*
*sphuranmadhurimārṇavaṁ navanavānurāgonmadaḥ || 21 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* [yah — he who]; sakrit-nayana-gocarikrita-tat-ashru-dhara-akula-praphulla-kamala-ikshana-pranaya-katara-shri-mukhah — "*pranaya-katara*" — overcome by love, "*shri*" — supreme beauty, the face endowed with it; what is it like? — "*ashru-dhara-akula*" — full of streams of tears, with eyes like full-blown lotuses; it has "*sakrit*" — even once — become the object of his sight; that is, he who has even once seen the beautiful face overcome by love, with lotus eyes full-blown and full of streams of tears; nava-nava-anuraga-unmadah [san] — filled with transformations of feeling, such as joy, pride and the rest, born of ever newer *anuraga*; lokottara-sphurat-madhurima-arnavam — the ocean of sweetness manifest in an unearthly way; gaura-caranam — Gaura's feet; kadapi — ever; na jihasati — does not wish to leave.

**21.** One who has even once seen Gaura's beautiful face, shy with love, with eyes like full-blown lotuses brimming with streams of tears — intoxicated with ever-new loving attachment (*anuraga*), will never wish to leave Gaura's feet, an ocean of unearthly, sparkling sweetness.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *For […] Gaura, attachment to anything other than Gaura is impossible.*

>> One who has even once seen the beautiful face of Sri Gaurasundara, full of the *rasa* of separation, shy with love, adorned with lotus eyes bathed in streams of tears — filled with ever-new transformations of feeling, such as joy and pride, born of ever newer attachment (*anuraga*), will never wish to leave the feet of that Gaurahari, the ocean of sweetness manifest in an unearthly way.

### Verse 22

आचर्य धर्मं परिचर्य विष्णुं
विचर्य तीर्थानि विचार्य वेदान् ।
विना न गौरप्रियपादसेवां
वेदादिदुष्प्राप्यपदं विदन्ति ॥ २२ ॥

*ācarya dharmaṁ paricarya viṣṇuṁ*
*vicarya tīrthāni vicārya vedān |*
*vinā na gaurapriyapādasevāṁ*
*vedādiduṣprāpyapadaṁ vidanti || 22 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* gaura-priya-pada-sevam — service to the feet of the *gaura-priyas*: those to whom Gaurasundara is dear, or those who are dear to Gaurahari; vina — without; dharmam — the *dharma* of *varnashrama* and the like; acarya [api] — even performing, properly observing; vishnum — the *vishnu-tattva*: Rama, Narayana, Nrisimha and the others; paricarya [api] — even worshipping excellently; tirthani — hundreds of holy places: Kashi, Kanchi, Gaya and the rest; vicarya [api] — even having wandered through them or made their *parikrama*; vedan — all the Vedas and *shastras*; vicarya [api] — even having studied; veda-adi-dushprapya-padam — the abode hard to reach by means of the Vedas and the rest, or: described very secretly in the Vedas and the rest and therefore incomprehensible to ordinary people — that secret place, the footstool of Sri Radha-Govinda, Sri Dhama Vrindavana; [ko'pi — no one]; na vidanti — can know.

**22.** One may practise *dharma*, serve Vishnu, travel to the holy places, study the Vedas — but without serving the feet of Gaura's beloved devotees one does not come to know the abode that is hard to reach even through the Vedas — Vrindavana, the footstool of Sri Radha-Govinda.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Even performing dharma, serving Vishnu, travelling to holy places or mastering the Vedas, no one comprehends the secret truth of the Vedas without serving the feet of Gaura's devotees.*

>> Even performing the *dharma* of *varnashrama*, worshipping Vishnu, travelling to hundreds of holy places, studying all the Vedas and *shastras* and so on — without service to the lotus feet of Gaura's dear ones no one can know the abode that is hard to reach by the Vedas and the rest (cannot find Sri Dhama Vrindavana, the field of the spiritual pastimes of Sri Radha-Govinda).

### Verse 23

अपारावारञ्चेदमृतमयपाथोधिमधिकं
विमथ्य प्राप्तं स्यात्किमपि परमं सारमतुलम् ।
तथापि श्रीगौराकृतिमदनगोपालचरण-
च्छटास्पृष्टानां तद्वहति विकटामेव कटुताम् ॥ २३ ॥

*apārāvārañcedamṛtamayapāthodhimadhikaṁ*
*vimathya prāptaṁ syātkimapi paramaṁ sāramatulam |*
*tathāpi śrīgaurākṛtimadanagopālacaraṇa-*
*cchaṭāspṛṣṭānāṁ tadvahati vikaṭāmeva kaṭutām || 23 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* [yadyapi — even if]; apara-avaram — shoreless: "*para-avara*" — both shores; that which has neither shore, that is, without shores, endless; amrita-maya-pathodhim — "*pathodhi*" — the ocean, the receptacle of waters; such an ocean whose water is nectar; adhikam — abundantly; vimathya — having thoroughly churned; idam kimapi — some indescribable; atulam — incomparable, without equal; paramam saram — supreme essence; praptam syat — should be obtained; tathapi tat vastu — still that thing; shri-gaura-akriti-madana-gopala-carana-cchata-sprishtanam — for the people touched by the radiance (beauty) of the feet of Madana-Gopala, golden [with the lustre] of Sri: "Sri" is Sri Radhika, the embodiment of all the Lakshmis; Madana-Gopala, who has become golden by Her lustre, that is, Krishna clothed in the mood and lustre of Sri Radha — Sri Gaurasundara; for those whom the radiance of His feet has touched; vikatam eva katutam — a terrible bitterness indeed; vahati — seems to have.

**23.** If, by long churning the shoreless ocean of nectar, some supreme, incomparable essence were obtained — even that would taste bitter and harsh to those touched by the radiance of the feet of Madana-Gopala, who has become golden with the lustre of Sri Radha.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *For a devotee attracted by the radiance of the toenails of Gaura's feet, everything best in the world — the best things, the best dharmas — is insignificant.*

>> Even if, by churning with the utmost effort the ocean of nectar that has no shores, some indescribable, supreme, incomparable precious essence were extracted — still, to those touched by the rays of the feet of Madana-Gopala, that is, Krishna clothed in the mood and lustre of Radha — Sri Gaurasundara — it will seem extremely bitter.

### Verse 24

तृणादपि च नीचता सहजसौम्यमुग्धाकृतिः
सुधामधुरभाषिता विषयगन्धथूथूत्कृतिः ।
हरिप्रणयविह्वला किमपि धीरनालम्बिता
भवन्ति किल सद्गुणा जगति गौरभाजाममी ॥ २४ ॥

*tṛṇādapi ca nīcatā sahajasaumyamugdhākṛtiḥ*
*sudhāmadhurabhāṣitā viṣayagandhathūthūtkṛtiḥ |*
*haripraṇayavihvalā kimapi dhīranālambitā*
*bhavanti kila sadguṇā jagati gaurabhājāmamī || 24 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* trinat api ca su-nicata — humility lower than grass, that is, absence of material pride; sahaja-saumya-mugdha-akritih — a natural, gentle, beautiful appearance; sudha-madhura-bhashita — speech sweet as nectar; vishaya-gandha-thuthut-kritih — spitting at the mere smell of sense objects unconnected with Krishna-Chaitanya; dhih hari-pranaya-vihvala [sati] kimapi analambita — the mind, overwhelmed by love for Hari, entirely devoid of external consciousness; ami sat-gunah — these good qualities; jagati — in the world; kila gaura-bhajam [eva] bhavanti — belong only to the devotees of Gaura.

**24.** Humility lower than a blade of grass, a naturally gentle and pure appearance, speech sweet as nectar, spitting at even the scent of sense objects, a mind leaning on nothing [external], indescribably overwhelmed by love for Hari — such good qualities are found in this world only in the devotees of Gaura.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *The virtues of body, speech, mind and intelligence proper to Gaura's devotee are found nowhere else.*

>> Humility lower than grass, that is, absence of worldly self-conceit; a naturally gentle, charming appearance; speech sweet as nectar; spitting at the mere smell of sense objects unconnected with Krishna Chaitanya; complete loss of external consciousness from being overwhelmed by love for Hari — all these virtues in the world belong only to Gaura's devotees.

### Verse 25

उपासतां वा गुरुवर्यकोटी-
रधीयतां वा श्रुतिशास्त्रकोटीः ।
चैतन्यकारुण्यकटाक्षभाजां
सद्यः परं स्याद्धि रहस्यलाभः ॥ २५ ॥

*upāsatāṁ vā guruvaryakoṭī-*
*radhīyatāṁ vā śrutiśāstrakoṭīḥ |*
*caitanyakāruṇyakaṭākṣabhājāṁ*
*sadyaḥ paraṁ syāddhi rahasyalābhaḥ || 25 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* guru-varya-kotih — millions of the best gurus; upasatam — let them take shelter of; va — or; shruti-shastra-kotih — millions of *shrutis* and *shastras*; adhiyatam — let them study; [parantu — but]; caitanya-karunya-kataksha-bhajam — of those who have obtained Chaitanya's merciful glance; hi — certainly; sadyah — immediately; param rahasya-labhah — the attainment of the secret *prema*; syat — takes place.

**25.** Let them serve millions of the best gurus, let them study millions of *shrutis* and *shastras* — but to those on whom Chaitanya's merciful glance has fallen, the highest secret is revealed at once.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *The secret prema which the devotees of Sri Gaura obtain at once cannot be obtained by millions of famous karmis, jnanis, yogis and servants of gurus, nor by millions of readers of shruti and smriti.*

>> Let them (those who have not taken shelter at Gaura's lotus feet) take shelter of millions of the best gurus, that is, receive from them as many […] instructions as they like, or study millions of *shrutis* and *shastras* (the *agamas*, *nigamas* and the rest) — this gives no possibility of obtaining the secret *prema*; but those whom the merciful glance of Sri Chaitanyadeva has touched certainly obtain that secret *prema* at once.

### Verse 26

आस्तां वैराग्यकोटिर्भवतु शमदमक्षान्तिमैत्रादिकोटि-
स्तत्त्वानुध्यानकोटिर्भवतु भवतु वा वैष्णवीभक्तिकोटिः ।
कोट्यंशोऽप्यस्य न स्यात्तदपि गुणगणो यः स्वतःसिद्ध आस्ते
श्रीमच्चैतन्यचन्द्रप्रियचरणनखज्योतिरामोदभाजाम् ॥ २६ ॥

*āstāṁ vairāgyakoṭirbhavatu śamadamakṣāntimaitrādikoṭi-*
*stattvānudhyānakoṭirbhavatu bhavatu vā vaiṣṇavībhaktikoṭiḥ |*
*koṭyaṁśo'pyasya na syāttadapi guṇagaṇo yaḥ svataḥsiddha āste*
*śrīmaccaitanyacandrapriyacaraṇanakhajyotirāmodabhājām || 26 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* vairagya-kotih — strong or manifold *vairagya*; *vairagya* is of two kinds, *phalgu* and *yukta*: giving up things connected with Hari as worldly is called *phalgu-vairagya*, while the proper acceptance of what is favourable and connected with Krishna is *yukta-vairagya*; astam — let there be; shama-dama-kshanti-maitri-adi-kotih va — or: *shama* — steadiness in Bhagavan, *dama* — control of the senses, *kshanti* — tolerance, the undisturbed state of the mind even when there is cause for agitation, *maitri* and the rest — purity and the rest — all these countless qualities; bhavatu — let there be; tattva-anudhyana-kotih — millions of meditations on "*tat tvam asi*," that is, on the oneness of the *jiva* and Brahman; bhavatu — let there be; vaishnavi-bhakti-kotih va — or millions of *bhaktis* connected with Vishnu; bhavatu — let there be; tat api — still; shrimat-caitanya-candra-priya-carana-nakha-jyotih-amoda-bhajam — of the people who have obtained bliss from the radiance of the toenails of the dear devotees of the all-powerful Chaitanya-chandra; yah — which; svatah-siddhah — naturally inherent; guna-ganah — host of qualities; aste — abides; asya — of it; koti-amshah api — even a millionth part; [anyatra — elsewhere]; na syat — is not.

**26.** Let there be millions of kinds of renunciation, millions of kinds of restraint, self-control, patience, friendliness and the rest, millions of meditations on the truth, and even millions of kinds of Vaishnava *bhakti* — all of these together do not equal even a millionth part of the qualities that dwell of themselves in those who delight in the fragrance of the radiance of the toenails of the dear devotees of Sri Chaitanya-chandra.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Others do not possess even a small part of a millionth part of the natural qualities of Gaura's devotee.*

>> Let there be the strongest renunciation, let there be countless qualities — *shama*, *dama*, *kshanti*, *maitri* and the rest; let millions of reflections on the oneness of […] Brahman and the *jivatma* take possession of the heart, or let there be millions of kinds of *bhakti* connected with Vishnu — still, not one part in a million of those natural qualities which always abide in the people who rejoice in the radiance of the toenails of the dear devotees of Sri Chaitanya-chandra is to be found anywhere else.

### Verse 27

केचित्सागरभूधरानपि पराक्रामन्ति नृत्यन्ति वै
केचिद्देवपुरन्दरादिषु महाक्षेपं क्षिपन्तो मुहुः ।
आनन्दोद्भटजालविह्वलतया तेऽद्वैतचन्द्रादयः
के के नोद्धतवन्त ईदृशि पुनश्चैतन्यनृत्योत्सवे ॥ २७ ॥

*kecitsāgarabhūdharānapi parākrāmanti nṛtyanti vai*
*keciddevapurandarādiṣu mahākṣepaṁ kṣipanto muhuḥ |*
*ānandodbhaṭajālavihvalatayā te'dvaitacandrādayaḥ*
*ke ke noddhatavanta īdṛśi punaścaitanyanṛtyotsave || 27 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* ananda-udbhata-jala-vihvalataya — because of being overwhelmed by the boundless bliss born of tasting the nectar of *prema*; kecit — some of the devotees headed by Murari Gupta; sagara-bhudharan api — even oceans and mountains; parakramanti — stride over; kecit — some of the devotees headed by Srivasa; deva-purandara-adishu — at Indra and the other gods; muhuh — again and again; maha-akshepam — great reproach; kshipantah — hurling; vai nrityanti — dance; idrishi — at such a; caitanya-nritya-utsave — festival of Sri Gauranga's dance; te advaitacandra-adayah — those headed by Sri Advaita Acharya; ke ke — who, who; punah na uddhatavantah — did not become wild?

**27.** Some of them stride over oceans and mountains and dance; others again and again hurl great reproaches at Indra and the other gods; and Advaita-chandra and the others, beside themselves with tumultuous bliss — who did not run wild at this festival of Chaitanya's dance?

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *The madness of love of Gaura's devotees at the festival of the dance is incomparable.*

>> Caught in the net of boundless bliss born of tasting the nectar of *prema*, devoid of external consciousness, some of the devotees headed by Murari Gupta seem to stride over mountains and oceans; others of the devotees headed by Srivasa dance, again and again heaping reproaches on Indra and the other gods; and at such a festival of Sri Chaitanya's dance which of the devotees headed by Advaita (as venerable, learned and acharyas as they were) did not become wild!

### Verse 28

भूतो वा भविताऽपि वा भवति वा कस्यापि यः कोऽपि वा
सम्बन्धो भगवत्पदाम्बुजरसे नास्मिन् जगन्मण्डले ।
तत्सर्वं निजभक्तिरूपपरमैश्वर्येण विक्रीडितो
गौरस्यास्य कृपाविजृम्भिततया जानन्ति निर्मत्सराः ॥ २८ ॥

*bhūto vā bhavitā'pi vā bhavati vā kasyāpi yaḥ ko'pi vā*
*sambandho bhagavatpadāmbujarase nāsmin jaganmaṇḍale |*
*tatsarvaṁ nijabhaktirūpaparamaiśvaryeṇa vikrīḍito*
*gaurasyāsya kṛpāvijṛmbhitatayā jānanti nirmatsarāḥ || 28 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* asmin jagat-mandale — in this earthly world; bhagavat-pada-ambuja-rase — in the *rasa* of Bhagavan's lotus feet; kasyapi — of no one; yah ko'pi sambandhah — any relationship whatever; na bhutah — has been; [na] bhavita — will be; [na] bhavati api va — nor is even now; nija-bhakti-rupa-parama-aishvaryena — together with the supreme wealth in the form of devotion to Himself; vikriditah — sporting; asya gaurasya — of this Gaurachandra; kripa-vijrimbhitataya — owing to the manifestation of mercy; [tat-upalakshitah — illumined by that mercy]; nirmatsarah — people merciful to all beings; tat sarvam — all those sweetnesses of *rasa*; jananti — come to know.

**28.** Such a relationship with the *rasa* of the Lord's lotus feet as no one in this world has had, will have or has — all this the non-envious come to know through the outpouring mercy of this Gaura, who sports with the supreme wealth: devotion to Himself.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *The secret prema, impossible for anyone in the past, future and present, is always accessible to Gaura's devotees.*

>> No relationship with the *rasa* of Bhagavan's lotus feet has anyone in this world had, will have or has; but since the mercy of Gaurasundara, who sports with His supreme wealth — devotion to Himself — has become manifest, the non-envious devotees marked by that mercy have come to know all that sweetness of *rasa*. That is, that secret relationship with the *rasa* of Krishna's lotus feet which no one has ever had, will have or has even now, the associates of Gaura constantly realize by Gaura's mercy.

[^01-1]: In the 1951 edition this verse stands in brackets without a number; it is probably a later insertion. It could not be found in the OCR copy of the Bengali edition with Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati's translation.

[^01-2]: Mahaprabhu worshipped Jagannatha in Puri as another form of His own (more precisely, as Himself). Here it is said: "longing to see Himself — Jagannatha."

[^01-3]: In the Bengali edition with Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati's translation this verse stands here too (No. 17), and is then repeated in the seventh section (No. 75) with the words "in our hearts" (*asmakam* instead of *yushmakam*).

# Part Two

*Censure of the non-devotees. Humility (verses 29–56)*

## Censure of those not devoted to Chaitanya

### Verse 29

महापुरुषमानिनां सुरमुनीश्वराणां निजं
पदाम्बुजमजानतां किमपि गर्वनिर्वासनम् ।
अहो नयनगोचरं निगमचक्रचूडाचरं
शचीसुतमचीकरत् क इह भूरिभाग्योदयः ॥

*mahāpuruṣamānināṁ suramunīśvarāṇāṁ nijaṁ*
*padāmbujamajānatāṁ kimapi garvanirvāsanam |*
*aho nayanagocaraṁ nigamacakracūḍācaraṁ*
