*śacīsutamacīkarat ka iha bhūribhāgyodayaḥ ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* nigama-cakra-cuda-cayam — Him whom the Upanishads seek ("*nigama-cakra*" — the collection of the *shrutis*, their "*cuda*" — crowns, and "*caya*" — that which is sought by them; that is, Him whom the Upanishads, the head-ornament of the Vedas, seek — Him); nijam pada-ambujam ajanatam — who do not know His own — Gaurahari's — lotus feet; maha-purusha-maninam — who think themselves great personalities; sura-muni-ishvaranam — of the best of the gods and the best of the sages; kimapi garva-nirvasanam — a certain destroyer of pride; shaci-sutam — the son of Shachi; [mat-vidhe jane api — even for a person such as me]; nayana-gocaram — visible to the eye; acikarat — has made; aho iha — ah, in this world; [idrik — such]; bhuri-bhagya-udayah — greatly fortunate; kah — who?

**29.** Oh, who in this world is that great fortunate one who has made visible even to one such as me the son of Shachi — He whom the crowns of the whole host of the Vedas seek! He drives out, in an indescribable way, the pride of the lords of the gods and of the sages, who think themselves great and do not know His lotus feet.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *The most fortunate of all is he who gives the opportunity to seek the lotus feet of Gaurahari, whom the Upanishads seek.*

>> Shachinandana — He whom the garland of the Upanishads, the ornament of all the *shrutis*, seeks; the destroyer of the pride of the best of sages and gods who think themselves great personalities and do not know His own lotus feet — who has made Him visible even to one such as me? Oh! Who in this world is such a greatly fortunate one?

### Verse 30

सर्वसाधनहीनोऽपि परमाश्चर्यवैभवे ।
गौराङ्गे न्यस्तभावो यः सर्वार्थपूर्ण एव सः ॥

*sarvasādhanahīno'pi paramāścaryavaibhave |*
*gaurāṅge nyastabhāvo yaḥ sarvārthapūrṇa eva saḥ ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* sarva-sadhana-hinah api — even devoid of all kinds of *sadhana*; yah — he who; parama-ashcarya-vaibhave gaurange — in Gaurasundara, who possesses supremely wonderful glory; nyasta-bhavah — has placed his *citta-vritti* (the movements of the mind); sah — he; sarva-artha-purnah — (*sarva* — […]; *artha* — *prema*, that is, the highest goal of man, *prema* for Krishna; having obtained it, *purna* — full, that is, one whose desires are all fulfilled); eva [bhavati] — certainly becomes.

**30.** Even if a man is devoid of every means of spiritual practice, if his heart is given to Gauranga, whose glory is wonderful beyond all, he has attained every goal.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Even one devoid of all sadhana but surrendered to Gaura alone obtains the crown of the goals of man.*

>> Even devoid of all *sadhana*, one who has given his mind to Sri Gaurasundara, who possesses supremely wonderful glory, certainly obtains the highest goal of man — *prema* for Krishna — and becomes fully satisfied; of this [there is no doubt].

### Verse 31

अप्यगण्यमहापुण्यमनन्यशरणं हरेः ।
अनुपासितचैतन्यमधन्यं मन्यते मतिः ॥

*apyagaṇyamahāpuṇyamananyaśaraṇaṁ hareḥ |*
*anupāsitacaitanyamadhanyaṁ manyate matiḥ ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* aganya-maha-punyam — one who has countless great merits — such a person; hareh ananya-sharanam api — even one exclusively surrendered to Hari; anupasita-caitanyam [janam] — the person who has not worshipped Krishna-Chaitanya-chandra; matih — my mind; adhanyam — (*dhana* is the fifth goal of man, *prema*; one worthy of that wealth is "*dhanya*," one who is not "*dhanya*" is "*adhanya*"; that is, no one except the worshippers of Sri Chaitanya-chandra is worthy of obtaining the wealth of *prema*); manyate — considers.

**31.** Even if someone possesses countless great merits and seeks no refuge other than Hari, my mind still considers him unfortunate if he has not worshipped Chaitanya.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Even an exclusive devotee of Hari, if he has no bhakti for Sri Chaitanya, is unfortunate (a-dhanya)*

>> Let someone have countless great merits, let him be exclusively surrendered to Hari — but if he has not worshipped Sri Chaitanya-chandra, my mind considers him "*a-dhanya*," that is, unworthy of obtaining the highest goal of man.

### Verse 32

क्रियासक्तान् धिग् धिग् विकटतपसो धिक् च यमिनः
धिगस्तु ब्रह्माहं वदनपरिफुल्लान् जडमतीन् ।
किमेतान् शोचामो विषयरसमत्तान्नरपशून्
न केषाञ्चिल्लेशोऽप्यहह मिलितो गौरमधुनः ॥

*kriyāsaktān dhig dhig vikaṭatapaso dhik ca yaminaḥ*
*dhigastu brahmāhaṁ vadanapariphullān jaḍamatīn |*
*kimetān śocāmo viṣayarasamattānnarapaśūn*
*na keṣāñcilleśo'pyahaha milito gauramadhunaḥ ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* kriya-asaktan — (*kriya* — the regular and occasional rites, *sandhya-vandana* and so on) those attached to them, that is, always eager for them, and therefore; jada-matin — dull-witted (*jada* — indifferent to the true search for the highest goal but absorbed in seeking a particle of transient, worldly, illusory happiness — such people); dhik — shame (an indeclinable word expressing contempt); vikata-tapasah — the harsh ascetics (those who in summer endure the heat of the sun, in the rainy season the incessant downpours, in the cold seasons stand in water, grow their nails, beards and hair or hang head downwards, go without food and sleep, performing very laborious austerities, *japa*, meditation and so on — them); dhik — shame; yaminah ca — and the self-restrained (those who try to subdue the senses or the mind by *yama*, *niyama* and the other steps of the eightfold yoga — them too); dhik — shame; brahma aham [iti shabda-uccarana-matrena eva — merely by uttering the words "I am Brahman"]; vadana-pariphullan — those whose faces blossom (who, merely uttering the words "I am Brahman," pride themselves on being "liberated," and whose faces are therefore very joyful — them); dhik astu — shame; vishaya-rasa-mattan — intoxicated with the *rasa* of sense objects (intoxicated with the pride of enjoying sense objects unconnected with Bhagavan: the false renunciants (*phalgu-tyagis*) seeking the impersonal Brahman are intoxicated with the evil *rasa* of aversion to Bhagavan — the thought "I am liberated," while the pleasure-seekers attached to rites are busy enjoying the fruits of this world and the next; so, these); nara-pashun — over these human animals (animals in human form: as village animals eat and amuse themselves, so these, having no connection with Gaura, eat and amuse themselves in every way like them — over them); kim shocamah — why should we grieve?; ahaha — alas!; [etesham — of them]; keshancit — no one; gaura-madhunah — of the honey of Gaura's lotus feet; leshah api — even a drop; na militah — has received.

**32.** Shame, shame on those attached to rituals and therefore dull-witted; shame on harsh ascetics and self-restraining yogis! Shame on those whose faces blossom at the mere words "I am Brahman"! And why should we grieve over these two-legged beasts intoxicated with the taste of sense pleasure? Alas, not one of them has received even a drop of the honey of Gaura.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Censure of the futile endeavours of the knowers (jnanis), the followers of rituals (karmis), the ascetics and the yogis*

>> Shame on the *smartas* attached to the regular and occasional rites; shame on the harsh ascetics, shame on the yogis of the eightfold yoga; shame also on the *ahamgraha-upasakas* (who worship themselves as God), who, as soon as they utter the words "*aham brahmasmi*" ("I am Brahman"), blossom with the conceit of the liberated. All of them are intoxicated with the enjoyment of sense objects unconnected with the Lord. Why should one grieve over these two-legged animals? Alas, alas! Not one of them has received even a drop of the nectar of Gaura's lotus feet.

### Verse 33

पाषाणः परिषेचितोऽमृतरसैर्नैवाङ्कुरः सम्भवेत्
लाङ्गूलं सरमापतेर्विवृणतः स्यादस्य नैवार्जवम् ।
हस्तावुन्नयता बुधाः कथमहो धार्यं विधोर्मण्डलं
सर्वं साधनमस्तु गौरकरुणाभावे न भावोत्सवः ॥

*pāṣāṇaḥ pariṣecito'mṛtarasairnaivāṅkuraḥ sambhavet*
*lāṅgūlaṁ saramāpatervivṛṇataḥ syādasya naivārjavam |*
*hastāvunnayatā budhāḥ kathamaho dhāryaṁ vidhormaṇḍalaṁ*
*sarvaṁ sādhanamastu gaurakaruṇābhāve na bhāvotsavaḥ ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* [he] budhah — O learned ones; pashanah — a stone; amrita-rasaih — with streams of nectar; parishecitah [api] — (*pari* — on all sides, abundantly; *secita* — watered) even if watered; [tasya — of it]; ankurah na eva sambhavet — the appearance of a sprout is never possible; langulam vivrinatah — of one stretching out its tail; sarama-pateh — of a dog; asya [langulasya] — of that stretched-out tail; arjavam — straightness; na eva [bhavati] — never comes about; hastau unnayata [janena] — by a person raising his hands; vidhoh mandalam — the disc of the moon; katham dharyam — how can it be held? (that is, merely by raising his hands a person cannot touch the disc of the moon); sarvam sadhanam astu — let there be all the means of practice, but even then; gaura-karuna-abhave — in the absence of Gaurasundara's mercy, that is, without mercy; na bhava-utsavah — the festival [of *prema*] (the OCR is unclear) is never possible.

**33.** However much a stone is watered with nectar, no sprout will appear; however much one straightens a dog's tail, it will never become straight; O wise ones, however high one raises one's hands, how can one hold the disc of the moon? Let there be every means of practice — but without Gaura's mercy there is no festival of *bhava* (love).

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Without Gaura's mercy, love (prema) is unattainable even for one who possesses all the means of practice*

>> O wise ones! As on a stone, however much one waters it with nectar, a sprout will never appear; as a dog's tail, however much one straightens it, will never become straight; as, raising both hands, no one can touch the disc of the moon — so, alas, even one who possesses all the means of practice will not become worthy of obtaining the bliss of *prema* without the mercy of Sri Gaurasundara.

### Verse 34

अवतीर्णे गौरचन्द्रे विस्तीर्णे प्रेमसागरे ।
सुप्रकाशितरत्नौघे यो दीनो दीन एव सः ॥

*avatīrṇe gauracandre vistīrṇe premasāgare |*
*suprakāśitaratnaughe yo dīno dīna eva saḥ ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* vistirne prema-sagare — in the wide ocean of *prema*; gaura-candre avatirne [sati] — when Gaurachandra has risen; [tena — by Him]; su-prakashita-ratna-oghe — when the multitude of jewels is beautifully illumined (*su* — beautifully, *prakashita* — illumined, *ratna* — jewels, *ogha* — multitude; that is, when the bright radiance of Sri Gaurachandra has beautifully illumined the jewels of the treasury of *prema* — the nine kinds of *bhakti*, or the *bhavas* nourished by the *vibhavas*, *anubhavas*, *sattvika-* and *vyabhichari-bhavas* and the other ingredients, or the necklace of the jewel-names "Hare Krishna" and the rest); yah dinah — whoever remains poor; sah dinah eva — he is truly poor (that is, whoever, leaving the jewels of the ocean of *prema* illumined by the rays of Gaurachandra, wishes to seek rust and slag elsewhere, is never rid of poverty: not having taken shelter of the lotus feet of the Gaura-moon, he will not be freed from poverty, forever labouring for what is not wealth and remaining deprived of the wealth of *prema*).

**34.** When Gaurachandra has descended and the ocean of love has spread wide, its multitude of jewels (the nine kinds of *bhakti* and the rest) shining brightly — whoever has remained poor is poor indeed.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Truly poor is only he who has not taken shelter of Gaura's lotus feet*

>> Over the wide ocean, the treasury of *prema*, the Gaura-moon has risen and revealed heaps of the jewels of the nine kinds of *bhakti* — hearing, chanting and the rest. Whoever has remained poor even with such an opportunity is, without doubt, extremely unfortunate.

### Verse 35

अवतीर्णे गौरचन्द्रे विस्तीर्णे प्रेमसागरे ।
ये न मज्जन्ति मज्जन्ति ते महानर्थसागरे ॥

*avatīrṇe gauracandre vistīrṇe premasāgare |*
*ye na majjanti majjanti te mahānarthasāgare ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* vistirne prema-sagare — in the wide ocean-treasury of *prema*, or in the ocean of the *rasa* of *prema*; gaura-candre avatirne [sati] — when the Gaura-moon has risen; ye [janah tatra] — those people who into that ocean of *rasa* or that treasury; na majjanti — do not plunge; te — they; maha-anartha-sagare — in the ocean of great *anarthas* (when the ocean is explained as a treasury of jewels: *artha* is Krishna, or the highest goal, *prema*, and *anartha* is its opposite: everything that is not Krishna, or the four goals of man; when it is explained as an ocean of *rasa* — in an ocean of donkey's urine, that is, of the *anarthas*: the desire for prestige (*pratishtha-asha*), crookedness (*kutinati*) and the like); majjanti — drown.

**35.** When Gaurachandra has descended and the ocean of love has spread wide, those who do not plunge into it drown in the ocean of great misfortunes.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Whoever has not plunged into the ocean of prema revealed by Gaurachandra drowns in the ocean of great evils (anarthas)*

>> Though the Gaura-moon has risen over the wide ocean, the treasury of *prema*, those who have not dived into that ocean of *prema* have remained drowning in the ocean of great evils (*anarthas*)!

### Verse 36

प्रसारितमहाप्रेमपीयूषरससागरे ।
चैतन्यचन्द्रे प्रकटे यो दीनो दीन एव सः ॥

*prasāritamahāpremapīyūṣarasasāgare |*
*caitanyacandre prakaṭe yo dīno dīna eva saḥ ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* prasarita-maha-prema-piyusha-rasa-sagare — in the ocean in which (*prasarita* — very wide, *mahan* — excellent) *prema* is *piyusha*, that is, the nectar *rasa* — in it; caitanya-candre prakate [sati] — when the Chaitanya-moon has risen; yah dinah sah dinah eva — whoever is poor is truly poor (whoever has not taken shelter of Gaura's feet is more foolish than the foolish).

**36.** When Chaitanya-chandra has appeared and the ocean of the nectar *rasa* of great love has spread forth — whoever has remained poor is poor indeed.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Whoever has turned away from the honey of Gaura's lotus feet is extremely foolish*

>> When the Chaitanya-moon has risen and poured forth an ocean of the nectar *rasa* of great love, one who has remained "poor," that is, deprived of the chance to drink that nectar of *prema*, is truly "poor," that is, extremely unfortunate.

### Verse 37

अचैतन्यमिदं विश्वं यदि चैतन्यमीश्वरम् ।
न विदुः सर्वशास्त्रज्ञा ह्यपि भ्राम्यन्ति ते जनाः ॥

*acaitanyamidaṁ viśvaṁ yadi caitanyamīśvaram |*
*na viduḥ sarvaśāstrajñā hyapi bhrāmyanti te janāḥ ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* sarva-shastra-jnah [janah] api — even people learned in all the *shastras*; yadi caitanyam — if Sri Chaitanyadeva; ishvaram na viduh — they do not know as Bhagavan Himself; te — they; acaitanyam idam vishvam — in this unconscious material world; bhramyanti — wander (that is, subject to the fruits of *karma*, they merely travel through heaven, hell and the other worlds, and their comings and goings never cease).

**37.** If people, even those who know all the shastras, do not know that Chaitanya is God, they merely wander about in this world devoid of consciousness (*a-chaitanya*).

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Whoever doubts that Gaurasundara is Bhagavan Himself, even if he knows all the shastras, is only a conditioned soul wandering in the material world*

>> If even the knowers of all the *shastras* do not know that Sri Chaitanya-chandra is "Bhagavan Himself," they merely come and go in this unconscious (*a-cit*) world.

### Verse 38

स्वादं स्वादं मधुरिमभरं स्वीयनामावलीनां
मादं मादं किमपि विवशीभूतविस्रस्तगात्रः ।
वारं वारं व्रजपतिगुणान् गाय गायेति जल्पन्
गौरो दृष्टः सकृदपि न यैर्दुर्घटा तेषु भक्तिः ॥

*svādaṁ svādaṁ madhurimabharaṁ svīyanāmāvalīnāṁ*
*mādaṁ mādaṁ kimapi vivaśībhūtavisrastagātraḥ |*
*vāraṁ vāraṁ vrajapatiguṇān gāya gāyeti jalpan*
*gauro dṛṣṭaḥ sakṛdapi na yairdurghaṭā teṣu bhaktiḥ ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* sviya-nama-avalinam — of His own names, "Hare Krishna" and the rest; madhurima-bharam — (*madhurima* — sweetness, *bhara* — fullness or excess; that is) the utmost sweetness; svadam svadam — tasting again and again; [tatah — from that]; madam madam — becoming intoxicated again and again; kimapi — in an indescribable way; vivashibhuta-visrasta-gatrah [san] — losing control, and then with His body grown limp; vrajapati-gunan — the qualities of the lord of Vrindavana; varam varam — again and again; gaya gaya — "sing," "sing"; iti — thus; jalpan — saying (that is, He says); gaurah — Gaurasundara; yaih — by those people; sakrit api na drishtah — has not been seen even once, that is, not realized in experience; teshu — in those people; bhaktih kutah [bhavati] — whence *bhakti*? (the possibility of *bhakti* is negligible).

**38.** Again and again tasting the fullness of the sweetness of His own names, again and again becoming intoxicated, indescribably losing control of Himself so that His body goes limp, again and again murmuring, "Sing, sing of the qualities of the lord of Vraja!" — such is Gaura. Those who have never once seen Him — how can they have *bhakti*?

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *One who does not know the glory of Gaura's lotus feet cannot obtain the secret of rasa*

>> He, again and again tasting the sweetness of His own names — "Hare Krishna" and the rest — becomes more and more intoxicated by them, indescribably loses control of Himself, and His body grows limp; He calls out to everyone and says again and again: "Chant, chant the qualities of the lord of Vrindavana!" For those who have never once seen such a Sri Gaurasundara, the possibility of obtaining *bhakti* is infinitely remote.

### Verse 39

विना बीजं किं नाङ्कुरजननमन्धोऽपि न कथं
प्रपश्येन्नो पङ्गुर्गिरिशिखरमारोहति कथम् ।
यदि श्रीचैतन्ये हरिरसमयाश्चर्यविभवे-
ऽप्यभक्तानां भावी कथमपि परप्रेमरभसः ॥

*vinā bījaṁ kiṁ nāṅkurajananamandho'pi na kathaṁ*
*prapaśyenno paṅgurgiriśikharamārohati katham |*
*yadi śrīcaitanye harirasamayāścaryavibhave-*
*'pyabhaktānāṁ bhāvī kathamapi parapremarabhasaḥ ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* yadi hari-rasamaya-ashcarya-vibhave — possessing supremely wondrous wealth, which is the *rasa* of *bhakti* for Hari Himself; shri-caitanye — in Sri Gaurasundara; abhaktanam api — even of those devoid of *bhakti*; para-prema-rabhasah — ("*para*" — the Supreme, Parameshvara; "*prema-rabhasa*" — the bliss of *prema*) the bliss of love for the Supreme; kathamapi — in some way; bhavi — will arise; [tada — then]; bijam vina kim na ankura-jananam [bhavet] — why does a sprout not appear without a seed?; andhah api katham na prapashyet — why does even the blind man not see?; panguh katham na giri-shikharam arohati — why does the lame man not climb the summit of Mount Sumeru? (that is, for those who have not taken shelter of Gaura's lotus feet, to taste the *rasa* of *prema* is utterly impossible).

**39.** Could a sprout not appear without a seed, a blind man not see, a lame man not climb a mountain peak — if even those not devoted to Sri Chaitanya, whose wondrous wealth is made of Hari-rasa, could somehow obtain the impulse of the highest love?

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *As without a seed no sprout appears, so without bhakti for Gaura it is impossible to obtain the secret love*

>> If those devoid of *bhakti* could, even with the greatest effort, ever awaken the bliss of love for the Supreme — for Sri Chaitanya-chandra, whose supremely wondrous wealth is the *rasa* of *bhakti* for Hari — then why should a sprout not appear without a seed? Why should the blind not see? And why should the lame not climb the summit of Mount Sumeru?

### Verse 40

अलौकिक्या प्रेमोन्मदरसविलासप्रथनया
न यः श्रीगोविन्दानुचरसचिवेष्वेषु कृतिषु ।
महाश्चर्यप्रेमोत्सवमपि हठाद्दातरि न य-
न्मतिर्गौरे साक्षात् पर इह स मूढो नरपशुः ॥

*alaukikyā premonmadarasavilāsaprathanayā*
*na yaḥ śrīgovindānucarasaciveṣveṣu kṛtiṣu |*
*mahāścaryapremotsavamapi haṭhāddātari na ya-*
*nmatirgaure sākṣāt para iha sa mūḍho narapaśuḥ ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* eshu shri-govinda-anucara-saciveshu kritishu — among those pious souls who, as associates, assist the pastimes of Sri Govinda; yah na [ganitah — one who is not counted; tasmai api — even to him]; alaukikya — in an unearthly way; prema-unmada-rasa-vilasa-prathanaya — by spreading the pastimes of *rasa* born of love — joy, pride and the rest; maha-ashcarya-prema-utsavam — the supremely wondrous bliss of *prema*; hathat datari sakshat gaure — towards Gaurasundara Himself, who suddenly bestows; yat-matih na [bhavet] — whose mind does not turn; iha — in this world; sah nara-pashuh — that human animal; parah mudhah — is the greatest fool.

**40.** He who, by an unearthly unfolding of the pastimes of the intoxicating *rasa* of love, suddenly gives the festival of great and wondrous love even to one who is not among these virtuous companions and ministers of Sri Govinda — whoever does not turn his mind to Gaura Himself is here the greatest of fools, a two-legged beast.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *A two-legged animal is he who has not turned his mind to Gaurahari, who gives unearthly love even to those who cling to the insentient*

>> Even one who is not counted among the pious souls who, as associates, assist in the pastimes of Sri Radha and Govinda, He suddenly endows with the wondrous bliss of *prema*, spreading in an unearthly way the pastimes of *rasa* born of love — joy, pride and the rest. Whoever has not turned his mind to this Sri Gaurasundara, manifest before our eyes, is a two-legged animal and the greatest fool in this world.

### Verse 41

असंख्याः श्रुत्यादौ भगवदवतारा निगदिताः
प्रभावं कः सम्भावयतु परमेशादितरतः ।
किमन्यत् स्वप्रेष्ठे कति कति सतां नाप्यनुभवा-
स्तथापि श्रीगौरे हरि हरि न मूढा हरिधियः ॥

*asaṁkhyāḥ śrutyādau bhagavadavatārā nigaditāḥ*
*prabhāvaṁ kaḥ sambhāvayatu parameśāditarataḥ |*
*kimanyat svapreṣṭhe kati kati satāṁ nāpyanubhavā-*
*stathāpi śrīgaure hari hari na mūḍhā haridhiyaḥ ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* shruti-adau — in the Vedas and the rest (in the Vedas, […] the natural commentary on the Vedanta, the *Srimad-Bhagavatam*, […] *shastras*); asankhyah — countless; avatarah — *avataras*; nigaditah — are named; [gaura-harau … — in Gaurahari …] (such wondrous power as is seen in Gaurahari, […]); prabhavam — such power; parama-ishat itaratah — in anyone other than Parameshvara; kah sambhavayatu — who can suppose?; anyat kim [vaktavyam] — what more is to be said?; sva-preshthe — in their beloved Gaurasundara; satam — of the saintly devotees; kati kati anubhavah api na [drishtah] — how many, how many experiences were there not? (the deliverance of Jagai and Madhai, […], the six-armed form shown to Sarvabhauma, […] — did they not realize Him as the Supreme?); hari hari — alas! (in grief); tathapi shri-gaure — still, in Gaurasundara; mudhah hari-dhiyah na — the fools have no understanding that He is Hari (the Supreme).

**41.** Countless incarnations of the Lord are named in the *shruti* and other scriptures — but who could ascribe such power to anyone other than the Supreme? And what more is there to say: how many, how many revelations have the saints had of their beloved Gaura! And yet, alas — Hari, Hari! — fools do not recognize Hari in Sri Gaura.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Deprived are those who doubt the divinity of Gaurahari, the crown of all avataras*

>> In the Vedas and the other *shastras* countless *avataras* are glorified, but who except Bhagavan Himself can display such wondrous power as is seen in Gaurahari? What more is there to say: did not the devotees see in their beloved Gaurahari six arms and many other wonders? Alas, alas, even seeing and hearing all this, the fools do not recognize the Supreme in Gaurahari.

### Verse 42

साक्षान्मोक्षादिकार्थान् विविधविकृतिभिस्तुच्छतां दर्शयन्तं
प्रेमानन्दं प्रसूते सकलतनुभृतां यस्य लीलाकटाक्षः ।
नासौ वेदेषु गूढो जगति यदि भवेदीश्वरो गौरचन्द्र-
स्तत्प्राप्तोऽनीशवादः शिव शिव गहने विष्णुमाये नमस्ते ॥

*sākṣānmokṣādikārthān vividhavikṛtibhistucchatāṁ darśayantaṁ*
*premānandaṁ prasūte sakalatanubhṛtāṁ yasya līlākaṭākṣaḥ |*
*nāsau vedeṣu gūḍho jagati yadi bhavedīśvaro gauracandra-*
*statprāpto'nīśavādaḥ śiva śiva gahane viṣṇumāye namaste ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* yasya lila-katakshah — the playful glance, full of *bhavas*, of that Gaurasundara; sakala-tanu-bhritam — of all embodied beings; sakshat moksha-adika-arthan — the direct goals of man, liberation and the rest; vividha-vikritibhih — by a multitude of transformations (*vikaras*); tucchatam darshayantam prema-anandam — the bliss of *prema*, which shows their insignificance, that is, their utter worthlessness (that is, the fifth goal, *prema-ananda*, "which makes liberation insignificant"); prasute — gives birth to (manifests); vedeshu gudhah asau gauracandrah — this Gaurachandra, hidden in the Vedas (hidden because He is the hidden *avatara*; that is, although the *shrutis* point to Gaurasundara with the *mantras* "*mahan prabhur vai purushah*" (*Shvetashvatara Upanishad* 3.12), "*rukma-varnam kartaram isham purusham brahma-yonim*" (*Mundaka Upanishad* 3.1.3) and others, for those who lack the realization of the wise this has not become an object of realization); jagati yadi ishvarah na bhavet — if He is not the one Lord in the world; tat anisha-vadah praptah — then atheism (the doctrine of godlessness) has come; shiva shiva — Shiva, Shiva!; [he] gahane vishnu-maye — O Vishnu-maya of inscrutable power; te — to you; namah — obeisance (that is, deluded by Vishnu-maya of inscrutable power, some cannot realize Gaurasundara as "the Supreme").

**42.** His playful glance gives rise in all embodied beings to the bliss of love, which by its various transformations shows the worthlessness even of liberation and the other goals. If this Gaurachandra, hidden in the Vedas, is not the Lord of the world, then the doctrine of a world without God is established of itself. Shiva, Shiva! Inscrutable art thou, O *maya* of Vishnu — obeisance to thee!

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Those who do not believe in Gaurahari, the giver of bhakti beside which liberation is insignificant, are atheists bewildered by maya*

>> Gaurasundara's playful glance, full of *bhavas*, gives rise in all embodied beings to the bliss of *prema*, which by a multitude of transformations (*vikaras*) shows how insignificant are the four goals of man headed by liberation. If this Sri Gaurachandra, hidden in the Vedas, is not the Lord of the world, that is, if the living beings of the world do not accept that He is "God Himself," then the world is covered by atheism! Shiva, Shiva! O inscrutable Vishnu-maya, how inscrutable is your power! Obeisance to you.

### Verse 43

धिगस्तु कुलमुज्ज्वलं धिगपि वाग्मितां धिग्यशो
धिगध्ययनमाकृतिं नववयःश्रियश्चास्तु धिक् ।
द्विजत्वमपि धिक् परं विमलमाश्रमाद्यं च धिक्
न चेत् परिचितः कलौ प्रकटगौरगोपीपतिः ॥

*dhigastu kulamujjvalaṁ dhigapi vāgmitāṁ dhigyaśo*
*dhigadhyayanamākṛtiṁ navavayaḥśriyaścāstu dhik |*
*dvijatvamapi dhik paraṁ vimalamāśramādyaṁ ca dhik*
*na cet paricitaḥ kalau prakaṭagauragopīpatiḥ ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* ujjvalam kulam dhik — shame on a noble lineage (shame on birth in a righteous family full of pious conduct); vagmitam api dhik — shame also on eloquence (wordiness); yashah [ca] dhik astu — shame on fame, or honour; adhyayanam dhik — shame on the study of the *shrutis* and the rest; akritim navam vayah shriyam [ca] dhik astu — shame on a beautiful build of hands, feet and other limbs, on full youth (*purna-kaishora*), on inexhaustible wealth; dvijatvam api dhik — shame also on receiving instruction in the Gayatri and so on after accepting the prescribed sacraments, *upanayana* and the rest; param vimalam ashrama-adyam ca dhik — shame also on the highest, pure *brahmacarya* and the other *ashramas* (the word "*adi*" also indicates [sacrifices], the practice of yoga, renunciation and the like — shame on them too); cet — if; kalau — in the age of Kali; prakata-gaura-gopi-patih — the manifest Gaura, the beloved of the *gopis* (Krishna, the beloved of the *gopis*, the embodiment of sweetness (*madhurya*), who, accepting the mood and lustre of "*gaura*," that is, of the golden Sri Radha, has appeared as the embodiment of the *rasa* of *vipralambha*); na paricitah [bhavet] — is not worshipped (does not become the object of worship).

**43.** Shame on an illustrious family, shame on eloquence, shame on fame, shame on learning, on a handsome form, on youth and on wealth; shame too on birth as a twice-born, on a pure *ashrama* and all the rest — if in the age of Kali one has not come to know Gaura, the Lord of the gopis made manifest.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Shame on the noble lineage, eloquence, learning, wealth and brahminhood of one who has turned away from Gaura*

>> If Sri Gauranga is not worshipped — Krishna, the beloved of the *gopis*, the embodiment of the *rasa* of sweetness (*madhurya*), who appeared in the age of Kali with the feelings and lustre of the golden Sri Radha as the embodiment of the *rasa* of separation (*vipralambha*) — then shame on a righteous noble lineage, shame on eloquence, shame on fame, shame on the study of the Vedas and the other *shastras*, shame on beauty, youth and wealth, shame on brahminhood adorned with the prescribed sacraments such as *upanayana*, shame on the pure *ashramas* such as *brahmacarya*, and also on yoga, sacrifices, renunciation and the rest.

### Verse 44

अहो वैकुण्ठस्थैरपि च भगवत्पार्षदवरैः
सरोमाञ्चं दृष्टा यदनुचरवक्रेश्वरमुखाः ।
महाश्चर्यप्रेमोज्ज्वलरससदावेशविवशी-
कृताङ्गास्तं गौरं कथमकृतपुण्यः प्रणयतु ॥

*aho vaikuṇṭhasthairapi ca bhagavatpārṣadavaraiḥ*
*saromāñcaṁ dṛṣṭā yadanucaravakreśvaramukhāḥ |*
*mahāścaryapremojjvalarasasadāveśavivaśī-*
*kṛtāṅgāstaṁ gauraṁ kathamakṛtapuṇyaḥ praṇayatu ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* aho — oh!; maha-ashcarya-prema-ujjvala-rasa-sada-avesha-vivashikrita-angah — those whose limbs are overpowered by constant absorption in the supremely wondrous radiant *rasa* of *prema*; yat-anucara-vakreshvara-mukhah — the associates of that Gaurasundara headed by Vakreshvara, connoisseurs of *madhura-rasa*, that is, Vakreshvara, Gadadhara, Raya Ramananda and others; vaikuntha-sthaih bhagavat-parshada-varaih api ca — even by the best associates of Narayana, who dwell in Vaikuntha, the abode of majesty (*aishvarya*); sa-romancam drishtah [abhavan] — were seen with gooseflesh (that is, seeing them, even the associates of Narayana in Vaikuntha cannot help getting gooseflesh from [rapture]); akrita-punyah — one devoid of piety; katham — how; tam gauram — that Gaurasundara; pranayatu — will be able to make the object of his love?

**44.** Oh! Even the best associates of the Lord who dwell in Vaikuntha look with thrilling awe, their hair standing on end, at His servants — Vakreshvara and the others, whose bodies are always overpowered by absorption in the wondrous *rasa* radiant with great love. How, then, will one devoid of merit come to love that Gaura?

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Gaurahari, surrounded by associates whom even the associates of Narayana admire, is not an object of love for one devoid of piety*

>> Seeing the great souls headed by Vakreshvara, whose bodies are overpowered by constant absorption in the wondrous radiant *rasa* of *prema*, even the associates of Narayana in Vaikuntha get gooseflesh. How can one devoid of piety make Sri Gaurasundara, whose servants are these great souls, connoisseurs of *madhura-rasa*, the object of his love?

### Verse 45

दत्त्वा यः कमपि प्रसादमथ सम्भाष्य स्मितश्रीमुखं
दूरात् स्निग्धदृशा निरीक्ष्य च महाप्रेमोत्सवं यच्छति ।
येषां हन्त कुतर्ककर्कशधिया तत्रापि नात्यादरः
साक्षात् पूर्णरसावतारिणि हरौ दुष्टा अमी केवलम् ॥

*dattvā yaḥ kamapi prasādamatha sambhāṣya smitaśrīmukhaṁ*
*dūrāt snigdhadṛśā nirīkṣya ca mahāpremotsavaṁ yacchati |*
*yeṣāṁ hanta kutarkakarkaśadhiyā tatrāpi nātyādaraḥ*
*sākṣāt pūrṇarasāvatāriṇi harau duṣṭā amī kevalam ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* yah — He who; kam api — to someone; prasadam — grace (favour); [dattva — having given]; smita-shri-mukham — shines with a softly smiling beautiful face; atha — then; sambhashya — having spoken; durat snigdha-drisha nirikshya ca — and having looked from afar with an affectionate glance; maha-prema-utsavam — the highest bliss of *prema*; yacchati — bestows; hanta — alas!; yesham — those who; kutarka-karkasha-dhiya — because of a mind hardened by false arguments; tatra sakshat-purna-rasa-avatarini harau api — even towards that Sri Gaurahari, whose nature is to bring down before our eyes, that is, to manifest, the complete *rasa* of *prema*; na atyadarah — have no great reverence; ami — they; kevalam dushtah — are simply wicked (have the nature of *asuras*).

**45.** He bestows His grace on whoever it may be, then speaks to him, and His beautiful face smiles; from afar He looks at him with a tender glance and gives the festival of great love. Those, alas, whose minds have been coarsened by false reasoning do not duly honour even Him — Hari, descended as the incarnation of complete *rasa*. They are simply wicked.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Whoever disregards Sri Gaurasundara, who gives love even to those who do not ask, is an asura*

>> He, having shown His grace to someone, shines with a softly smiling beautiful face, then speaks to him, looks at him from afar with an affectionate glance and bestows the highest love. Alas, those who, because of a mind hardened by false arguments, do not show the highest reverence even to Him — Sri Gaurahari, who manifests before our eyes the complete *rasa* of *prema* — are nothing but wicked *asuras*.

## Humility (dainya)

### Verse 46

वञ्चितोऽस्मि वञ्चितोऽस्मि वञ्चितोऽस्मि न संशयः ।
विश्वं गौररसे मग्नं स्पर्शोऽपि मम नाभवत् ॥

*vañcito'smi vañcito'smi vañcito'smi na saṁśayaḥ |*
*viśvaṁ gaurarase magnaṁ sparśo'pi mama nābhavat ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* vancitah asmi vancitah asmi vancitah asmi — I am cheated, cheated, certainly cheated; [yatah — because]; vishvam — the whole world; gaura-rase magnam — has plunged into the elevated radiant *rasa*, never given before (*anarpita-cari*), bestowed by Sri Gaurasundara, the embodiment of *vipralambha*; [kintu — but]; mama — of me; [tatra — with it]; sparshah api — even a touch; na abhavat — there was not.

**46.** I am cheated, I am cheated, I am cheated — there is no doubt! The whole world has been immersed in the *rasa* of Gaura, and it has not even touched me.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *The humility of a connoisseur of the rasa of love for Gaura*

>> I am cheated, cheated, truly I am cheated! The whole world has plunged into the ocean of the *rasa* of love for Sri Gaura, but me, alas, it has not even touched.

### Verse 47

कैर्वा सर्वपुमर्थमौलिरकृतायासैरिहासादितो
नासीद्गौरपदारविन्दरजसा स्पृष्टे महीमण्डले ।
हा हा धिङ् मम जीवनं धिगपि मे विद्यां धिगप्याश्रमं
यद्दौर्भाग्यभरादहो मम न तत्सम्बन्धगन्धोऽप्यभूत् ॥

*kairvā sarvapumarthamaulirakṛtāyāsairihāsādito*
*nāsīdgaurapadāravindarajasā spṛṣṭe mahīmaṇḍale |*
*hā hā dhiṅ mama jīvanaṁ dhigapi me vidyāṁ dhigapyāśramaṁ*
*yaddaurbhāgyabharādaho mama na tatsambandhagandho'pyabhūt ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* gaura-pada-aravinda-rajasa — by the pollen of Sri Gaurasundara's lotus feet; mahi-mandale sprishte [sati] — when the earth was touched; iha — in this world; kaih va — by whom; akrita-ayasaih — without effort; sarva-puma-artha-maulih — the crown of all the goals of man, *prema*; asaditah na asit — was not obtained? (that is, everyone obtained it); ha ha — alas, alas (in grief); mama — my; jivanam dhik — shame on the life; me — my; vidyam api dhik — shame also on the learning (knowledge of the *shastras* and the like); ashramam api dhik — shame also on the *ashrama*, *tridanda-sannyasa*, the fourth *ashrama*; yat — because; daurbhagya-bharat — through extreme misfortune; aho — oh!; mama — of me; tat-sambandha-gandhah api — even the scent of a connection with that [*prema*]; na abhut — there was not.

**47.** Who has not attained here, even without any effort, the crown of all the goals of man, on the earth touched by the dust of Gaura's lotus feet? Alas, alas! Shame on my life, shame on my learning, shame on my *ashrama* — through my great misfortune I have not had even a scent of connection with Him!

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Shame on the learning, the ashrama and the life of one who, at the time of Gaura's descent, remained deprived even of the scent of the rasa of love*

>> When the earth was touched by the pollen of Sri Gaura's lotus feet, who did not obtain "without practice" (without effort) *prema*, the crown of all the goals of man? But alas, alas, through my extreme misfortune I could not obtain even a particle of that *prema*! Shame on my life, shame on my learning, shame on my fourth *ashrama* — *tridanda-sannyasa*!

### Verse 48

उत्ससर्प जगदेव पूरयन् गौरचन्द्रकरुणामहार्णवः ।
बिन्दुमात्रमपि नापतन्महादुर्भगे मयि किमेतदद्भुतम् ॥

*utsasarpa jagadeva pūrayan gauracandrakaruṇāmahārṇavaḥ |*
*bindumātramapi nāpatanmahādurbhage mayi kimetadadbhutam ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* gauracandra-karuna-maha-arnavah — the great ocean of the Gaura-moon's mercy (the OCR is partly lost); jagat purayan eva — completely flooding the world; utsarpa — overflowed its shores (surged up); [kintu — but]; maha-durbhage mayi — on me, extremely unfortunate; bindu-matram api — even a drop of that mercy; na apatat — did not fall; etat kim adbhutam — what a wonder is this? (that is, everyone in the world is flooded with Gaurasundara's mercy; only I, through my extreme misfortune, could not receive even a drop).

**48.** The great ocean of Gaurachandra's mercy has overflowed its shores and flooded the whole world — yet not a single drop has fallen on me, a great unfortunate. What a wonder is this!

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Extremely unfortunate is one deprived of Gaura's merciful glance*

>> The great ocean of Sri Gaurachandra's mercy overflowed its shores and flooded the whole world, but, alas, what a wonder — not a single drop of it fell on an unfortunate one such as me!

### Verse 49

कालः कलिर्बलिन इन्द्रियवैरिवर्गाः
श्रीभक्तिमार्ग इह कण्टककोटिरुद्धः ।
हा हा क्व यामि विकलः किमहं करोमि
चैतन्यचन्द्र यदि नाद्य कृपां करोषि ॥

*kālaḥ kalirbalina indriyavairivargāḥ*
*śrībhaktimārga iha kaṇṭakakoṭiruddhaḥ |*
*hā hā kva yāmi vikalaḥ kimahaṁ karomi*
*caitanyacandra yadi nādya kṛpāṁ karoṣi ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* kalah kalih — the time is Kali (the promoter of *adharma*); indriya-vairi-vargah — the hosts of the enemies, the senses; balinah — are very strong; iha — in this age of Kali; shri-bhakti-margah — the supreme radiant path of *bhakti*; kantaka-koti-ruddhah — is blocked by countless thorns in the form of *karma*, *jnana* and the like; [atah — therefore]; [he] caitanya-candra — O Chaitanya-moon; yadi adya kripam — if today mercy; na karoshi — You do not show; [tada — then]; ha ha — alas, alas; vikalah — bewildered (overwhelmed, that is, afflicted by all these faults of Kali); aham — I; kim karomi — what am I to do?; kva — where; yami — am I to go?

**49.** The time is Kali; the enemies, the senses, are strong; the sacred path of *bhakti* is here barred by millions of thorns. Alas, alas, where shall I go, distraught? What shall I do, O Chaitanya-chandra, if You do not show mercy today?

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *In the age of Kali, without Gaura's mercy it is impossible to enter the path of bhakti, blocked by millions of thorns*

>> The time is Kali; the enemies, the senses, are extremely strong, and the supreme radiant path of *bhakti* is blocked by a net of millions of thorns. Therefore, O Chaitanya-chandra, if You do not show mercy today, what, alas, am I to do, bewildered as I am (that is, having lost my wits in searching for pure *bhakti*, for in the age of Kali the path of *bhakti* is covered by many mental speculations); where am I to go?

### Verse 50

सोऽप्याश्चर्यमयः प्रभुर्नयनयोर्यन्नाभवद्गोचरो
यन्नास्वादि हरेः पदाम्बुजरसस्तद्यद्गतं तद्गतम् ।
एतावन्मम तावदस्तु जगतीं येऽन्येऽप्यलङ्कुर्वते
श्रीचैतन्यपदे निखातमनसस्तैर्यत्प्रसङ्गोत्सवः ॥

*so'pyāścaryamayaḥ prabhurnayanayoryannābhavadgocaro*
*yannāsvādi hareḥ padāmbujarasastadyadgataṁ tadgatam |*
*etāvanmama tāvadastu jagatīṁ ye'nye'pyalaṅkurvate*
*śrīcaitanyapade nikhātamanasastairyatprasaṅgotsavaḥ ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* sah api ashcaryamayah prabhuh — even that Lord Gaurahari, full of supreme wonders; yat — that through misfortune; nayanayoh — to the eyes; gocarah na abhavat — did not become visible; yat — or that for some reason; hareh pada-ambuja-rasah — the *rasa* of Hari's lotus feet; na asvadi — was not tasted; tat gatam tat gatam [eva] — what has been has passed; [kintu — but]; mama — for me; etavat [eva] — only this; [astu — let there be]; shri-caitanya-pade nikhata-manasah — those who have fixed their minds on Sri Chaitanya's lotus feet; ye anye api — others who; jagatim — the earth; alankurvate — adorn; taih — with them; yat prasanga-utsavah — elevated association (*pra* — excellently, *sanga* — association) and its bliss; tavat astu — let there be.

**50.** That wondrous Lord never came before my eyes, I never tasted the *rasa* of Hari's lotus feet — what is gone is gone. But let me have at least this one thing: the festival of association with those who adorn the earth, their minds fixed in the feet of Sri Chaitanya.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Only association with Gaura's people, the ornaments of the world, is desirable*

>> Though through my misfortune that Lord Gaurahari, full of supreme wonders, never came before my eyes (that is, even seeing Him, I could not see His true nature for lack of the realization of the wise), though for some reason it did not fall to my lot to taste love for Hari's lotus feet — what has been has been. Now I have only one prayer: may I have the festival of elevated association with those other devotees who, having fixed their minds on Sri Chaitanya's lotus feet, still adorn the earth.

### Verse 51

दुष्कर्मकोटिनिरतस्य दुरन्तघोर-
दुर्वासनानिगडशृङ्खलितस्य गाढम् ।
क्लिश्यन्मतेः कुमतिकोटिकदर्थितस्य
गौरं विनाद्य मम को भवितेह बन्धुः ॥

*duṣkarmakoṭiniratasya durantaghora-*
*durvāsanānigaḍaśṛṅkhalitasya gāḍham |*
*kliśyanmateḥ kumatikoṭikadarthitasya*
*gauraṁ vinādya mama ko bhaviteha bandhuḥ ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* dushkarma-koti-niratasya — of one given to millions of evil deeds; gadham — firmly; duranta-ghora-durvasana-nigada-shrinkhalitasya — bound by the chains of untamable, terrible evil tendencies; klishyat-mateh — with a mind exhausted [by labours for] *karma*, *jnana* and the like; kumati-koti-kadarthitasya — led and overpowered by countless people of evil mind, obstinate in *karma*; mama — to me; gauram vina — except Gaurahari; adya — today; iha — in this world; kah bandhuh bhavita — who will become a friend?

**51.** I am given to millions of evil deeds, firmly bound in the chains of endless, terrible evil tendencies; my mind is in torment, harassed by millions of false views. Who but Gaura will be my friend here today?

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *In the age of Kali the only saviour of the fallen is Gaurahari*

>> I am wholly given to millions of evil deeds, firmly bound by the chains of untamable, terrible evil tendencies, my mind is exhausted by labours for *karma*, *jnana* and the like, millions of evil thoughts drag me along a false path and overpower me. Who but Gaurahari will today in this world become the friend of one as wretched as I?

### Verse 52

हा हन्त हन्त परमोषरचित्तभूमौ
व्यर्थीभवन्ति मम साधनकोटयोऽपि ।
सर्वात्मना तदहमद्भुतभक्तिबीजं
श्रीगौरचन्द्रचरणं शरणं करोमि ॥

*hā hanta hanta paramoṣaracittabhūmau*
*vyarthībhavanti mama sādhanakoṭayo'pi |*
*sarvātmanā tadahamadbhutabhaktibījaṁ*
*śrīgauracandracaraṇaṁ śaraṇaṁ karomi ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* ha hanta hanta — alas! alas! alas!; mama — of my; parama-ushara-citta-bhumau — in the supremely barren soil of the heart; sadhana-kotayah api — even millions of practices; vyarthah bhavanti — are lost in vain; tat — therefore; aham — I; sarvatmana — with all my heart; adbhuta-bhakti-bijam — which are the seed of unearthly (*aprakrita*) *bhakti*; shri-gauracandra-caranam — the lotus feet of Sri Gaurachandra; sharanam karomi — take as my shelter.

**52.** Alas, alas! On the utterly barren soil of my heart even millions of my practices go to waste. Therefore with all my soul I take refuge in the feet of Sri Gaurachandra — the wondrous seed of *bhakti*.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Without the shelter of Gaura's feet all means of practice are fruitless*

>> Alas, alas! In the extremely hard, barren soil of my heart millions of seeds of practice are lost in vain! Therefore I now with all my heart take shelter of the lotus feet of Sri Gaura — the seed of unearthly *bhakti*.

### Verse 53

हा हन्त चित्तभुवि मे परमोषरायां
सद्भक्तिकल्पलतिकाङ्कुरिता कथं स्यात् ।
हृद्येकमेव परमाश्वसनीयमस्ति
चैतन्यनाम कलयन्न कदापि शोच्यः ॥

*hā hanta cittabhuvi me paramoṣarāyāṁ*
*sadbhaktikalpalatikāṅkuritā kathaṁ syāt |*
*hṛdyekameva paramāśvasanīyamasti*
*caitanyanāma kalayanna kadāpi śocyaḥ ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* ha hanta — alas!; me — of my; parama-usharayam citta-bhuvi — in the extremely barren soil of the heart; katham — how; sat-bhakti-kalpa-latika-ankurita bhavet — will the sprout of *bhakti* in the form of *prema* — the *sthayi-bhava*, or *rati* — appear?; [kintu — but]; hridi — in the heart; ekam eva — the only; param ashvasaniyam — chief consolation; asti — there is; [yat — that]; caitanya-nama — the name of Chaitanya; kalayan — repeating; kadapi — ever; na shocyah — one is not an object of grief.

**53.** Alas, alas! How will the sprout of the wish-fulfilling creeper of true *bhakti* break through on the utterly barren soil of my heart? But in my heart there is one supreme consolation: one who chants the name "Chaitanya" will never be pitiable.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *One has only to utter the name of Sri Chaitanya, and even in a hard heart the creeper of the desires of bhakti sprouts*

>> Alas, alas! How will the sprout of *prema-bhakti*, that is, the *sthayi-bhava* or *rati*, grow in the extremely barren soil of my heart? There is no hope! But here is the one supreme consolation: whoever utters the name of Sri Chaitanya never has anything to grieve over.

### Verse 54

संसारदुःखजलधौ पतितस्य काम-
क्रोधादिनक्रमकरैः कवलीकृतस्य ।
दुर्वासनानिगडितस्य निराश्रयस्य
चैतन्यचन्द्र मम देहि पदावलम्बम् ॥

*saṁsāraduḥkhajaladhau patitasya kāma-*
*krodhādinakramakaraiḥ kavalīkṛtasya |*
*durvāsanānigaḍitasya nirāśrayasya*
*caitanyacandra mama dehi padāvalambam ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* [he] caitanya-candra — O Chaitanya-moon!; samsara-duhkha-jaladhau patitasya — to one fallen into the ocean of the miseries of *samsara*; kama-krodha-adi-nakra-makaraih kavalikritasya — seized by the crocodiles — lust and anger — and by the *makaras* and the rest; durvasana-nigaditasya — bound by the chains of evil tendencies; nirashrayasya — without support; mama — to me; pada-avalambanam — the shelter of the feet; dehi — give.

**54.** I have fallen into the ocean of worldly suffering, I have been swallowed by the crocodiles and sea-monsters of lust, anger and the rest, I am fettered by evil tendencies and have no refuge. O Chaitanya-chandra, give me the support of Your feet!

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Sri Chaitanya is the shelter of the shelterless*

>> O Chaitanya-chandra, I have fallen into the ocean of worldly miseries, my hands and feet are bound by the chains of evil tendencies, I have no support; the crocodiles and sea monsters (*makaras*) of lust, anger and the rest have swallowed me. (O Lord! In such a calamity) save me by giving me shelter in the boat of Your feet!

### Verse 55

मृग्यापि सा शिवशुकोद्धवनारदाद्यै-
राश्चर्यभक्तिपदवी न दवीयसी नः ।
दुर्बोधवैभवपते मयि पामरेऽपि
चैतन्यचन्द्र यदि ते करुणाकटाक्षः ॥

*mṛgyāpi sā śivaśukoddhavanāradādyai-*
*rāścaryabhaktipadavī na davīyasī naḥ |*
*durbodhavaibhavapate mayi pāmare'pi*
*caitanyacandra yadi te karuṇākaṭākṣaḥ ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* [he] durbodha-vaibhava-pate caitanya-candra — O Chaitanya-moon, master of inconceivable power (*shakti*); pamare api mayi — even on me, a fallen one; yadi te — if Your; karuna-katakshah — merciful glance; [syat — falls]; [tada — then]; shiva-shuka-uddhava-narada-adyaih api — even by the great souls Shiva, Shuka, Uddhava, Narada and others; mrigya — sought; sa ashcarya-bhakti-padavi — that wondrous path of *bhakti*; nah — from us; na daviyasi — will not be far.

**55.** That wondrous path of *bhakti* which Shiva, Shuka, Uddhava, Narada and others seek is not far even from us, O Chaitanya-chandra, Lord of inconceivable wealth, if Your merciful glance falls even on me, a wretch.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *The path of bhakti, hard to reach even for the gods, is by Gaura's mercy accessible even to the fallen*

>> O Chaitanya-chandra, You are the master of inconceivable wealth! If You cast Your merciful glance even on a fallen one such as me, then even for us that supreme, wondrous, radiant path of *bhakti*, which even the great souls — Shiva, Shuka, Narada, Uddhava and the others — seek, will not be far away.

### Verse 56

क्व सा निरङ्कुशा कृपा क्व तद्वैभवमद्भुतम् ।
क्व सा वत्सलता शौरे गौरे यादृक् तवात्मनि ॥

*kva sā niraṅkuśā kṛpā kva tadvaibhavamadbhutam |*
*kva sā vatsalatā śaure gaure yādṛk tavātmani ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* [he] shaure — O Krishna of the Shura dynasty; tava gaure atmani — in Your golden form; yadrik — such as; nirankusha-kripa — causeless mercy; sa kva — where else is such?; tat adbhutam vaibhavam kva — where else is such supremely wondrous glory?; sa vatsalata [ca] kva — where else is such *vatsalya* (love for the devotees)?

**56.** O Shauri (Krishna)! Where else is such unrestrained mercy, where such wondrous majesty, where such tenderness as in Yourself in the form of Gaura?

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *It is impossible to find anyone other than Gaurahari whose mercy is so boundless*

>> O Krishna, lamp of the Shura dynasty! Where else is to be found such causeless mercy as in Your golden form? Where else such wealth, astonishing all the worlds? Where else such love for the devotees?

---

# Part Three

*Fidelity to the object of worship. Instruction to the world (verses 57–98)*

## Fidelity to the object of worship (upasya-nishtha)

### Verse 57

स्वतेजसा कृष्णपदारविन्दमहारसावेशितविश्वमीशम् ।
कमप्यशेषश्रुतिगूढवेशं गौराङ्गमङ्गीकुरु मूढचेतः ॥ ५७ ॥

*svatejasā kṛṣṇapadāravindamahārasāveśitaviśvamīśam |*
*kamapyaśeṣaśrutigūḍhaveśaṁ gaurāṅgamaṅgīkuru mūḍhacetaḥ || 57 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* [he — O]; mudha-cetah — foolish heart; sva-tejasa — by His own power (influence); krishna-pada-aravinda-maha-rasa-aveshita-vishvam — Him who by the elevated radiant (*unnata-ujjvala*) *rasa* of the lotus feet of Sri Krishna, His own form (*sva-svarupa*), has filled the universe with *anuraga* (attachment); ashesha-shruti-gudha-vesham — Him who abides secretly among all the *shrutis*, that is, has entered them in a hidden form; kam api isham gaurangam — that indescribable Supreme, Gaurahari; angikuru — accept, that is, take shelter of His lotus feet.

**57.** O foolish heart, accept Gauranga — the Lord who by His own splendour has immersed the whole world in the great *rasa* of Krishna's lotus feet, the indescribable One whose form is hidden in all the *shrutis*!

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Leaving […], everyone must take shelter of Gaurahari, hidden in the shrutis*

>> O foolish heart! Take shelter of the lotus feet of the indescribable Supreme — Sri Gaurasundara, who by His power has filled the whole world with love for the supreme *rasa* of *prema* of the lotus feet of Sri Krishna, His own form (*sva-svarupa*), and who is secretly present in all the *shrutis*, that is, hidden in the Vedas.

### Verse 58

श्रवणमननसङ्कीर्त्यादिभक्त्या मुरारे-
र्यदि परमपुमर्थं साधयेत् कोऽपि भद्रम् ।
मम तु परमपारप्रेमपीयूषसिन्धोः
किमपि रसरहस्यं गौरधाम्नो नमस्यम् ॥ ५८ ॥

*śravaṇamananasaṅkīrtyādibhaktyā murāre-*
*ryadi paramapumarthaṁ sādhayet ko'pi bhadram |*
*mama tu paramapārapremapīyūṣasindhoḥ*
*kimapi rasarahasyaṁ gauradhāmno namasyam || 58 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* yadi kah api — if someone; murareh — of Murari; shravana-manana-sankirti-adi-bhaktya — by the ninefold *sadhana-bhakti* — hearing, reflection and the rest; parama-puma-artham — the highest goal of man, *prema*; sadhayet — attains by practice; [tarhi — then]; bhadram — good; let him attain it; mama tu — for me, however; apara-prema-piyusha-sindhoh gaura-dhamnah — of Sri Hari, who has a golden lustre (*gaura-kanti*) and is the ocean of the nectar of boundless *prema*; kim api rasa-rahasyam — whatever secret there is in the *rasa* of *bhakti*, that is, the treasure of love; param namasyam — is alone worthy of worship.

**58.** If someone attains the highest goal of man through devotion to Murari — hearing, reflection, *sankirtana* and the rest — good for him. But for me the object of worship is the indescribable secret of the *rasa* of Hari clothed in a golden radiance,[^03-1] a shoreless ocean of the nectar of supreme love.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *The secret of rasa given by Gaura is more wondrous than the love for Krishna born of practice according to rules (vaidhi)*

>> If someone, worshipping Murari by means of *bhakti-yoga* — hearing, chanting and the rest — attains the highest goal of man, let him attain it; that is good. For me, however, the only object of worship is that secret treasure of love which is hidden in the *rasa* of *bhakti* for Sri Hari in golden lustre — the ocean of the nectar of boundless *prema*.

### Verse 59

ईशं भजन्तु पुरुषार्थचतुष्टयाशा
दासा भवन्तु च विहाय हरेरुपास्यान् ।
किञ्चिद्रहस्यपदलोभितधीरहं तु
चैतन्यचन्द्रचरणं शरणं करोमि ॥ ५९ ॥

*īśaṁ bhajantu puruṣārthacatuṣṭayāśā*
*dāsā bhavantu ca vihāya harerupāsyān |*
*kiñcidrahasyapadalobhitadhīrahaṁ tu*
*caitanyacandracaraṇaṁ śaraṇaṁ karomi || 59 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* purusha-artha-catushtaya-ashah — those who long for the four goals — *dharma*, *artha*, *kama* and *moksha* — some of them; isham — the Supreme; bhajantu — let them worship; [anyan — other]; upasyan — objects of worship; vihaya — leaving; hareh dasah ca bhavantu — let them become exclusive servants of Hari; aham tu — I, however; kincit rahasya-pada-lobhita-dhih [san — being] — longing to taste a certain extremely rare, secret love of Radha and Krishna; caitanya-candra-caranam sharanam karomi — take shelter of the feet of the Chaitanya-moon.

**59.** Let some worship the Lord in the hope of the four goals of man; let others, giving up other objects of worship, become servants of Hari. But my mind is enticed by a certain secret state, and I take refuge in the feet of Chaitanya-chandra.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *The secret love of Radha and Krishna, unattainable even by the exclusive devotees of Krishna, is obtained only in the shelter of Chaitanya's feet*

>> Let someone worship the Lord of the world in the hope of *dharma*, *artha*, *kama* and *moksha* — let him worship; let someone, leaving all other objects of worship, become an exclusive servant of Sri Hari — let him become so. I, however, longing to taste that extremely rare, secret *rasa* of the love of Sri Radha and Krishna, take shelter of the feet of Sri Chaitanya-chandra.

### Verse 60

निष्ठां प्राप्ता व्यवहृतिततिर्लौकिकी वैदिकी वा
या वा लज्जा प्रहसनसमुद्गाननाट्योत्सवेषु ।
ये वाभूवन्नहह सहजप्राणदेहार्थधर्मा
गौरश्चौरः सकलमहरत् कोऽपि मे तीव्रवीर्यः ॥ ६० ॥

*niṣṭhāṁ prāptā vyavahṛtitatirlaukikī vaidikī vā*
*yā vā lajjā prahasanasamudgānanāṭyotsaveṣu |*
*ye vābhūvannahaha sahajaprāṇadehārthadharmā*
*gauraścauraḥ sakalamaharat ko'pi me tīvravīryaḥ || 60 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* laukiki-vaidiki-nishtham prapta va vyavahriti-tatih — that series of worldly and Vedic dealings in which I was firmly established; prahasana-samudgana-natya-utsaveshu ya va lajja — that shame which there was at laughter (jokes), loud chanting, dancing, festivals and the like; ahaha — oh!; ye va sahaja-prana-deha-artha-dharmah — those natural properties that are the cause (support) of life and body; abhuvan — that there were; kah api tivra-viryah gaurah caurah — a certain thief, very powerful, who has assumed the form of Gaura; me sakalam aharat — has stolen all this of mine.

**60.** The whole series of worldly and Vedic duties in which I was established; my bashfulness at jesting, loud singing, dancing and festivals; the natural duties tied to life and body — alas! all of this has been stolen by a certain thief, Gaura, whose might is irresistible.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Sri Gaurahari steals away the duties of the body, the properties of the mind and the like*

>> My adherence to worldly and Vedic dealings, my bashfulness at loud laughter, loud chanting, dancing and the like at festivals, and all the natural properties of life and body that I had — all this has been stolen by some thief who has assumed the golden form of Gaura, whose power is irresistible.

### Verse 61

सान्द्रानन्दोज्ज्वलरसमयप्रेमपीयूषसिन्धोः
कोटिं वर्षन् किमपि करुणास्निग्धनेत्राञ्चलेन ।
कोऽयं देवः कनककदलीगर्भगौराङ्गयष्टि-
श्चेतोऽकस्मान्मम निजपदे गाढयुक्तं चकार ॥ ६१ ॥

*sāndrānandojjvalarasamayapremapīyūṣasindhoḥ*
*koṭiṁ varṣan kimapi karuṇāsnigdhanetrāñcalena |*
*ko'yaṁ devaḥ kanakakadalīgarbhagaurāṅgayaṣṭi-*
*śceto'kasmānmama nijapade gāḍhayuktaṁ cakāra || 61 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* karuna-snigdha-netra-ancalena — with the corners of His eyes, moist with compassion; sandra-ananda-ujjvala-rasamaya-prema-piyusha-sindhoh — of the ocean of the nectar of *prema*, consisting of *rasa* that is dense (*sandra*), that is, […], and radiant (*ujjvala*), that is, ever renewing itself; kim api — indescribable; kotim varshan — pouring millions; kah ayam — who is this; kanaka-kadali-garbha-gauranga-yashtih devah — the playful (*lilamaya*) Purusha, whose beautiful slender body is golden like the core of a golden plantain tree; akasmat — suddenly; mama cetah — my heart; nija-pade — on His own, that is, His holy feet; gadha-yuktam cakara — has firmly fixed.

**61.** Pouring forth millions of oceans of the nectar of love made of the dense and radiant *rasa* of bliss through the indescribable corner of His eyes moist with compassion — who is this Lord, His slender golden body like the heart of a golden plantain tree, who has suddenly bound my mind fast to His feet?

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Sri Gaurahari forcibly binds the mind to His lotus feet*

>> Who is this indescribable, playful Lord with a golden body like the core of a golden plantain tree, who, pouring from the corners of His eyes, moist with compassion, millions of oceans of the nectar of *prema*, full of dense, blissful, radiant *rasa*, has suddenly bound my mind firmly to His lotus feet?

### Verse 62

स्वयं देवो यत्र द्रुतकनकगौरः करुणया
महाप्रेमानन्दोज्ज्वलरसवपुः प्रादुरभवत् ।
नवद्वीपे तस्मिन् प्रतिभवनभक्त्युत्सवमये
मनो मे वैकुण्ठादपि च मधुरे धाम्नि रमते ॥ ६२ ॥

*svayaṁ devo yatra drutakanakagauraḥ karuṇayā*
*mahāpremānandojjvalarasavapuḥ prādurabhavat |*
*navadvīpe tasmin pratibhavanabhaktyutsavamaye*
*mano me vaikuṇṭhādapi ca madhure dhāmni ramate || 62 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* yatra — where; druta-kanaka-gaurah — golden like molten gold; maha-prema-ananda-ujjvala-rasa-vapuh — possessing a body consisting of *shringara-rasa* in the form of *mahabhava*; devah — the playful Bhagavan; karunaya — out of mercy (subject to mercy); svayam pradurabhavat — appeared of His own accord; tasmin — in that; prati-bhavana-bhakti-utsavamaye — where every house is full of the festival of the goddess of *prema-bhakti*; vaikunthat api ca madhure dhamni navadvipe — in Navadvipa, the abode full of a sweetness greater even than Vaikuntha; me manah — my mind; ramate — delights (abides in pastimes).

**62.** Where God Himself, golden as molten gold, His body the radiant *rasa* of great love and bliss, appeared out of mercy — in Navadvipa, where every house is a festival of *bhakti* — in that sweet abode my mind delights even more than in Vaikuntha.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *The glory of [Navadvipa], the best abode (pitha) of prema-bhakti*

>> Where by His mercy the playful Bhagavan, golden like molten gold, the embodiment of *shringara-rasa* in the form of *mahabhava*, has appeared of His own accord, where every house is full of the festivals of the goddess of *prema-bhakti* — in that abode, Navadvipa, full of greater sweetness even than Vaikuntha, my mind roams.

### Verse 63

यत्तद्वदन्तु शास्त्राणि यत्तद्व्याख्यान्तु तार्किकाः ।
जीवनं मम चैतन्यपादाम्भोजसुधैव तु ॥ ६३ ॥

*yattadvadantu śāstrāṇi yattadvyākhyāntu tārkikāḥ |*
*jīvanaṁ mama caitanyapādāmbhojasudhaiva tu || 63 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* shastrani — the *shastras*; yat tat vadatu — let them say whatever they please; tarkikah ca — and the logicians; yat yat vyakhyantu — let them interpret as they please; tu — but; caitanya-pada-ambhoja-sudha eva — the nectar of Chaitanya's lotus feet alone; mama jivanam — is my life.

**63.** Let the shastras say what they will, let the logicians explain as they will — my life is only the nectar of the lotus feet of Chaitanya.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Fidelity to Gaura's lotus feet*

>> Let the *shastras* say what they please, let the learned logicians interpret as they like — but the nectar of Sri Chaitanya's lotus feet alone is my life.

### Verse 64

पतन्ति यदि सिद्धयः करतले स्वयं दुर्लभाः
स्वयं च यदि सेवकीभवितुमागताः स्युः सुराः ।
किमन्यदिदमेव वा यदि चतुर्भुजं स्याद्वपु-
स्तथापि मम नो मनाक् चलति गौरचन्द्रान्मनः ॥ ६४ ॥

*patanti yadi siddhayaḥ karatale svayaṁ durlabhāḥ*
*svayaṁ ca yadi sevakībhavitumāgatāḥ syuḥ surāḥ |*
*kimanyadidameva vā yadi caturbhujaṁ syādvapu-*
*stathāpi mama no manāk calati gauracandrānmanaḥ || 64 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* durlabhah siddhayah [api] — even extremely rare *siddhis*; yadi kara-tale svayam patanti — if they come of themselves into my hand; surah — the gods; sevaki bhavitum — to become servants; yadi svayam ca agatah syuh — if they come of their own accord; kim anyat — what more (moreover); yadi va idam eva vapuh — if even this very body; catur-bhujam syat — becomes four-armed, that is, if even *sarupya-mukti* is obtained; tathapi mama manah — still my mind; gauracandrat — from Gaurachandra; manak no calati — will not swerve in the least.

**64.** Should rare *siddhis* fall into my palm of themselves, should the gods themselves come to become my servants, should I even — what more? — have a four-armed body, still my mind would not stir for a moment from Gaurachandra.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Deep attachment to Gaura's lotus feet*

>> Let the extremely rare *siddhis* — *anima* and the rest — come of themselves and lie in my palm like an *amalaki* fruit; let all the gods come of their own accord to serve me; more than that, let even this body of mine become four-armed — still my mind will not swerve a hair's breadth from Sri Gaurachandra.

### Verse 65

वासो मे वरमस्तु घोरदहनज्वालावलीपञ्जरे
श्रीचैतन्यपदारविन्दविमुखैर्मा कुत्रचित् सङ्गमः ।
वैकुण्ठादिपदं स्वयं च मिलितं नो मे मनो लिप्सते
पादाम्भोजरजश्छटा यदि मनाक् गौरस्य नो रस्यते ॥ ६५ ॥

*vāso me varamastu ghoradahanajvālāvalīpañjare*
*śrīcaitanyapadāravindavimukhairmā kutracit saṅgamaḥ |*
*vaikuṇṭhādipadaṁ svayaṁ ca militaṁ no me mano lipsate*
*pādāmbhojarajaśchaṭā yadi manāk gaurasya no rasyate || 65 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* ghora-dahana-jvala-avali-panjare — in a cage of many tongues of terrible flame; varam me vasah astu — better let my dwelling be; [kintu — but, tathapi — still]; shri-caitanya-pada-aravinda-vimukhaih — with those who have turned away from Chaitanya's lotus feet; kutracit — at any time; sangamah — association; ma — not; [astu — let there be]; yadi gaurasya pada-ambhoja-rajah-chata — if the radiance of the pollen of Chaitanya's lotus feet; manak — even a little; no rasyate — cannot be tasted; [tada — then]; me manah — my mind; svayam militam vaikuntha-adi padam ca — even the abode of Vaikuntha and the rest, obtained of themselves, without *sadhana* and the like; no lipsate — does not wish to obtain.

**65.** Better for me to live in a cage of the tongues of a terrible fire than to associate anywhere with those who have turned away from the lotus feet of Sri Chaitanya. Even if the abode of Vaikuntha and the rest came to me of itself, my mind would not desire it if it did not taste even a little of the lustre of the dust of Gaura's lotus feet.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Rejection of association with the asuras who have turned away from Gaura*

>> If I must live even in a cage full of terrible tongues of flame — so be it, only let me never have association with people who have turned away from Sri Chaitanya's lotus feet. If my mind does not taste even a little of the radiance of love for the pollen of Sri Gaura's lotus feet, it will not desire even the abode of Vaikuntha and the rest, though it come of itself, without any *sadhana*.

### Verse 66

आस्तां नाम महान् महानिति रवः सर्वक्षमामण्डले
लोके वा प्रकटास्तु नाम महती सिद्धिश्चमत्कारिणी ।
कामं चारुचतुर्भुजत्वमयतामाराध्य विश्वेश्वरं
चेतो मे बहु मन्यते नहि नहि श्रीगौरभक्तिं विना ॥ ६६ ॥

*āstāṁ nāma mahān mahāniti ravaḥ sarvakṣamāmaṇḍale*
*loke vā prakaṭāstu nāma mahatī siddhiścamatkāriṇī |*
*kāmaṁ cārucaturbhujatvamayatāmārādhya viśveśvaraṁ*
*ceto me bahu manyate nahi nahi śrīgaurabhaktiṁ vinā || 66 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* sarva-kshama-mandale — throughout the whole circle of the earth; mahan mahan iti ravah — such a report (fame): "he is a great man," "he is a great man"; astam — let there be; nama — (a particle expressing concession); va — or; loke — on earth; mahati — long-lasting; camatkarini siddhih — a supernatural *siddhi*; prakata astu — let it appear; nama — (a particle expressing concession); [kim va — or else]; vishva-ishvaram — the Lord of the universe, Vishnu; aradhya — worshipping; kamam — desired for oneself; caru-catur-bhujatvam — a charming four-armed form; ayatam — let it come; [kintu — but]; shri-gaura-bhaktim vina — without *bhakti* for Sri Gaura; me cetah — my mind; [etat sarvam — all this]; na hi bahumanyate na hi — truly does not value, truly does not value.

**66.** Let the cry go round me over the whole earth: "Great, great!" Let a great and wondrous *siddhi* be manifested to the world. Let me, worshipping the Lord of the universe, attain a beautiful four-armed form — no, no, without devotion to Sri Gaura my mind values none of this!

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Without Gaura's lotus feet, enjoyment, liberation and siddhis are utterly insignificant*

>> Let my fame spread over the whole earth: "Great, great!"; or let an everlasting, unearthly *siddhi* appear on earth; or let me, worshipping Vishnu, the Lord of the universe, obtain the desired beautiful four-armed form — but without *bhakti* for Gaura my mind never values any of this, never values it.

### Verse 67

चैतन्येति कृपामयेति परमोदारेति नानाविध-
प्रेमावेशितसर्वभूतहृदयेत्याश्चर्यधामन्निति ।
गौराङ्गेति गुणार्णवेति रसरूपेति स्वनामप्रिये-
त्यश्रान्तं मम जल्पतो जनिरियं यायादिति प्रार्थये ॥ ६७ ॥

*caitanyeti kṛpāmayeti paramodāreti nānāvidha-*
*premāveśitasarvabhūtahṛdayetyāścaryadhāmanniti |*
*gaurāṅgeti guṇārṇaveti rasarūpeti svanāmapriye-*
*tyaśrāntaṁ mama jalpato janiriyaṁ yāyāditi prārthaye || 67 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* [he — O]; caitanya iti — "Chaitanya" (the word "Chaitanya" means the form consisting of being, consciousness and bliss, or He who attracts the hearts of all beings — O such Chaitanya); [he — O]; kripamaya iti — "O merciful one"; [he — O]; parama-udara iti — "O most magnanimous"; [he — O]; nana-vidha-prema-aveshita-sarva-bhuta-hridaya iti — "O You by whom the hearts of all beings are filled with various kinds of *prema* — *dasya*, *sakhya*, *vatsalya* and *madhurya*"; [he — O]; ashcarya-dhaman iti — "O possessor of a supremely wondrous lustre like that of molten gold"; [he — O]; gauranga iti — "O Gaurasundara"; [he — O]; guna-arnava iti — "O ocean of auspicious qualities"; [he — O]; rasa-rupa iti — "O embodied form of *vipralambha-rasa*"; [he — O]; sva-nama-priya iti — "O lover of Your own names" — thus; ashrantam jalpatah — incessantly chanting; mama iyam janih — this life of mine; yayat — may it pass; iti prarthaye — for this alone I pray.

**67.** "Chaitanya! Merciful One! Supremely generous One! You who have filled the hearts of all beings with love of many kinds! You whose wondrous radiance is like molten gold! Gauranga! Ocean of virtues! Embodiment of *rasa*! You who love Your own names!" — I pray that, ceaselessly repeating this, I may pass this life.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *The prayer of Gaura's devotee*

>> "O Chaitanya-chandra, who attract the hearts of all beings, form of eternity, knowledge and bliss!", "O treasury of mercy!", "O most magnanimous!", "O You who have filled the hearts of all beings with love of various kinds!" (that is, You who fill the hearts of all beings with various love — *dasya*, *sakhya* and the rest), "O possessor of a wondrous lustre like that of molten gold!", "O Sri Hari in a golden body!", "O ocean of auspicious qualities!", "O embodied form of the *rasa* of separation (*vipralambha*)!", "O lover of Your own names!" — may I pass this life chanting such names: this alone I pray for.

### Verse 68

कदा शौरे गौरे वपुषि परमप्रेमरसदे
सदेकप्राणे निष्कपटकृतभावोऽस्मि भविता ।
कदा वा तस्यालौकिकसदनुमानेन मम हृ-
द्यकस्मात् श्रीराधापदनखमणिज्योतिरुदगात् ॥ ६८ ॥

*kadā śaure gaure vapuṣi paramapremarasade*
*sadekaprāṇe niṣkapaṭakṛtabhāvo'smi bhavitā |*
*kadā vā tasyālaukikasadanumānena mama hṛ-*
*dyakasmāt śrīrādhāpadanakhamaṇijyotirudagāt || 68 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* [he — O]; shaure — Shauri (Krishna, descendant of Shura); [tava — in Your]; parama-prema-rasade — bestowing the highest *rasa* of *prema*, that is, bestowing the elevated radiant (*unnata-ujjvala*) *rasa* of *prema*; sat-eka-prane — the very life of the *rasika* devotees; gaure vapushi — in the Gaura-body; kada — when; nishkapata-krita-bhavah — guileless feeling; bhavita asmi — will arise; kada va — or when; tasya — of that guileless feeling; alaukika-sat-anumanena — by an unearthly, that is, inaccessible to the material senses, true inference, that is, the inference: "where the radiance of the jewel-like toenails of Sri Radha's feet has risen, there is the feeling for Gaurahari"; mama hridi — in my heart; akasmat — suddenly; shri-radha-pada-nakha-mani-jyotih — the radiance of the jewels, the toenails, on the lotus feet of Sri Radha; udagat — will rise (with the word "*kada*" the past tense is used in the sense of the future).

**68.** O Shauri! When will I have a guileless feeling for Your body of Gaura, which gives the *rasa* of supreme love, the one life of the saints? Or when, by an unearthly true inference from that feeling, will the radiance of the jewel-like toenails of Sri Radha suddenly rise in my heart?

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Without bhakti for Gaura, rati for Radha is impossible*

>> O Krishna, ornament of the Shura dynasty! When will guileless *rati* arise in me for Your Gaura-body, which bestows the elevated, radiant *rasa* of love and is the very life of the *rasika* devotees? And when, by the transcendental (*adhokshaja*) inference that proceeds from this guileless *rati*, will the radiance of the jewel-like toenails of Sri Radhika suddenly rise in my heart?

### Verse 69

उद्दामदामनकदामगणाभिराम-
मारामराममविरामगृहीतनाम ।
कारुण्यधाम कनकोज्ज्वलगौरधाम
चैतन्यनाम परमं कलयाम धाम ॥ ६९ ॥

*uddāmadāmanakadāmagaṇābhirāma-*
*mārāmarāmamavirāmagṛhītanāma |*
*kāruṇyadhāma kanakojjvalagauradhāma*
*caitanyanāma paramaṁ kalayāma dhāma || 69 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* uddama-damanaka-damam — Him who wears a garland of full-blown *damanaka* flowers; gana-abhiramam — *gana* — people, *abhitah* — wholly, *ramayati* — gladdens; that is, Him who wholly gladdens multitudes of people; arama-ramam — Him who, staying in a secluded place, experiences bliss within Himself; avirama-grihita-nama — Him who incessantly chants the names "Krishna" and the rest; karunya-dhama — the abode of mercy; kanaka-ujjvala-gaura-dhama — golden, radiant like gold; caitanya-nama paramam dhama — the supreme abode named Chaitanya, that is, the highest shelter; [vayam — we]; kalayamah — contemplate (meditate on).

**69.** Charming with garlands of luxuriant *damanaka* flowers, delighting all people, sporting in secluded gardens, ceaselessly taking the Name, the abode of compassion, the abode of Gaura's golden radiance — the supreme abode named Chaitanya we contemplate.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Meditation on Sri Chaitanya*

>> Adorned with garlands of full-blown *damanaka* flowers, giving complete bliss to multitudes of people, the abode of mercy, with a golden lustre bright as gold — the supreme abode named "Chaitanya," that is, the best shelter, we contemplate.

### Verse 70

सदा रङ्गे नीलाचलशिखरशृङ्गे विलसतो
हरेरेव भ्राजन्मुखकमलभृङ्गेक्षणयुगम् ।
समुत्तुङ्गप्रेमोन्मदरसतरङ्गं मृगदृशा-
मनङ्गं गौराङ्गं स्मरतु गतसङ्गं मम मनः ॥ ७० ॥

*sadā raṅge nīlācalaśikharaśṛṅge vilasato*
*harereva bhrājanmukhakamalabhṛṅgekṣaṇayugam |*
*samuttuṅgapremonmadarasataraṅgaṁ mṛgadṛśā-*
*manaṅgaṁ gaurāṅgaṁ smaratu gatasaṅgaṁ mama manaḥ || 70 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* mama manah — my mind; range nilacala-shikhara-shringe — on the festive summit of Nilachala; sada — always; vilasatah hareh eva — of Hari alone (Jagannathadeva), who sports there; bhrajat-mukha-kamala-bhringa-ikshana-yugam — *bhrajat* — on the radiant, *mukha-kamalam* — lotus of the face, *tasmin* — on it, *bhringa-rupam* — in the form of bees, *ikshana-yugam* — the pair of eyes, *yasya* — whose; that is, Him whose eyes are bees on the radiant lotus of the face; samuttunga-prema-unmada-rasa-tarangam — Him in whom flow the waves of the *rasas* — joy, pride and the rest — born of the most exalted great *prema* in the form of *mahabhava*; mriga-drisham anangam — to doe-eyed women Kandarpa himself; gata-sangam — extremely detached (that is, utterly disinclined to the enjoyment of *sambhoga-rasa* — looking at women and the like — enacting the pastime of an acharya); gaurangam — Gauranga; smaratu — may it remember.

**70.** May my mind remember Gauranga, detached from all: His eyes are bees on the shining lotus face of Hari, who ever sports on the festive summit of Niladri;[^03-2] in Him rise high waves of the intoxicating *rasa* of love; to the doe-eyed He is Ananga (the bodiless Kamadeva) himself.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Meditation on Gaurahari, immersed in mahabhava, dwelling at Nilachala*

>> May my mind remember Sri Gaurasundara — Him whose eyes, like bees, rest on the radiant lotus face of Jagannathadeva, who is ever engaged in pastimes on the festive summit of Nilachala; in whom, in the highest love, which has the nature of *mahabhava*, flow the waves of *rasa* — joy, pride and the other [feelings]; who in beauty is Kandarpa himself to the doe-eyed young women; and who at the same time is supremely detached (that is, enacting the role of an acharya, He is extremely detached from the *rasa* of enjoyment — from looking at women and the like).

### Verse 71

अलङ्कारः पङ्केरुहनयननिःस्यन्दिपयसां
पृषद्भिः सन्मुक्ताफलसुललितैर्यस्य वपुषि ।
उदञ्चद्रोमाञ्चैरपि च परमा यस्य सुषमा
तमालम्बे गौरं हरिमरुणरोचिष्णुवसनम् ॥ ७१ ॥

*alaṅkāraḥ paṅkeruhanayananiḥsyandipayasāṁ*
*pṛṣadbhiḥ sanmuktāphalasulalitairyasya vapuṣi |*
*udañcadromāñcairapi ca paramā yasya suṣamā*
*tamālambe gauraṁ harimaruṇarociṣṇuvasanam || 71 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* aruna-rocishnu-vasanam — Him whose garment is the colour of the red dawn; pankeruha-nayana-nihsyandi-payasam sat-mukta-phala-sulalitaih prishadbhih — by drops of tears flowing from His lotus-like eyes, most charming, like pearls; yasya vapushi alankarah — whose body is adorned; udancat-romancaih api ca — and also by the hairs standing on end, that is, by the blazing (*uddipta*) *sattvika-bhavas* — the thrill born of *prema* and the rest — by which the beauty of His body is produced; tam — that; gauram — golden; harim — Hari; alambe — I take as my shelter.

**71.** His body is adorned with drops of tears flowing from His lotus eyes, lovely as the finest pearls; His supreme beauty is also the thrill of His hair standing on end. I take shelter of Gaura — Hari wearing a garment the colour of the red dawn.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Meditation on Gaurahari with blazing sattvika-bhavas*

>> He whose garment is the colour of the dawn; whose holy body is adorned with lovely drops of tears like pearls, flowing from His lotus eyes; whose body is beautified also by the blazing (*uddipta*) *sattvika-bhavas* — the thrill of the hair born of *mahabhava* and the rest — in that Sri Hari, Gauranga, I take shelter.

### Verse 72

कन्दर्पादपि सुन्दरः सुरसरित्पूरादहो पावनः
शीतांशोरपि शीतलः सुमधुरो माध्वीकसारादपि ।
दाता कल्पमहीरुहादपि महास्निग्धो जनन्या अपि
प्रेम्णा गौरहरिः कदा नु हृदि मे ध्यातः पदं धास्यति ॥ ७२ ॥

*kandarpādapi sundaraḥ surasaritpūrādaho pāvanaḥ*
*śītāṁśorapi śītalaḥ sumadhuro mādhvīkasārādapi |*
*dātā kalpamahīruhādapi mahāsnigdho jananyā api*
*premṇā gaurahariḥ kadā nu hṛdi me dhyātaḥ padaṁ dhāsyati || 72 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* kandarpat api sundarah — more beautiful even than Kandarpa ([…] — he who is able to scorn even him, that is, to crush even Brahma's pride consisting in beauty and power — Kandarpa; more beautiful even than him; the worldly Kandarpa, or Madana, produces agitation and bewilderment, while Gaurahari's beauty, destroying agitation and bewilderment, gives one to taste the highest happiness of the bliss of *prema*); aho — oh! (expresses wonder); sura-sarit-purat pavanah — purifying more than the stream of the heavenly river ("*sura-sarit*" — the Ganga, the best of all purifiers; "*pura*" — her stream; purifying more even than it: the stream of the Ganga destroys sins but cannot purify a sinful heart, while Sri Gaurasundara, having purified the heart, causes a stream of the highest *prema* to rise in it — therefore His purity and wonderfulness surpass those of the Ganga's stream); shita-amshoh api shitalah — cooler even than the moon (he whose rays are cool is the moon; cooler even than it: the moon can remove external darkness, heat and the like, while Sri Gaurasundara completely destroys the darkness of the heart — the gloom of ignorance accumulated over millions of births — and the heat of suffering, and floods it with the […] rays of the bliss of *prema*); madhvika-sarat api sumadhurah — sweeter even than the finest honey (*madhvika-sara*, that is, *amrita*; sweeter even than it: the sweetness of *amrita* only the gods can taste, and as the fruit of tasting it worldly pride, envy and the like arise in them, while Gaura's sweetness is inaccessible even to the gods, and as the result of tasting it worldly pride, envy and the like completely vanish and the bliss of *prema* appears); kalpa-mahiruhat api data — more generous even than the wish-fulfilling tree (the wish-fulfilling tree gives fruit to one who asks it, while Sri Gaurasundara is so generous that He Himself, becoming the petitioner, bestows the wealth of *prema* and the like even on the unworthy and those who do not ask);
