jananyah api maha-snigdhah — more tender even than a mother (a mother's love brings poisonous fruit in the future, for it is directed to the perishable body and the like, while Sri Gaurasundara's love is directed to the soul of the living being, and therefore it obtains the right to […] the highest bliss of *prema*; besides, a mother loves only her own child, while Sri Gaurasundara sprinkles all living beings with the *rasa* of love); gaura-harih — Gaurahari; dhyatah — becoming the object of meditation; kada nu — when indeed; me hridi — in my heart; premna — with love; padam dhasyati — will step.

**72.** More beautiful than Kandarpa, — oh! — purer than the floods of the river of the gods, cooler than the moon, sweeter than the essence of the finest honey, more generous than the wish-fulfilling tree, more tender in His love than a mother — when will Gaurahari, on whom I meditate, set His foot in my heart with love?

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Gaurahari's beauty, purifying power, coolness and bestowal of sweetness*

>> More beautiful than Kandarpa, purer than the stream of the Ganga, the saviour of the fallen, cooler than the moon, sweeter than nectar, more generous than the wish-fulfilling tree, more tender in love than a mother — when will Gaurahari, becoming the object of my meditation, step lovingly into my heart?

### Verse 73

पुञ्जं पुञ्जं मधुरमधुरप्रेममाध्वीरसानां
दत्त्वा दत्त्वा स्वयमुरुदयो मोदयन् विश्वमेतत् ।
एको देवः कटितटमिलन्मञ्जुमञ्जिष्ठवासा
भासा निर्भर्त्सितनवतडित्कोटिरेव प्रियो मे ॥ ७३ ॥

*puñjaṁ puñjaṁ madhuramadhurapremamādhvīrasānāṁ*
*dattvā dattvā svayamurudayo modayan viśvametat |*
*eko devaḥ kaṭitaṭamilanmañjumañjiṣṭhavāsā*
*bhāsā nirbhartsitanavataḍitkoṭireva priyo me || 73 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* madhura-madhura-prema-madhvi-rasanam — of the *rasas* of the nectar of *prema*, sweeter than sweet; punjam punjam — in heaps; dattva dattva — giving again and again; etat vishvam — this world; modayan — gladdening; kati-tata-milat-manju-manjishtha-vasah — *manju* — very charming, *manjishtha* — a particular red creeper; He whose garment is dyed with it, that is, who wears on His hips a charming ochre-coloured (*gairika*) garment; bhasa-nirbhartsita-nava-tadit-koti — He who by His radiance has put to shame millions of lightnings, that is, possessing a bodily radiance that puts to shame millions of lightnings; svayam uru-dayah — Himself supremely merciful; ekah devah eva — *eka* — non-dual, or having neither equal nor superior; *deva* — the Purusha devoted to pastimes; me priyah [astu] — may He be dear to me.

**73.** Giving again and again heaps upon heaps of the sweetest intoxicating honey of love, Himself boundlessly merciful, delighting this world — the one Lord, on whose hips is a lovely madder-coloured cloth, whose lustre puts to shame millions of fresh lightning flashes — He alone is dear to me.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Love for Gaurahari in saffron cloth, the giver of love*

>> He who, again and again giving heaps of the nectar *rasa* of love, sweeter than the sweet, gladdens this world; who wears on His hips a lovely saffron (*gairika*) cloth; whose bodily radiance puts to shame millions of new lightnings — that boundlessly merciful, non-dual Purusha Himself, engaged in spiritual pastimes — may He alone be dear to me.

### Verse 74

कान्त्या निन्दितकोटिकोटिमदनः श्रीमन्मुखेन्दुच्छटा-
विच्छायीकृतकोटिकोटिशरदुन्मीलत्तुषारच्छविः ।
औदार्येण च कोटिकोटिगुणितं कल्पद्रुमं ह्यल्पयन्
गौरो मे हृदि कोटिकोटिजनुषां भाग्यैः पदं धास्यति ॥ ७४ ॥

*kāntyā ninditakoṭikoṭimadanaḥ śrīmanmukhenducchaṭā-*
*vicchāyīkṛtakoṭikoṭiśaradunmīlattuṣāracchaviḥ |*
*audāryeṇa ca koṭikoṭiguṇitaṁ kalpadrumaṁ hyalpayan*
*gauro me hṛdi koṭikoṭijanuṣāṁ bhāgyaiḥ padaṁ dhāsyati || 74 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* kantya — by the charming beauty of His form; nindita-koti-koti-madanah — He who puts to shame millions upon millions of Kandarpas; shrimat-mukha-indu-chata-vicchayikrita-koti-koti-sharad-unmilat-tushara-cchavih — *shrimat-mukha-indu* — the moon of the face, full of supreme beauty; *chata* — radiance; *tena* — by it; *vicchayikrita* — eclipsed; *koti-koti sharad-unmilat-tushara-cchavi* — millions upon millions of rising autumn moons, white as snow; that is, He by the radiance of whose most beautiful moon-face millions upon millions of autumn moons are eclipsed; audaryena ca koti-koti-gunitam kalpa-drumam hi alpayan — He by whose great generosity even millions of wish-fulfilling trees are belittled; gaurah — Gaurasundara; koti-koti-janusham — of countless births; bhagyaih — by the fruits of good deeds (*sukriti*); me hridi — in my heart; padam dhasyati — will step; [kim — will He?].

**74.** His beauty puts to shame millions upon millions of Madanas; the radiance of His beautiful moon-face eclipses the light of millions upon millions of rising autumn moons; by His generosity He belittles the wish-fulfilling tree multiplied millions upon millions of times — through the good fortune of millions upon millions of births Gaura will set His foot in my heart.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Gaurahari's incomparable form and qualities*

>> He whose captivating beauty and charm put to shame millions upon millions of Kandarpas, by the splendour of whose most beautiful moon-face the radiance of millions upon millions of rising autumn moons grows dim, whose great generosity belittles millions upon millions of wish-fulfilling trees — will that Gaurasundara step into my heart by virtue of the heaps of pious merits (*sukriti*) of millions upon millions of births?

### Verse 75

क्षणं क्षीणः पीनः क्षणमहह साश्रुः क्षणमथ
क्षणं स्मेरः शीतः क्षणमनलतप्तः क्षणमपि ।
क्षणं धावन् स्तब्धः क्षणमधिकजल्पन् क्षणमहो
क्षणं मूको गौरः स्फुरतु मम देहो भगवतः ॥ ७५ ॥

*kṣaṇaṁ kṣīṇaḥ pīnaḥ kṣaṇamahaha sāśruḥ kṣaṇamatha*
*kṣaṇaṁ smeraḥ śītaḥ kṣaṇamanalataptaḥ kṣaṇamapi |*
*kṣaṇaṁ dhāvan stabdhaḥ kṣaṇamadhikajalpan kṣaṇamaho*
*kṣaṇaṁ mūko gauraḥ sphuratu mama deho bhagavataḥ || 75 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* kshanam kshinah — for a moment emaciated (the state in which Srimati Radhika was in prolonged *vipralambha*, that is, in the separation of *mathura-viraha* — Gaurahari, imbued with that mood, for a moment emaciated in separation from Krishna); kshanam [ca] pinah — and for a moment full (again in *vipralambha*, […], for a moment full); ahaha — alas!; kshanam sashruh — for a moment full of tears of bliss; atha — then; kshanam shitah — for a moment cooled (scorched by the fire of separation from Krishna, again at […] for a moment cooled); kshanam smerah — for a moment gently smiling (from jokes and laughter with […] Krishna); [kshanam] anala-taptah — scorched by fire (in *prema-vaicittya*, that is, even while near Krishna, again for a moment scorched by the fire of future separation from Him); kshanam dhavan — for a moment running (absorbed in the thought "Krishna is going away," for a moment running after Him); kshanam stabdhah — for a moment stunned (again coming to another place and […], for a moment stunned; *stambha* is the first of the eight *sattvika-bhavas*); aho kshanam adhika-jalpan — oh! for a moment talking much (for a moment uttering *prajalpa*: "O libertine, O destroyer of the vow of chastity, do not touch me!"); kshanam [ca] mukah — and for a moment mute (for some time keeping silent from the weight of offended pride, *mana*); [etadrishah — such]; bhagavatah dehah — the body of Bhagavan; gaurah — Gaurasundara; mama [hridaye — in the heart] sphuratu — may He appear in my heart.

**75.** Now wasted, now full, — ah! — now in tears, now smiling, now cold, now burning as if with fire, now running, now stunned, now speaking without end, — oh! — now silent: may Gaura, the very body of the Lord, appear in my heart.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Sri Gaurahari, in whom there is a mingling of feelings (bhava-shabalya) — vibhavas, anubhavas and the rest*

>> Now emaciated, now full, now with eyes full of tears of the bliss of love, now with a smile on His face, now cool, now burning, now running, now stunned, now talkative, now silent — may such a Gaura-body of Krishna appear in my heart.

### Verse 76

पात्रापात्रविचारणां न कुरुते न स्वं परं वीक्षते
देयादेयविमर्शको नहि न वा कालप्रतीक्षः प्रभुः ।
सद्यो यः श्रवणेक्षणप्रणमनध्यानादिना दुर्लभं
दत्ते भक्तिरसं स एव भगवान् गौरः परं मे गतिः ॥ ७६ ॥

*pātrāpātravicāraṇāṁ na kurute na svaṁ paraṁ vīkṣate*
*deyādeyavimarśako nahi na vā kālapratīkṣaḥ prabhuḥ |*
*sadyo yaḥ śravaṇekṣaṇapraṇamanadhyānādinā durlabhaṁ*
*datte bhaktirasaṁ sa eva bhagavān gauraḥ paraṁ me gatiḥ || 76 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* yah prabhuh — that Prabhu (*prabhu* — the independent Lord) who; patra-apatra-vicaranam — deliberation on whether a person is worthy or unworthy; na kurute — does not make; na svam param vikshate — does not look to whether one is His own or another's; na hi deya-adeya-vimarshakah — does not reflect or deliberate on what may be given and what may not; na va kala-pratikshah — nor waits for a suitable or unsuitable time; shravana-ikshana-pranamana-dhyana-adina — by hearing, seeing, bowing, meditation and the like; durlabham bhakti-rasam — the *rasa* of *bhakti*, hard to attain; sadyah datte — at once bestows; sah bhagavan gaurah eva — that very Bhagavan Gaurasundara; me param gatih — is my only refuge.

**76.** The Lord does not consider whether a person is worthy or unworthy, does not look whether he is His own or another's, does not weigh what may be given and what may not, does not wait for a suitable time. He who at once grants the rare *rasa* of *bhakti* through hearing about Him, seeing Him, bowing down, meditation and the rest — that Lord Gaura is my supreme refuge.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Sri Gaurahari, who does not consider place, time or the worthiness of the recipient, and impartially bestows love*

>> That Lord (the independent Ishvara) who does not consider whether a person is worthy or unworthy, does not look to whether he is His own or another's, does not deliberate on what may be given and what may not, and does not wait for a suitable time — who at once bestows the *rasa* of *bhakti*, hard to attain by hearing, seeing, bowing, meditation and the like — that Bhagavan Gaurasundara is my only refuge.

### Verse 77

पापीयानपि हीनजातिरपि दुःशीलोऽपि दुष्कर्मणां
सीमापि श्वपचाधमोऽपि सततं दुर्वासनाढ्योऽपि च ।
दुर्देशप्रभवोऽपि तत्र विहितावासोऽपि दुःसङ्गतो
नष्टोऽप्युद्धृत एव येन कृपया तं गौरमेवाश्रये ॥ ७७ ॥

*pāpīyānapi hīnajātirapi duḥśīlo'pi duṣkarmaṇāṁ*
*sīmāpi śvapacādhamo'pi satataṁ durvāsanāḍhyo'pi ca |*
*durdeśaprabhavo'pi tatra vihitāvāso'pi duḥsaṅgato*
*naṣṭo'pyuddhṛta eva yena kṛpayā taṁ gauramevāśraye || 77 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* papiyan api — even the greatest sinner (like Gopala Chapala and others, struck with leprosy); hina-jatih api — even the low-born (like tailors, *yavanas* and other low castes); duhshilah api — even a man of evil character; dushkarmanam sima api — even one who has reached the limit of extremely evil deeds (the abduction of others' wives, drunkenness and the like), like Jagai, Madhai and their like; shvapaca-adhamah api — even one worse than a *candala*; satatam durvasana-adhyah api — even one forever given to evil desires; durdesha-prabhavah api ca — and even one born in an evil land (where there is no Ganga or where the Pandavas did not go — in such lands devoid of the Ganga and the Pandavas); tatra vihita-avasah api — even one living in those places as a consequence of evil deeds; duhsangatah nashtah api — even one ruined by evil association; yena kripaya eva uddhritah — He by whose mercy alone was delivered; tam gauram eva — that Gaurasundara alone; ashraye — I take as my shelter.

**77.** Be a man the most sinful, low-born, vicious, the very limit of evil deeds, lower than a dog-eater, ever full of evil tendencies, born in a bad land and dwelling there, ruined by bad company — even he is delivered by the mercy of Gaura. I take refuge in that Gaura alone.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *An example for the preceding words*

>> He by whose mercy were delivered even the greatest sinners such as Gopala Chapala, the low-born such as the *yavana* tailor and others, people of evil character who had reached the limit of evil deeds — Jagai, Madhai and their like, given to sin and forbidden acts — beings lower than the dog-eating *candala*, always given to evil tendencies, born in an evil land where there is no Ganga and where the Pandavas never set foot, and living in an evil land, and also those ruined by evil association — in that Gaurasundara I take my only refuge.

### Verse 78

कलिन्दतनयातटे स्फुरदमन्दवृन्दावनं
विहाय लवणाम्बुधेः पुलिनपुष्पवाटीं गतः ।
धृतारुणपटः परीहृतसुपीतवासा हरि-
स्तिरोहितनिजच्छविः प्रकटगौरिमा मे गतिः ॥ ७८ ॥

*kalindatanayātaṭe sphuradamandavṛndāvanaṁ*
*vihāya lavaṇāmbudheḥ pulinapuṣpavāṭīṁ gataḥ |*
*dhṛtāruṇapaṭaḥ parīhṛtasupītavāsā hari-*
*stirohitanijacchaviḥ prakaṭagaurimā me gatiḥ || 78 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* kalinda-tanaya-tate — Kalinda is the mountain Kalinda, its *tanaya* — daughter, the Yamuna, *tate* — on the bank; or Kalinda means the sun, his daughter is the Yamuna, on her bank; that is, on the bank of the Yamuna; sphurat-amanda-vrindavanam — the radiant, most charming Vrindavana; vihaya — leaving; lavana-ambudheh — of the salt ocean; pulina-pushpa-vatim — to the flower garden on the shore; gatah — He who went; parihrita-supita-vasah — He who, leaving His most beautiful yellow garment; dhrita-aruna-patah — put on a garment the colour of the dawn; tirohita-nija-cchavih — He who, concealing His own dark colour, like an *indranila* sapphire (in the *anuvada*: covering it with the feeling and lustre of His most beloved Srimati Radhika); prakata-gaurima — manifested a golden lustre; harih — that Sri Hari; me gatih — is my shelter.

**78.** Leaving radiant Vrindavana on the bank of the daughter of Kalinda (the Yamuna), He went to the flowering gardens on the shore of the salt ocean; He put on a garment the colour of dawn, casting aside His beautiful yellow one; concealing His own complexion, He manifested a golden hue. That Hari is my refuge.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Bhagavan Krishna Himself, clothed in the feeling and lustre of Radha, is Gaura*

>> He who left the radiant, beautiful Vrindavana on the bank of the daughter of Kalinda and went to the flowering gardens on the shore of the salt ocean; who left His most beautiful yellow garment and put on a garment the colour of the dawn; who concealed His own colour, that is, His dark colour (with the feeling and lustre of His most beloved Srimati Radhika), and manifested a golden lustre — that Sri Hari is my only refuge.

## Instruction to the world (loka-shiksha)

### Verse 79

अरे मूढा गूढां विचिनुत हरेर्भक्तिपदवीं
दवीयस्या दृष्ट्याप्यपरिचितपूर्वां मुनिवरैः ।
न विश्रम्भश्चित्ते यदि यदि च दौर्लभ्यमिव तत्
परित्यज्याशेषं व्रजत शरणं गौरचरणम् ॥ ७९ ॥

*are mūḍhā gūḍhāṁ vicinuta harerbhaktipadavīṁ*
*davīyasyā dṛṣṭyāpyaparicitapūrvāṁ munivaraiḥ |*
*na viśrambhaścitte yadi yadi ca daurlabhyamiva tat*
*parityajyāśeṣaṁ vrajata śaraṇaṁ gauracaraṇam || 79 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* are mudhah — ho, fools; muni-varaih daviyasya drishtya api aparicita-purvam — that which the sages formerly could not know even with their far-seeing vision; gudham — secret; hareh bhakti-padavim — the path of *bhakti* to Sri Hari; vicinuta — seek; yadi citte na vishrambhah — if there is no faith in the heart (because the path of *bhakti* is too secret); yadi ca daurlabhyam iva — and if it seems hard to reach; tat [sarvam] ashesham parityajya — giving up all that entirely; gaura-caranam sharanam vrajata — take shelter of Gaurahari's feet.

**79.** Hey, fools! Seek out the secret path of devotion to Hari, unknown before to the best of sages even with their far-seeing vision. And if there is no faith in your heart, or if it seems unattainable — give up everything and take refuge at Gaura's feet.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *To obtain the great love, hard to reach even for the lords of the gods, one should take shelter of Gaurahari's feet*

>> Ho, fools! Seek that secret path of devotion to Hari which formerly even the sages could not know with their far-seeing vision (foresight). And if there is no faith in your heart, if it seems hard to reach — give up all that (your endeavours) entirely and take shelter at Gaurahari's feet.

### Verse 80

दधन्मूर्धन्यूर्ध्वं मुकुलितकराम्भोजयुगलं
गलन्नेत्राम्भोभिः स्नपितमृदुगण्डस्थलयुगम् ।
दुकूलेनावीतं नवकमलकिञ्जल्करुचिना
परं ज्योतिर्गौरं कनकरुचिचौरं प्रणमत ॥ ८० ॥

*dadhanmūrdhanyūrdhvaṁ mukulitakarāmbhojayugalaṁ*
*galannetrāmbhobhiḥ snapitamṛdugaṇḍasthalayugam |*
*dukūlenāvītaṁ navakamalakiñjalkarucinā*
*paraṁ jyotirgauraṁ kanakarucicauraṁ praṇamata || 80 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* mukulita-kara-ambhoja-yugalam — the pair of lotus palms joined (in *anjali*); murdhni — on the head; urdhvam — raised upwards; dadhat — holding; galat-netra-ambhobhih — with the water flowing from the eyes; snapita-mridu-ganda-sthala-yugam — Him whose tender cheeks are bathed; nava-kamala-kinjalka-rucina — with a lustre like that of the filaments of a fresh lotus; dukulena — with a cloth; avitam — adorned; kanaka-ruci-cauram — *kanaka* — gold, *ruci* — its lustre, *caura* — the thief; that is, Him who has taken on the golden lustre; param jyotih — the supreme light; gauram — to Gaurasundara; pranamata — bow down (offer *pranama*).

**80.** He holds His folded lotus hands above His head like a bud; His tender cheeks are bathed in streaming tears; He is clothed in cloth the colour of the filaments of a fresh lotus. Bow to that supreme light — Gaura, the thief of golden lustre!

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Obeisance to Gaurahari, bathed in tears at the sight of Jagannatha*

>> He who holds His joined lotus palms high above His head, who bathes His tender cheeks with flowing tears, who is adorned with a garment having the lustre of the filaments of a fresh lotus — bow down to that Gaurasundara, clothed in the lustre of molten gold, the supreme light.

### Verse 81

उच्चैः कीर्तय नाम गोकुलपतेरुद्दामनामावलीं
यद्वा भावय तस्य दिव्यमधुरं रूपं जगन्मङ्गलम् ।
हन्त प्रेममहारसोज्ज्वलपदे नाशापि ते सम्भवेत्
श्रीचैतन्यमहाप्रभोर्यदि कृपादृष्टिः पतेन्न त्वयि ॥ ८१ ॥

*uccaiḥ kīrtaya nāma gokulapateruddāmanāmāvalīṁ*
*yadvā bhāvaya tasya divyamadhuraṁ rūpaṁ jaganmaṅgalam |*
*hanta premamahārasojjvalapade nāśāpi te sambhavet*
*śrīcaitanyamahāprabhoryadi kṛpādṛṣṭiḥ patenna tvayi || 81 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* [he bhratah — O brother]; uccaih — loudly; gokula-pateh — of the son of the king of Vraja; uddama-nama-avalim — the series of names possessing the highest power; nama — (an ornament of speech, in the sense of assent or certainty); kirtaya — chant; yat va — or; tasya — His; divya-madhuram — radiant, full of sweetness; jagat-mangalam — bringing good to the world; rupam bhavaya — contemplate the form; yadi shri-caitanya-mahaprabhoh — if Sri Chaitanya Mahaprabhu's; kripa-drishtih — merciful glance; tvayi — on you; na patet — does not fall; hanta — alas; [tada — then]; prema-maha-rasa-ujjvala-pade — in the object of *ujjvala* (the radiant *rasa*), in which, because of *prema*, there is the "*mahat*," the highest *rasa*; *ujjvala* is *shringara*, "*pada*" — that is, the object; *shringara-rasa* is called *ujjvala-rasa*; te — of you; asha api na sambhavet — not even hope is possible.

**81.** Loudly chant the mighty series of the names of the Lord of Gokula, or contemplate His divine, sweet form, which blesses the world — but alas, you will not even have hope of the state of the radiant great *rasa* of love if the merciful glance of Sri Chaitanya Mahaprabhu does not fall upon you.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Without Mahaprabhu's mercy it is impossible to obtain the elevated, radiant rasa*

>> O brother! Loudly chant the mighty series of names of the lord of Gokula or meditate on His divinely sweet form, which blesses the world — but if Sri Chaitanya Mahaprabhu's merciful glance has not fallen on you, you cannot have even a hope of that supreme, elevated and radiant *rasa* of love.

### Verse 82

अये न कुरु साहसं तव हसन्ति सर्वोद्यमं
जनाः परित उन्मदा हरिरसामृतास्वादिनः ।
इदं तु निभृतं शृणु प्रणयवस्तु प्रस्तूयते
यदेव निगमेषु तत्पतिरयं हि गौरः परम् ॥ ८२ ॥

*aye na kuru sāhasaṁ tava hasanti sarvodyamaṁ*
*janāḥ parita unmadā harirasāmṛtāsvādinaḥ |*
*idaṁ tu nibhṛtaṁ śṛṇu praṇayavastu prastūyate*
*yadeva nigameṣu tatpatirayaṁ hi gauraḥ param || 82 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* aye — ho! [bhratah — brother]; [shri-caitanya-pada-ashrayam vina anya-sadhaneshu — in other *sadhanas* apart from the shelter of Sri Chaitanya's feet]; sahasam na kuru — do not act rashly, that is, do not take them up hastily; [yatah — since]; hari-rasa-amrita-asvadinah — those expert in tasting the nectar of the *bhakti-rasa* of Hari, who steals the heart and its wealth, that is, of Sri Krishna; [atah eva — therefore]; paritah unmadah — those in whom intoxication, that is, the bliss of *prema*, has wholly arisen — these devotees of Gaura; tava — at your; sarva-udyamam — every kind of endeavour; hasanti — laugh; idam tu nibhritam shrinu — but hear this secret word; nigameshu — in the Vedas and other *shastras*; yat eva pranaya-vastu prastuyate — that which is set forth as the object (substance) of love; tat-patih — its master, that is, its object (*vishaya*); ayam hi — certainly this; [param — only]; gaurah — Gaura.

**82.** Hey, do not rashly take up anything else! People all around, intoxicated with the taste of the nectar of Hari-rasa, laugh at all your efforts. Hear, then, this secret: that which in the Vedas is presented as the object of love — its Lord is this Gaura, He alone.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Instruction to take shelter of the feet of Gaura, the source of all perfections*

>> Ho, do not rashly throw yourself into any *sadhana* other than the shelter of Sri Chaitanya's feet: Gaura's people, wholly intoxicated by drinking the nectar *rasa* of love for Krishna, laugh at all your endeavours. And now hear one most secret word: that which in all the Vedas and other *shastras* is set forth as the object of love — know for certain: its only object (*vishaya*) is this Sri Gauranga.

### Verse 83

ज्ञानादिवर्त्मविरुचिं व्रजनाथभक्ति-
रीतिं न वेद्मि न च सद्गुरवो मिलन्ति ।
हा हन्त हन्त मम कः शरणं विमूढ
गौरो हरिस्तव न कर्णपथं गतोऽस्ति ॥ ८३ ॥

*jñānādivartmaviruciṁ vrajanāthabhakti-*
*rītiṁ na vedmi na ca sadguravo milanti |*
*hā hanta hanta mama kaḥ śaraṇaṁ vimūḍha*
*gauro haristava na karṇapathaṁ gato'sti || 83 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* jnana-adi-vartma-virucim — producing distaste for the paths of knowledge (*jnana* — the knowledge of Brahman without distinctions; "and the rest" — the regular and occasional duties, *adhyatma-yoga*, dry renunciation and the like); vrajanatha-bhakti-ritim — the method of worshipping Sri Krishna; na vedmi — I do not know; na ca mama sat-guravah milanti — and true gurus do not come my way; mama kah sharanam [bhavita — will be] — what is my way (who is my shelter)?; ha hanta hanta — alas, alas!; [are — ho]; vimudha — O, you are a thorough fool!; gaurah harih — Gaurahari; tava karna-patham na gatah asti — has He not entered the path of your ears? — that is, quickly bring the worship of Gaurahari into the path of your ears; delay no longer.

**83.** "I do not know the way of devotion to the Lord of Vraja, which turns one away from the path of knowledge and the rest; I do not meet true gurus; alas, alas, who is my refuge?" — O fool! has Gaura-Hari never reached your ears?

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Sri Gaurahari is worthy of worship even for one who lacks pure bhakti and […]*

>> "The path of worship of Sri Krishna, which produces distaste for the path of knowledge of the undifferentiated Brahman and the like — that I do not know! And I do not happen to meet true gurus! (What am I to do now?) In whom am I to seek shelter?" — Alas, alas, what a fool you are! (You still cannot decide who your shelter is?) Has not even the name of Gaurahari entered your ears?

### Verse 84

वृथावेशं कर्मस्वपनयत वार्तामपि मनाक्
न कर्णाभ्यर्णेऽपि क्वचन नयताध्यात्मसरणेः ।
न मोहं देहादौ भजत परमाश्चर्यमधुरः
पुमर्थानां मौलिर्मिलति भवतां गौरकृपया ॥ ८४ ॥

*vṛthāveśaṁ karmasvapanayata vārtāmapi manāk*
*na karṇābhyarṇe'pi kvacana nayatādhyātmasaraṇeḥ |*
*na mohaṁ dehādau bhajata paramāścaryamadhuraḥ*
*pumarthānāṁ maulirmilati bhavatāṁ gaurakṛpayā || 84 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* karmasu — in the regular and occasional rites; vritha-avesham — vain absorption; apanayata — cast away; adhyatma-saraneh — *adhyatma* — the reasoning "*neti, neti*" of the adherents of the ascending path (*aroha-vadis*), *sarani* — its path; manak vartam api — even the slightest talk; kvacana — ever; karna-abhyarne api — even into the vicinity of the ears; na nayata — do not let come; deha-adau — in the body and the rest (the words "and the rest" mean home, family, country, society and all other things); moham na bhajata — do not fall into delusion; [tada — then]; parama-ashcarya-madhurah — possessing the highest, astonishing, special sweetness; puma-arthanam maulih — the crown of the goals of man, the fifth goal, *prema*; bhavatam — by you; gaura-kripaya — by Gaurasundara's mercy; milati — will be obtained (the present tense in the sense of the near future).

**84.** Put away your vain absorption in rituals; do not let even talk of the path of contemplation of the Self come anywhere near your ears; do not cherish the delusion of the body and the rest — and by Gaura's mercy the crown of the goals of man, supremely wondrous and sweet, will come to you.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Leaving […], one should pray only for Gaura's mercy, which fulfils all goals*

>> Cast far away your vain absorption in the regular, occasional and other rites (*karma*); never let even the slightest word about the path of ascending reasoning (*aroha*) come even to the threshold of your ears, and never fall into delusion about the body and everything connected with it — then by the mercy of Sri Gaurasundara you will obtain the crown of the goals of man: love for Krishna, full of supreme wondrous sweetness.

### Verse 85

अलं शास्त्राभ्यासैरलमहह तीर्थाटनिकया
सदा योषिद्व्याघ्र्यास्त्रसत वितथां थूत्कुरु दिवम् ।
तृणंमन्या धन्याः श्रयत किल सन्न्यासिकपटं
नटन्तं गौराङ्गं निजरसमदादम्बुधितटे ॥ ८५ ॥

*alaṁ śāstrābhyāsairalamahaha tīrthāṭanikayā*
*sadā yoṣidvyāghryāstrasata vitathāṁ thūtkuru divam |*
*tṛṇaṁmanyā dhanyāḥ śrayata kila sannyāsikapaṭaṁ*
*naṭantaṁ gaurāṅgaṁ nijarasamadādambudhitaṭe || 85 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* ahaha — alas!; sada yoshit-vyaghryah trasata — always beware of the tigress, woman; (if it is asked: "since, comparing woman to a tigress, association with women has been forbidden, how then, without sacrifices and the other rites performed together with one's wife, is heaven to be attained?" — anticipating this question, he says:) vitatham — false, that is, transient; divam — heaven; trinam-manyah — considering it as insignificant as a straw; thutkuru — spit at it; [anticipating the question: "very well, let it be so; then we shall study the *shastras* on yoga and perform yoga and the rest — for by this all goals can be attained," he says:] shastra-abhyasaih alam — the study of *shastras* is in vain; [for with those who practise yoga having studied them, perfection is a distant matter; even the fall of those established in yoga is spoken of; what is the sense of such a practice, whose success is so uncertain and whose very practice is extremely painful?]; [anticipating the question: "if so, then service of the holy places is surely more pleasant? serving the *tirthas*, we shall obtain knowledge and the like, and as a result become worthy of liberation," he says:] tirtha-atanikaya [ca] alam — and what is the use of pilgrimages?; [for, first, pilgrimage is hard labour; second, since the *karmis* and *jnanis* with worldly minds have offences against the holy abode, their going to the *tirthas* is only a vain endeavour for sense gratification; what then should be done — this he now concludes:]; dhanyah — O people worthy of obtaining the wealth of *prema*; [yuyam — you]; nija-rasa-madat ambudhi-tate — from the bliss born of the *rasa* of *prema* of His own form, Krishna, on the shore of the ocean at Nilachala; natantam — dancing; sannyasi-kapatam — enacting the pastime of sannyasa; kila — certainly; gaurangam shrayata — take shelter of Gauranga.

**85.** Enough of studying the shastras! Enough, alas, of wandering to holy places! Always fear the tigress, woman; regarding the false heavens as a straw, spit on them! O fortunate ones, take refuge in Gauranga, who has assumed the guise of a *sannyasi* and dances on the ocean shore, intoxicated with His own *rasa*.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *The insignificance of talking with women, the pursuit of heaven, […] and the like, and the supremacy of the shelter of Gaura's feet alone*

>> Always beware of association with women, those tigresses; considering heaven (transient) as insignificant as a straw, spit at it; what is the use of studying many *shastras*? — give that up too; and what is the gain of pilgrimages? — renounce them too. (Look here:) Sri Gaura, enacting the role of a sannyasi, dances on the shore of the blue ocean at Nilachala in the bliss of love of His own essence — Krishna! O fortunate devotees, (go, go,) take shelter at His feet.

### Verse 86

किं तावद्बत दुर्गमेषु विफलं योगादिमार्गेष्वहो
भक्तिं कृष्णपदाम्बुजे विदधतः सर्वार्थमालुण्ठत ।
आशा प्रेममहोत्सवे यदि शिवब्रह्माद्यलभ्येऽद्भुते
गौरे धामनि दुर्विगाहमहिमोदारे तदा रज्यताम् ॥ ८६ ॥

*kiṁ tāvadbata durgameṣu viphalaṁ yogādimārgeṣvaho*
*bhaktiṁ kṛṣṇapadāmbuje vidadhataḥ sarvārthamāluṇṭhata |*
*āśā premamahotsave yadi śivabrahmādyalabhye'dbhute*
*gaure dhāmani durvigāhamahimodāre tadā rajyatām || 86 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* bata — alas (expresses regret); aho — ho (an address) [aho bhratarah — O brothers]; durgameshu yoga-adi-margeshu — on the hard-to-travel paths of the eightfold yoga and the rest; kim tavat viphalam [anusandhanam kriyate — is search made] — what is the use of wandering fruitlessly?; krishna-pada-ambuje bhaktim vidadhatah — performing *bhakti* to Krishna's lotus feet; sarva-artham — *prema*, the crown of all goals, or that which among all things is the only goal (*prayojana*) — the wealth of *prema*; aluntata — seize by force, plunder completely; shiva-brahma-adi-alabhye — inaccessible even to Shiva, Brahma and the rest; adbhute — wondrous; prema-maha-utsave — in the great festival of *prema*; yadi asha [vartate] — if there is hope; tada — then; durvigaha-mahima-udare gaure dhamani — to Gaura, the abode, whose glory is unfathomable and who is generous, that is, great in generosity (*maha-vadanya*); rajyatam — be filled with attachment (*anuraga*).

**86.** Why, alas, labour fruitlessly on the hard paths of yoga and the rest? By devoting yourselves to *bhakti* to Krishna's lotus feet you will carry off every goal. But if you have hope for the wondrous great festival of love, unattainable even by Shiva, Brahma and the others — then give your heart to Gaura, the abode whose generous majesty is unfathomable.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *The essence of the instruction — leaving futile endeavours on the painful paths of yoga and the like, love Gaura alone*

>> O brothers! What is the use of vain searching on the painfully difficult paths of *ashtanga-yoga* and the rest? Practising *bhakti* to Krishna's lotus feet, plunder the wealth of love — everything most valuable. And if you have hope of the festival of the bliss of love, supremely wondrous, inaccessible even to Shiva, Brahma and the others — then love the most magnanimous Gaurahari, whose glory is unfathomable.

### Verse 87

यथा यथा गौरपदारविन्दे विन्देत भक्तिं कृतपुण्यराशिः ।
तथा तथोत्सर्पति हृद्यकस्माद्राधापदाम्भोजसुधाम्बुराशिः ॥ ८७ ॥

*yathā yathā gaurapadāravinde vindeta bhaktiṁ kṛtapuṇyarāśiḥ |*
*tathā tathotsarpati hṛdyakasmādrādhāpadāmbhojasudhāmburāśiḥ || 87 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* krita-punya-rashih — a person who has performed heaps of good deeds (*sukriti*); gaura-pada-aravinde — for Sri Gaurasundara's lotus feet; yatha yatha — to the extent that; bhaktim vindeta — obtains *bhakti*; akasmat — suddenly; [tasya — his]; hridi — in the heart; tatha tatha — to that same extent; radha-pada-ambhoja-sudha-ambu-rashih — the ocean of the nectar of Sri Radha's lotus feet; utsarpati — surges up.

**87.** As one who has amassed heaps of pious deeds attains *bhakti* to Gaura's lotus feet, so the ocean of the nectar of Radha's lotus feet suddenly rises in his heart.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *The fruit of bhakti for Gaura is service to Radha*

>> To the extent that a person possessing heaps of piety (*sukriti*) obtains *bhakti* for Sri Gaura's lotus feet, to that same extent the ocean of the nectar of love for the lotus feet of Sri Sri Radha suddenly surges up in his heart.

### Verse 88

अपारस्य प्रेमोज्ज्वलरसरहस्यामृतनिधे-
र्निधानं ब्रह्मेशार्चित इह हि चैतन्यचरणः ।
अतस्तं ध्यायन्तः प्रणयभरतो यान्तु शरणं
तमेव प्रोन्मत्तास्तमिह किल गायन्तु कृतिनः ॥ ८८ ॥

*apārasya premojjvalarasarahasyāmṛtanidhe-*
*rnidhānaṁ brahmeśārcita iha hi caitanyacaraṇaḥ |*
*atastaṁ dhyāyantaḥ praṇayabharato yāntu śaraṇaṁ*
*tameva pronmattāstamiha kila gāyantu kṛtinaḥ || 88 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* caitanya-caranah — Chaitanya's feet; hi — since; brahma-isha-arcitah — are worshipped even by Shiva and Brahma; aparasya — of the shoreless; prema-ujjvala-rasa-rahasya-amrita-nidheh — of the ocean of nectar which is the secret of the radiant *rasa* of *prema*; nidhanam — the receptacle; atah — therefore; kritinah — those possessing *sukriti*, that is, people who accept the essence (*saragrahi*); iha — in this age of Kali; pranaya-bharatah — in the abundance of love; tam — those feet of Chaitanya; dhyayantah — contemplating; tam eva — of them alone; sharanam yantu — let them seek shelter; pronmattah [santah] — filled with the greatest joy; tam — the glory of His lotus feet; kila — certainly; iha — in this age; gayantu — let them sing.

**88.** The feet of Chaitanya, worshipped by Brahma and Shiva, are the treasury of the shoreless ocean of the nectar of the secret of the *rasa* radiant with love. Therefore let the wise, meditating on them, take refuge in them in the fullness of love, and let them, as if mad, sing of them alone here.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *The supremacy of the shelter of Gaura's feet*

>> Chaitanya's feet, worshipped even by Shiva and Brahma, are the receptacle of the shoreless ocean of the nectar of the secret of the elevated, radiant *rasa*. Therefore in this age of Kali let pious people, [lovingly meditating on them,] take shelter of them and, maddened with love, in this very age sing of them alone (the glory of Chaitanya's feet).

### Verse 89

दन्ते निधाय तृणकं पदयोर्निपत्य
कृत्वा च काकुशतमेतदहं ब्रवीमि ।
हे साधवः सकलमेव विहाय दूरा-
द्गौराङ्गचन्द्रचरणे कुरुतानुरागम् ॥ ८९ ॥

*dante nidhāya tṛṇakaṁ padayornipatya*
*kṛtvā ca kākuśatametadahaṁ bravīmi |*
*he sādhavaḥ sakalameva vihāya dūrā-*
*dgaurāṅgacandracaraṇe kurutānurāgam || 89 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* dante trinakam nidhaya — taking a tiny straw between the teeth (this indicates the humility "lower than grass" of the preacher of *kirtana*, the *tridandi-gosvami*: for of the grasses that the people of the world trample underfoot, he holds between his teeth the smallest straw, that is, shows respect even to it); padayoh nipatya — falling at the feet (this indicates the quality of "*amani*" — freedom from the desire for respect — of the preacher of the message of Gaura, the *tridandi-gosvami*: unlike the *brahma-sannyasi*, he is not intoxicated with pride and does not consider himself "Narayana," and, unlike the *smartas*, he is not deprived of the vision of the true nature (*svarupa*) of all beings and is not fettered by worldly judgements about the gross and subtle body — brahmana or *candala*, senior or junior; therefore, having become "*amani*" before all, he strives to proclaim the truth); kaku-shatam kritva ca — with a hundred plaintive entreaties (this indicates the "tolerance like a tree" of the preacher of *kirtana*, the *tridandi-gosvami*: the people of the world, turned away from Krishna, do not at all wish to hear the truth, but the *tridandi-gosvami*, entreating them, [tries] to make them hear *hari-katha*); etat aham bravimi — I say this; he sadhavah — O *sadhus* (this indicates the quality of "*manada*" — giving respect to others — of the preacher of *kirtana*, the *tridandi-gosvami*); sakalam eva vihaya durat — leaving everything from afar (you are, of course, *sadhus*, but everything on account of which you have in your heart the pride "I am a *sadhu*" or the fame of a *sadhu* in the world — *karma*, *jnana*, Sankhya and yoga, *phalgu-vairagya*, the four goals, the performance of *dharma* and *adharma*, and all good and bad endeavours or desires opposed to the gratification of Krishna's senses — leaving that from afar); caitanya-candra-carane — for the feet of the Sri Chaitanya-moon; anuragam kuruta — be filled with attachment.

**89.** Holding a straw between my teeth, falling at your feet, with a hundred plaintive entreaties I say this: "O saints! Leave everything far behind and love the feet of Gauranga-chandra!"

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *The method of preaching bhakti for Gaura of the tridandi-gosvamis: leaving everything, the only duty is love for Gaura's feet*

>> O *sadhus*! I (a *tridandi* mendicant), holding a straw between my teeth and falling at your feet, with hundreds of entreaties say only this (beg this alms): leaving everything (all the sanctity or *dharma* you have invented) far behind (rejecting it), be filled with love for the feet of Sri Chaitanya-chandra.

### Verse 90

अहो न दुर्लभा मुक्तिर्न च भक्तिः सुदुर्लभा ।
गौरचन्द्रप्रसादस्तु वैकुण्ठेऽपि सुदुर्लभः ॥ ९० ॥

*aho na durlabhā muktirna ca bhaktiḥ sudurlabhā |*
*gauracandraprasādastu vaikuṇṭhe'pi sudurlabhaḥ || 90 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* [if it is asked: "why, leaving liberation, *bhakti* (*vaidhi-bhakti*, full of majesty) and the rest, become attached to Gaura's lotus feet?" — anticipating this question, he says]; aho — oh!; muktih na durlabha — liberation is not hard to obtain (the four kinds of *mukti* — *salokya* and the rest — are not hard to attain, for they are easily obtained by *vaidhi-bhakti* mixed with *jnana*); bhaktih ca na sudurlabha — and *bhakti* is not extremely hard to attain (for by *vaidhi-sadhana* service to Narayana in Vaikuntha is obtained); gaura-candra-prasadah tu — but the favour of Gaurachandra, that is, the tasting of the extremely secret nectar of *prema*, manifested by Gaura's mercy; vaikunthe api sudurlabhah — is extremely hard to attain even in Vaikuntha.

**90.** Oh! Liberation is not hard to attain, and *bhakti* is not so very hard; but the grace of Gaurachandra is hard to attain even in Vaikuntha.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Gaura's mercy — the tasting of the most secret nectar of love — is hard to attain even in Vaikuntha*

>> Liberation — *brahma-sayujya* or the four kinds of liberation, *salokya* and the rest, attained by *vaidhi-bhakti* mixed with knowledge — is not hard to attain. Service to Narayana, attained by *vaidhi-bhakti*, is not hard to attain either. But Gaura's mercy (Gaura's mercy is threefold: (1) pure — in the form of the removal of *anarthas*; (2) […] — in the form of attaining the *rasa* of the *siddhanta* of *bhakti*; (3) joyful — in the form of attaining the dignity of *madhurya*) is hard to attain even in Vaikuntha, for even the associates of Narayana in Vaikuntha pray for Gaura's mercy.

### Verse 91

भजन्तु चैतन्यपदारविन्दं भवन्तु सद्भक्तिरसेन पूर्णाः ।
आनन्दयन्तु त्रिजगद्विचित्रं माधुर्यसौभाग्यदयाक्षमाद्यैः ॥ ९१ ॥

*bhajantu caitanyapadāravindaṁ bhavantu sadbhaktirasena pūrṇāḥ |*
*ānandayantu trijagadvicitraṁ mādhuryasaubhāgyadayākṣamādyaiḥ || 91 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* caitanya-pada-aravindam bhajantu — take shelter of the lotus feet of Sri Chaitanyadeva; sat-bhakti-rasena ca purnah bhavantu — and be filled with the highest *rasa* of *bhakti*, that is, be immersed in the *unnata-ujjvala-rasa*, never given before (*anarpita-cari*) (this is possible only in the shelter of Chaitanya's feet); madhurya-saubhagya-daya-kshama-adyaih — *madhurya* — by a sweet nature, *saubhagya* — by the highest tasting of *prema*, *daya* — by the distribution of this *prema* to all living beings, *kshama* — by humility "lower than grass" and the other Vaishnava qualities; tri-jagat — the three worlds; vicitram — in manifold ways; anandayantu — gladden.

**91.** Worship the lotus feet of Chaitanya, be filled with the *rasa* of true *bhakti*, and delight the three worlds with manifold sweetness, the highest fortune of tasting *prema*, compassion, forbearance and the rest.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *By taking shelter of Gaura, world-attracting qualities become possible — a sweet nature, good fortune, compassion and forbearance*

>> Take shelter of the lotus feet of the Chaitanya-moon, be filled with the supreme, that is, never before given (*anarpita-cari*), elevated, radiant *rasa*, and give the three worlds manifold bliss through such good qualities as sweetness (a sweet nature), good fortune (the tasting of the highest love), compassion (turning averse souls towards Krishna, or preaching the Name) and forbearance (tolerance like a tree).

### Verse 92

संसारसिन्धुतरणे हृदयं यदि स्यात्
सङ्कीर्तनामृतरसे रमते मनश्चेत् ।
प्रेमाम्बुधौ विहरणे यदि चित्तवृत्ति-
श्चैतन्यचन्द्रचरणे शरणं प्रयातु ॥ ९२ ॥

*saṁsārasindhutaraṇe hṛdayaṁ yadi syāt*
*saṅkīrtanāmṛtarase ramate manaścet |*
*premāmbudhau viharaṇe yadi cittavṛtti-*
*ścaitanyacandracaraṇe śaraṇaṁ prayātu || 92 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* yadi samsara-sindhu-tarane hridayam syat — if someone has the desire to cross the ocean of worldly existence (that is, the desire, having crossed *samsara*, to plunge "into the ocean of *bhakti*"); manah cet sankirtana-amrita-rase ramate — if the mind wishes to delight in the sweetness of the nectar *rasa* of *sankirtana*; yadi prema-ambudhau viharane citta-vrittih [syat] — if the mind inclines to play in the ocean of the *rasa* of *prema*; [tada — then]; caitanya-candra-carane sharanam prayatu — let him take shelter of the feet of the Chaitanya-moon.

**92.** If the heart longs to cross the ocean of worldly existence, if the mind delights in the nectar *rasa* of *sankirtana*, if thought wishes to sport in the ocean of love — let it take refuge in the feet of Chaitanya-chandra.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Without the shelter of Gaura's feet alone it is impossible to cross samsara, taste the rasa of sankirtana and obtain the wealth of love*

>> If there is a desire to cross the ocean of *samsara*, if the mind wishes to delight in the sweetness of the nectar *rasa* of *sankirtana*, if there is a longing to play in the ocean of love — then take shelter of the feet of Sri Chaitanya-chandra.

### Verse 93

ज्ञानवैराग्यभक्त्यादि साधयन्तु यथा तथा ।
चैतन्यचरणाम्भोजभक्तिलभ्यसमं कुतः ॥ ९३ ॥

*jñānavairāgyabhaktyādi sādhayantu yathā tathā |*
*caitanyacaraṇāmbhojabhaktilabhyasamaṁ kutaḥ || 93 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* jnana-vairagya-bhakti-adi — knowledge, renunciation, *bhakti* and the rest; yatha-tatha — in whatever way; sadhayantu — let them practise (or produce); caitanya-candra-carana-ambhoja-bhakti-labhya-samam — like that special knowledge, renunciation and *bhakti* which are obtained by serving the lotus feet of Chaitanya-chandra, that is, what the devotees of Chaitanya-chandra's feet possess — the knowledge of the truth of the all-embracing sweetness of Svayam Bhagavan Sri Krishna, […] detachment and the *unnata-ujjvala bhakti-rasa*; kutah — where else? (that is, it is found nowhere else).

**93.** Let them practise knowledge, renunciation, *bhakti* and the rest as they may — but where is anything equal to what is attained by devotion to the lotus feet of Chaitanya?[^03-3]

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Siddhanta, prema-bhakti and renunciation abide only in Gaura's devotees*

>> Let (people) cultivate knowledge, renunciation, *bhakti* and the rest in whatever way they like — but where [is one to find] that special knowledge, renunciation and *bhakti* which are obtained by serving the lotus feet of the Chaitanya-moon? Nothing like it is to be seen anywhere else.

### Verse 94

अचैतन्यमिदं विश्वं यदि चैतन्यमीश्वरम् ।
न भजेत् सर्वतो मृत्युरुपास्यममरोत्तमैः ॥ ९४ ॥

*acaitanyamidaṁ viśvaṁ yadi caitanyamīśvaram |*
*na bhajet sarvato mṛtyurupāsyamamarottamaiḥ || 94 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* idam — this visible; acaitanyam — devoid of consciousness, that is, devoid of realization of one's own *svarupa*, or devoid of *bhakti* for the all-pervading Consciousness (*vibhu-caitanya*); vishvam — whole world; amara-uttamaih — by the best of the gods; upasyam — worshipped; caitanyam ishvaram — the Lord Chaitanya, that is, Chaitanya who rules all; yadi na bhajet — if it does not worship; [tada — then]; sarvatah mrityuh — death in every respect; [bhavati — there is] (in the *anuvada*: death of every kind, that is, subject to birth and death, they taste the fruits of *karma* — happiness, misery and the like).

**94.** This world, devoid of consciousness (*a-chaitanya*, that is, devoid of devotion to Krishna), if it does not worship Chaitanya — the Lord worshipped by the best of the gods — meets death on every side.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *One not devoted to Chaitanya obtains only the round of coming and going in samsara*

>> If this world, devoid of *bhakti* for Krishna and devoid of consciousness, does not worship Bhagavan Sri Chaitanya Himself, the all-controlling, worthy of worship even by the best of the gods — Shiva, Brahma and the others — it obtains only death in all its forms, that is, people subject to birth and death merely taste the fruits of *karma* — happiness, misery and the like.

### Verse 95

आशा यस्य पदद्वन्द्वे चैतन्यस्य महाप्रभोः ।
तस्येन्द्रो दासवद्भाति का कथा नृपकीटके ॥ ९५ ॥

*āśā yasya padadvandve caitanyasya mahāprabhoḥ |*
*tasyendro dāsavadbhāti kā kathā nṛpakīṭake || 95 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* caitanyasya mahaprabhoh — of Sri Chaitanya Mahaprabhu; pada-dvandve — in the two feet; yasya asha — one who has hope; tasya — to him; indrah [api] — even Indra; dasavat bhati — appears as a servant; [anye — of others]; nripa-kitake — of a king who is like an insignificant worm; ka katha — what to speak.

**95.** For one whose hope is in the two feet of Chaitanya Mahaprabhu, Indra himself seems like a servant — what to speak of a worm of a king!

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *A person having even the slightest bhakti for the Chaitanya-moon is able to engage even […] the gods in his service*

>> To one who has hope in the two feet of Sri Chaitanya Mahaprabhu, even the lord of the gods appears as a servant; what to speak of a king, who is like an insignificant worm?

### Verse 96

यस्याशा कृष्णचैतन्ये नृपद्वारि किमर्थिनः ।
चिन्तामणिमयं प्राप्य को मूढो रजतं व्रजेत् ॥ ९६ ॥

*yasyāśā kṛṣṇacaitanye nṛpadvāri kimarthinaḥ |*
*cintāmaṇimayaṁ prāpya ko mūḍho rajataṁ vrajet || 96 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* yasya arthinah — of the petitioner who; krishna-caitanye asha [vartate] — has the hope of obtaining Sri Krishna-Chaitanya; [tasya — of him]; nripa-dvari — at the door of a king; kim — what need?; cintamanim prapya — having obtained a *cintamani*; kah mudhah — who is such a fool; rajatam vrajet — that he would go (to distant lands) for silver?

**96.** Who, having placed his hope in Krishna Chaitanya, would go begging at a king's door? What fool, having obtained a *chintamani*, would go after silver?

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *The great souls who have obtained the cintamani of the worship of Gaura take no thought for their own maintenance*

>> What need of the royal gate has that petitioner who longs for the wealth of the feet of Sri Krishna-Chaitanya? Is there such a fool who, having obtained a *cintamani*, will go (to distant lands) for silver?

### Verse 97

ध्यायन्तो गिरिकन्दरेषु बहवो ब्रह्मानुभूयासते
योगाभ्यासपराश्च सन्ति बहवः सिद्धा महीमण्डले ।
विद्याशौर्यधनादिभिश्च बहवो जल्पन्ति मिथ्योद्धताः
को वा गौरकृपां विनाद्य जगति प्रेमोन्मदो नृत्यति ॥ ९७ ॥

*dhyāyanto girikandareṣu bahavo brahmānubhūyāsate*
*yogābhyāsaparāśca santi bahavaḥ siddhā mahīmaṇḍale |*
*vidyāśauryadhanādibhiśca bahavo jalpanti mithyoddhatāḥ*
*ko vā gaurakṛpāṁ vinādya jagati premonmado nṛtyati || 97 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* giri-kandareshu — in mountain caves; bahavah — many people; [dhyayantah] — meditating on the impersonal Brahman; brahma anubhuya — having realized Brahman; asate — abide; ca — and; mahi-mandale — in the circle of the earth; yoga-abhyasa-parah — devoted to the practice of the eightfold yoga; bahavah siddhah — many who have obtained *siddhis* (*anima* and the rest); santi — there are; vidya-shaurya-dhana-adibhih ca — and with learning, strength, wealth and the like; mithya-uddhatah — intoxicated with unreal things; bahavah — many; jalpanti — indulge in idle talk; [kintu — but]; gaura-kripam vina — without Gaura's mercy; jagati — in the world; kah va — who indeed; adya — today; prema-unmadah [san — being] — intoxicated with *prema*; nrityati — dances?

**97.** Many, meditating in mountain caves, abide in the experience of Brahman; there are many *siddhas* on the earth devoted to the practice of yoga; many, falsely puffed up with learning, valour, wealth and so on, prattle on. But who in the world today, without Gaura's mercy, dances intoxicated with love?

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *In the world, apart from Gaura's devotees, there is no one intoxicated with love*

>> In mountain caves many *jnana-yogis*, given to meditation and the search for the undifferentiated Brahman, abide, having realized Brahman; on earth there are many people devoted to the practice of *ashtanga-yoga* who have obtained *siddhis* — *anima* and the rest; and many more, intoxicated with false objects — learning, strength, wealth and the like — engage in empty disputes. But who in the world today, without Gaurahari's mercy, dances in the intoxication of love?

### Verse 98

काशीवासीनपि न गणये किं गयां मार्गयामो
मुक्तिः शुक्ती भवति यदि मे कः परार्थप्रसङ्गः ।
त्रासाभासः स्फुरति न महारौरवेऽपि क्व भीतिः
स्त्रीपुत्रादौ यदि कृपयते देवदेवः स गौरः ॥ ९८ ॥

*kāśīvāsīnapi na gaṇaye kiṁ gayāṁ mārgayāmo*
*muktiḥ śuktī bhavati yadi me kaḥ parārthaprasaṅgaḥ |*
*trāsābhāsaḥ sphurati na mahāraurave'pi kva bhītiḥ*
*strīputrādau yadi kṛpayate devadevaḥ sa gauraḥ || 98 ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* yadi devadevah — if He who is worshipped even by the gods; sah gaurah — that Gaurasundara; kripayate — is merciful; [tada — then]; kashi-vasin api — even the residents of Kashi (that is, those who have settled in Kashi for the sake of undifferentiated liberation); na ganaye — I count as nothing; kim gayam margayamah — why seek Gaya? (that is, Gaura's devotee, unlike the *karmis* seeking fruits, does not bustle about the *shraddha* at Gaya in order to offer *pindas* to the ancestors); yadi me muktih — if for me even liberation; shukti bhavati — becomes a shell; [tada — then]; kah para-artha-prasangah — what to speak of the fruits of the three other goals and the rest!; maha-raurave api — even in the great hell Raurava; [yadi — if]; trasa-abhasah na sphurati — there is not a shadow of fear; [tada — then]; stri-putra-adi — with regard to wife, sons and the rest; kva bhitih — where is fear?

**98.** I do not count even the dwellers of Kashi — why should I seek out Gaya? Liberation is to me an empty shell — what to speak of other goals? Even in the great hell Raurava not a shadow of fear appears to me — how then could I fear for wife, sons and the rest — if Gaura, the God of gods, is merciful to me.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Gaura's devotee […] is free from petty desires*

>> If Sri Gaurasundara, the Lord even of the gods — Brahma, Shiva and the rest — is merciful, then I count the residents of Kashi as nothing; why should I seek the abode of Gaya? If liberation itself seems to me like mother-of-pearl, what is there to say of the three goals — *dharma*, *artha* and *kama*? And if even in Maharaurava not a shadow of fear approaches me, where then is fear for wife, sons and the like?

[^03-1]: *Gaura-dhaman* — according to Bhaktisiddhanta, "possessing a golden radiance (*gaura-kanti*)," i.e. Sri Hari in the form of Gaura; literally it may also mean "He whose abode is Gaura."

[^03-2]: The summit of Niladri (Nilachala) is Puri; "the face of Hari" is Jagannatha, on whom Mahaprabhu gazes unceasingly.

[^03-3]: The first line has been restored from the Bengali edition (No. 94 there).

---

# Part Four

*The supremacy of Sri Chaitanya. The glory of His descent. The beauty and dance of Gaura. Lament (verses 99–142)*

## The supremacy of Sri Chaitanya (utkarsha)

### Verse 99

मत्तकेशरिकिशोरविक्रमः प्रेमसिन्धुजगदाप्लवोद्यमः ।
कोऽपि दिव्यनवहेमकन्दलीकोमलो जयति गौरचन्द्रमाः ॥

*mattakeśarikiśoravikramaḥ premasindhujagadāplavodyamaḥ |*
*ko'pi divyanavahemakandalīkomalo jayati gauracandramāḥ ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* matta-keshari-kishora-vikramah — mighty as an intoxicated young lion, that is, in subduing Kali possessing valour like that of an intoxicated young lion; prema-sindhu-jagat-aplava-udyamah — striving to flood the world by making the ocean of love surge; divya-nava-hema-kandali-komalah — (*divya* — beautiful; *nava-hema-kandali-komala* — tender as a freshly blossomed golden *kandali* [flower]); kah api gaura-candramah — a certain indescribable Gaurachandra; jayati — shines, surpassing all.

**99.** With the valour of a young lion in rut, eager to flood the world with the ocean of love, tender as a wondrous, newly blossomed golden *kandali* flower — glory to the indescribable Gaura-chandra, the Moon!

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Description of the supremacy of Gaurahari's form, qualities, sweetness, valour and generosity*

>> A certain indescribable Gaurachandra — mighty as an intoxicated young lion in subduing Kali, more tender than a beautiful, freshly blossomed golden *kandali* bud, striving to flood the world by making the ocean of love surge — shines, surpassing all.

### Verse 100

सौन्दर्ये कामकोटिः सकलजनसमाह्लादने चन्द्रकोटि-
र्वात्सल्ये मातृकोटिस्त्रिदशविटपिनां कोटिरौदार्यसारे ।
गाम्भीर्येऽम्भोधिकोटिर्मधुरिमणि सुधाक्षीरमाध्वीककोटि-
र्गौरो देवः स जीयात् प्रणयरसपदे दर्शिताश्चर्यकोटिः ॥

*saundarye kāmakoṭiḥ sakalajanasamāhlādane candrakoṭi-*
*rvātsalye mātṛkoṭistridaśaviṭapināṁ koṭiraudāryasāre |*
*gāmbhīrye'mbhodhikoṭirmadhurimaṇi sudhākṣīramādhvīkakoṭi-*
*rgauro devaḥ sa jīyāt praṇayarasapade darśitāścaryakoṭiḥ ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* [yah — He who]; saundarye — in beauty; kama-kotih — millions of Kamadevas, that is, He whose wealth of beauty puts to shame countless Madanas; sakala-jana-samahladane — in giving delight (refreshing tenderness) to all people; candra-kotih — millions of moons, that is, belittling the coolness of countless moons; vatsalye — in loving tenderness; matri-kotih — millions of mothers, that is, belittling the loving tenderness of countless mothers; audarya-sare — in the height of generosity; tridasha-vitapinam kotih — millions of the trees of the gods, that is, putting to shame the generosity of countless wish-fulfilling trees; gambhirye — in depth; ambhodhi-kotih — millions of oceans, that is, He whose [depth] surpasses the depth of thousands upon thousands of oceans; madhurimani — in sweetness; sudha-kshira-madhvika-kotih — millions of essences of nectar, [of milk drinks] and of essences of honey ("*madhvika*" — an intoxicating drink made from honey; here the word "*sudha*" indicates immortality and an unprecedented taste, the word "*kshira*" satisfaction and [nourishment], the word "*madhvika*" the madness of love); pranaya-rasa-pade — with regard to *shringara-rasa*; darshita-ashcarya-kotih — who has displayed millions of wonders, that is, the *pranaya-rasa* manifested by Sri Gaurasundara is millions of times more wondrous than the sweetness of *shringara-rasa* shown by the former connoisseurs of *rasa*; sah devah gaurah — that Gaurasundara who sports in pastimes (*lilamaya*); jiyat — may He be glorious.

**100.** In beauty — millions of Kamadevas; in delighting all people — millions of moons; in tenderness — millions of mothers; in the essence of generosity — millions of trees of heaven; in depth — millions of oceans; in sweetness — millions of nectars, milks and honeys; in the realm of the *rasa* of love He has shown millions of wonders. Glory to the Lord Gaura!

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *The supreme excellence of the beauty, gladdening power, loving tenderness, generosity, depth and sweetness of Krishna clothed in the mood and lustre of Radha.*

>> He who in beauty is millions of Kamadevas, in gladdening all living beings millions of moons, in loving tenderness millions of mothers, in the height of generosity millions of wish-fulfilling trees, in depth millions of oceans, in sweetness millions of essences of nectar, millions of [milk drinks] and millions of essences of honey, and who in the field of *shringara-rasa* displays millions of wonders (variety of *rasa*) — may that Gaurahari, who sports in pastimes, be glorious!

### Verse 101

स्वपादाम्भोजैकप्रणयलहरीसाधनभृतां
शिवब्रह्मादीनामपि च सुमहाविस्मयभृताम् ।
महाप्रेमावेशात् किमपि नटतामुन्मद इव
प्रभुर्गौरो जीयात् प्रकटपरमाश्चर्यमहिमा ॥

*svapādāmbhojaikapraṇayalaharīsādhanabhṛtāṁ*
*śivabrahmādīnāmapi ca sumahāvismayabhṛtām |*
*mahāpremāveśāt kimapi naṭatāmunmada iva*
*prabhurgauro jīyāt prakaṭaparamāścaryamahimā ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* sva-pada-ambhoja-eka-pranaya-lahari-sadhana-bhritam — (*sva-pada-ambhoja* — His own lotus feet; *eka* — surpassing all; *pranaya-lahari* — the wave of *bhava*; *sadhana* — the means to manifest it in the heart; of the *sadhana-bhaktas*, that is, of those *sadhana-bhaktas* who abide [in *sadhana*] in order to manifest in the heart the all-surpassing wave of *prema* for His lotus feet); shiva-brahma-adinam api ca — even of Shiva, Brahma and the others; su-maha-vismaya-bhritam — who give boundless astonishment; maha-prema-aveshat — because of absorption in *maha-prema*; unmadah iva — as if mad; kim api natam — of the devotees who dance wondrously; prakatita-parama-ashcarya-mahima — He who has manifested their supremely wondrous glory; prabhuh — the independent Lord; gaurah — Gaurasundara; jiyat — may He be glorious.

**101.** Glory to the Lord Gaura, who has revealed the supreme, wondrous glory of those who hold to the *sadhana* that leads to the matchless wave of love for His lotus feet, and who, in the transport of great love, dance indescribably as if mad, filling with great amazement even Shiva, Brahma and the others.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Sri Gaurahari, who displays […] that astonish even Shiva, Brahma and the others.*

>> May Bhagavan Sri Gaurahari Himself be glorious in supreme excellence — He who manifested the most wondrous glory of the devotees abiding in *sadhana-bhakti*, the means to obtain the highest wave of *prema-bhakti* for His lotus feet, and of the devotees who, immersed in *mahabhava*, dance wondrously as if mad, causing the greatest astonishment even to Shiva, Brahma and the others.

### Verse 102

माद्यत्कोटिमृगेन्द्रहुङ्कृतिरवस्तिग्मांशुकोटिच्छविः
कोटीन्दूद्भटशीतलो गतिजितप्रोन्मत्तकोटिद्विपः ।
नाम्ना दुर्गतकोटिनिष्कृतिकरो ब्रह्मादिकोटीश्वरः
कोट्यद्वैतशिरोमणिर्विजयते श्रीश्रीशचीनन्दनः ॥

*mādyatkoṭimṛgendrahuṅkṛtiravastigmāṁśukoṭicchaviḥ*
*koṭīndūdbhaṭaśītalo gatijitapronmattakoṭidvipaḥ |*
*nāmnā durgatakoṭiniṣkṛtikaro brahmādikoṭīśvaraḥ*
*koṭyadvaitaśiromaṇirvijayate śrīśrīśacīnandanaḥ ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* matta-koti-mrigendra-hunkriti-ravah — He whose voice is like the roar of millions of intoxicated lions; tigmamshu-koti-chavih — possessing a bodily radiance brighter than that of millions of suns; koti-indu-udbhata-shitalah — cooler than millions of moons; gati-jita-pronmatta-koti-dvipah — with a gait more beautiful than the stride of millions of intoxicated elephants; namna — by the name (the *maha-mantra* "Hare Krishna" and the rest, or the names "Krishna-Chaitanya," "Vishvambhara" and the rest); durgata-koti-nishkriti-karah — the deliverer of millions of the distressed; brahma-adi-koti-ishvarah — the master of millions upon millions of Brahmas and others; koti-advaita-shiromanih — (the crown of millions upon millions of *advaita-vadis*, worshipped by them, the highest summit of the perception of the impersonal Brahman — Parabrahman, that is, the condensed Parabrahman in human form); shri-shri-shacinandanah — Sri Sri Shachinandana; vijayate — shines with supreme excellence.

**102.** His roar is like the roar of millions of maddened kings of beasts, His lustre like that of millions of suns, His coolness greater than that of millions of moons; by His gait He surpasses millions of maddened elephants; by His Name He redeems millions of the wretched; He is the Lord of millions of Brahmas and others, the crest-jewel of millions of knowers of non-duality[^04-1] — glory to Sri Sri Shachinandana!

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Description of Gaurahari's supremacy through the display of His great might, radiance, beauty, coolness, the sweetness of His gait and […].*

>> Possessing a voice deep as the roar of millions of intoxicated lions; bearing a radiance brighter than that of millions of suns; cooler than millions of moons; with a gait more beautiful than the stride of millions of maddened king-elephants; delivering millions of the distressed by the *sankirtana* of the names "Hare Krishna" and the rest; the master of millions of Brahmas and others; worshipped by millions of *advaita-vadis*, the highest summit [of the realization] of the impersonal Brahman — Parabrahman, that is, the embodiment of the complete realization of *advaya-jnana* (non-dual knowledge) — may Sri Sri Shachinandana be especially glorious!

### Verse 103

यो मार्गो दूरशून्यो बत इह बलवत्कण्टको योऽतिदुर्गो
मिथ्यार्थभ्रामको यः सपदि रसमयानन्दनिःस्यन्दको यः ।
सद्यः प्रद्योतयंस्तं प्रकटितमहिमा स्नेहवान् हृद्गुहायाः
कोऽप्यन्तर्ध्वान्तहन्ता स जयति नवद्वीपदीप्यत्प्रदीपः ॥

*yo mārgo dūraśūnyo bata iha balavatkaṇṭako yo'tidurgo*
*mithyārthabhrāmako yaḥ sapadi rasamayānandaniḥsyandako yaḥ |*
*sadyaḥ pradyotayaṁstaṁ prakaṭitamahimā snehavān hṛdguhāyāḥ*
*ko'pyantardhvāntahantā sa jayati navadvīpadīpyatpradīpaḥ ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* iha — in this world; yah dura-shunyah — that which is "*dura*" — inaccessible to the material intellect — and "*shunya*" — devoid of the gross material aspect of the endeavours of *karma* and the like; [yo va — or which]; bata — alas (in regret); balavat-kantakah — blocked by thorns — dry knowledge and attachment to regular and occasional *karma*; [atah — therefore]; yah ati-durgah — hard to travel, extremely difficult; yah mithya-artha-bhramakah — (*mithya-artha* — a false, unreal object; producing the knowledge of it as real, that is, making even the wise (*suris*) consider false *bhakti*, pretended *bhakti* and the like to be "*bhakti*"); yah sapadi rasa-maya-ananda-nihsyandakah — which at once brings a stream of the *rasa* of the bliss of *prema*; margah — the path; tam — that [path of *bhakti*]; sadyah — at once; pradyotayan — […] illumining; [prakatita-mahima — not legible in the OCR of the *anvaya*; in the *anuvada*: "who manifests the glory of *bhakti*"]; hrit-guhah — of the cave of the heart; antar-dhvanta-hanta — destroying the darkness of ignorance; snehavan — full of tender love (with reference to a lamp, "*sneha*" is oil); navadvipa-dipyat-pradipah — the lamp shining in Navadvipa; ko 'pi — a certain indescribable Purusha; sah jayati — may He be glorious.

**103.** The path that is beyond the reach of worldly intellect and devoid of outward glitter — alas, overgrown here with mighty thorns and therefore hard to travel, leading astray with false meanings, yet at once pouring forth bliss full of *rasa* — that path He at once illumines, revealing its glory; full of tenderness, He drives the darkness out of the cave of the heart — glory to that indescribable shining lamp of Navadvipa.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *The lamp of Gaura, which destroys […] darkness and illumines the path of tasting the secret rasa of prema.*

>> The path of *bhakti* is inaccessible to the material intellect and devoid of the external pomp of *karma* and the like; alas, it is blocked by the thorns of dry knowledge and attachment to *karma* and is therefore extremely hard to travel; it misleads, making one take the false for the true, and at once brings a stream of the *rasa* of the bliss of *prema*. Glory to the indescribable Purusha, the lamp of Navadvipa full of tender love, who, instantly illumining that path of *bhakti*, destroys the darkness of ignorance in the cave of the heart and manifests the glory of *bhakti*!

### Verse 104

दूरादेव दहन् कुतर्कशलभान् कोटीन्दुसंशीतलो
ज्योतिःकन्दलसद्म सन्मधुरिमा बाह्यान्तरध्वान्तहृत् ।
सस्नेहाशयवर्तिदिव्यविसरत्तेजाः सुवर्णद्युतिः
कारुण्यादिह जाज्वलीति स नवद्वीपप्रदीपोऽद्भुतः ॥

*dūrādeva dahan kutarkaśalabhān koṭīndusaṁśītalo*
*jyotiḥkandalasadma sanmadhurimā bāhyāntaradhvāntahṛt |*
*sasnehāśayavartidivyavisarattejāḥ suvarṇadyutiḥ*
*kāruṇyādiha jājvalīti sa navadvīpapradīpo'dbhutaḥ ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* kutarka-shalabhan — the moths of false logic; durat eva — even from afar; dahan — burning; koti-indu-samshitalah — cooler than millions of moons; jyotih-kandala-sadma — the abode of a mass of light; sat-madhurima — (*sat* — excellent, *madhurima* — sweetness; that is, supremely sweet); bahya-antara-dhvanta-hrit — destroying darkness outer and inner; sa-sneha-ashaya-varti-divya-visarat-tejah — (*sa-sneha* — abiding together with *sneha* (love), and in another sense with ghee or other oil; *ashaya* — the heart, and in another sense a vessel; that is the *varti* — the wick; *divya-visarat-tejah* — He from whom a radiance issues divinely, beautifully; that is, He whose radiance issues when the wick of the heart, which abides together with the oil of love, is kindled); suvarna-dyutih — possessing a golden lustre; adbhutah — unearthly (*aprakrita*); sah navadvipa-pradipah — that lamp of Navadvipa; karunyat — out of mercy; iha — in this world (*prapanca*); jajvaliti — shines brightly.

**104.** Burning from afar the moths of false logic, cool as millions of moons, the abode of sprouting light, the highest sweetness, dispelling the outer and inner darkness; His divine light spreads from the wick of the heart steeped in the oil of love; shining like gold — out of mercy this wondrous lamp of Navadvipa blazes brightly here.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *The lamp of Navadvipa, ever burning with a bright flame.*

>> That unearthly (*aprakrita*) lamp which even from afar burns the moths of false logic; which is cooler than millions of moons and is the abode of a mass of light; supremely sweet; destroying darkness outer and inner; from whose wick of the heart, soaked in the oil of love (*sneha*), a divine radiance issues; whose splendour is like gold — that lamp of Navadvipa (Gaurasundara) shines in this world out of mercy.

### Verse 105

चीत्कारैर्दशदिङ्मुखं मुखरयन्नट्टाट्टहासच्छटा-
वीचीभिः स्फुटकुन्दकैरवगणप्रोद्भासि कुर्वन्नभः ।
सर्वाङ्गं पवनोच्चलच्चलदलप्रायप्रकम्पं दध-
न्मत्तः प्रेमरसोन्मदाप्लुतगतिर्गौरो हरिः शोभते ॥

*cītkārairdaśadiṅmukhaṁ mukharayannaṭṭāṭṭahāsacchaṭā-*
*vīcībhiḥ sphuṭakundakairavagaṇaprodbhāsi kurvannabhaḥ |*
*sarvāṅgaṁ pavanoccalaccaladalaprāyaprakampaṁ dadha-*
*nmattaḥ premarasonmadāplutagatirgauro hariḥ śobhate ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* citkaraih — with cries (sounds uttered with extreme longing from joy and sorrow); dasha-dig-mukham — the ten directions; mukharayan — making resound, making echo; atta-atta-hasa-cchata-vicibhih — with waves of the radiance of pealing laughter; nabhah — the sky; sphuta-kunda-kairava-gana-prodbhasi — ([making it] extremely bright as if with full-blown *kunda* and *kumuda* flowers); pavana-uccalat-caladala-praya-prakampam — (*pavana-uccala* — shaken by the wind; *caladala* — the *ashvattha* tree; *praya* — like it; *prakampa* — trembling); sarva-angam — the whole body; dadhat — having; prema-rasa-unmada-apluta-gatih — (*prema-rasa-unmada* — the intoxication born of the *rasa* of *prema*; by it *apluta-gati*: among the various kinds of movement, the *uddanda*, a particular dance; that is, intoxicated with the joy, pride and the rest born of the *rasa* of *prema*, dancing with high leaps); mattah gaurah harih — the intoxicated Gaurahari; shobhate — shines with supreme excellence.

**105.** Filling all the directions with cries, by the waves of His peals of laughter making the sky white as with blossoming jasmines and lilies, His whole body trembling like the leaves of the *ashvattha* in the wind, intoxicated with the *rasa* of love, dancing with high leaps — Gaura-Hari shines.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Gaurahari, adorned with the vibhavas and the rest and the sattvika-bhavas.*

>> In extreme agitation from joy and sorrow making the ten directions resound with loud cries, with waves of the radiance of pealing loud laughter making the vault of the sky bright as if with full-blown *kunda* and *kumuda* flowers, possessing a body trembling like an *ashvattha* tree shaken by the wind — intoxicated with joy, pride and the other states born of the *rasa* of *prema*, Gaurahari, dancing the *uddanda* (with high leaps), abides in supreme excellence.

### Verse 106

निर्दोषश्चारुनृत्यो विधुतमलिनतावक्रभावः कदाचि-
न्निःशेषप्राणितापत्रयहरणमहाप्रेमपीयूषवर्षी ।
उद्भूतः कोऽपि भाग्योदयरुचिरशचीगर्भदुग्धाम्बुराशे-
र्भक्तानां हृच्चकोरस्वादितपदरुचिर्भाति गौराङ्गचन्द्रः ॥

*nirdoṣaścārunṛtyo vidhutamalinatāvakrabhāvaḥ kadāci-*
*nniḥśeṣaprāṇitāpatrayaharaṇamahāpremapīyūṣavarṣī |*
*udbhūtaḥ ko'pi bhāgyodayaruciraśacīgarbhadugdhāmburāśe-*
*rbhaktānāṁ hṛccakorasvāditapadarucirbhāti gaurāṅgacandraḥ ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* nirdoshah — (without a spot, or: He who has no need of "*dosha*" — night, darkness — that is, ever shining); caru-nrityah — dancing beautifully; vidhuta-malinata-vakra-bhavah — free of dimness and crookedness; nihshesha-prani-tapa-traya-harana-maha-prema-piyusha-varshi — pouring the nectar of *prema* to remove the threefold miseries of all living beings; bhaktanam hrit-cakora-svadita-pada-rucih — (*bhaktanam* — of the devotees; *hrit-cakora* — the *cakora* birds of the hearts; *svadita* — tasted; *pada* — rays; *ruci* — sweetness; that is, He the sweetness of whose rays the *cakoras* of the devotees' hearts taste); ko 'pi gauranga-candrah — a certain indescribable Purusha, Sri Gaurachandra; bhagya-udaya-rucira-shaci-garbha-dugdha-ambu-rasheh — (*bhagya-udaya* — the rising of good fortune, *rucira* — most beautiful; from the milk ocean of the womb of Shachidevi); udbhutah — risen; [san — being]; bhati — shines.

**106.** Without fault (knowing no night), dancing beautifully, utterly free of impurity and crookedness, pouring forth the nectar of great love that removes the threefold miseries of all living beings without exception; risen from the milk ocean of Shachi's womb, made beautiful by the dawning of good fortune; the sweetness of His rays is tasted by the *chakora* birds — the hearts of the devotees: the indescribable moon Gauranga shines.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Gaurachandra, risen in the ocean of Shachi's womb and pouring the nectar of prema.*

>> Spotless and ever risen, dancing wondrously, devoid of dimness and crookedness, pouring the nectar of *prema* to remove the threefold miseries of all living beings, the indescribable Sri Gaurachandra, the sweetness of whose rays the *cakora* birds of the devotees' hearts taste (that is, the sweetness of His rays the *cakoras* of the devotees' hearts taste), having risen from the milk ocean of the womb of the fortunate and most beautiful Sri Shachidevi, shines.

### Verse 107

सिञ्चन् सिञ्चन्नयनपयसा पाण्डुगण्डस्थलान्तं
मुञ्चन् मुञ्चन् प्रतिमुहुरहो दीर्घनिःश्वासजातम् ।
उच्चैः क्रन्दन् करुणकरुणोद्गीर्णहाहेतिरावो
गौरः कोऽपि व्रजविरहिणीभावमग्नश्चकास्ति ॥

*siñcan siñcannayanapayasā pāṇḍugaṇḍasthalāntaṁ*
*muñcan muñcan pratimuhuraho dīrghaniḥśvāsajātam |*
*uccaiḥ krandan karuṇakaruṇodgīrṇahāhetirāvo*
*gauraḥ ko'pi vrajavirahiṇībhāvamagnaścakāsti ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* vraja-virahini-bhava-magnah [san] — immersed in the ocean of the nectar of the *mahabhava* of Srimati Radhika, suffering from separation from Krishna; nayana-payasa — with streams of tears; pandu-ganda-sthala-antam — the edge of the pale cheeks; sincan sincan — wetting again and again; aho — ah!; dirgha-nihshvasa-jatam — many long sighs; pratimuhuh — every moment; muncan muncan — heaving again and again; uccaih krandan — weeping aloud; karuna-karuna-udgirna-ha-ha-iti-ravah — He who utters the sounds "ha! ha!", expressing an extremely piteous *rasa*; kah api gaurah — a certain indescribable Purusha, Sri Gaurasundara; cakasti — manifesting His mood, shines with supreme excellence.

**107.** Again and again moistening His pale cheeks with the water of His eyes, again and again — oh! — heaving long sighs, sobbing aloud, uttering most piteous cries of "Ha! Ha!" — the indescribable Gaura shines, immersed in the mood of a maiden of Vraja in separation.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Sri Gaurahari, immersed in the mood of Srimati Radhika, overwhelmed by the prolonged rasa of separation (vipralambha).*

>> He who, immersed in the ocean of the nectar of the *mahabhava* of Srimati Radhika, suffering in Vraja from separation from Krishna, again and again wets with tears the edges of His pale cheeks; ah! — He who, every moment heaving long sighs, weeps aloud and utters the sounds "ha! ha!", full of an extremely piteous *rasa* — such an indescribable Purusha, Sri Gaurasundara, manifesting His mood, abides in supreme excellence.

### Verse 108

बिभ्रद्वर्णं किमपि दहनोत्तीर्णसौवर्णसारं
दिव्याकारं किमपि कलयन् दृप्तगोपालबालः ।
आविष्कुर्वन् क्वचिदवसरे तत्तदाश्चर्यलीलां
साक्षाद्राधामधुरिपुवपुर्भाति गौराङ्गचन्द्रः ॥

*bibhradvarṇaṁ kimapi dahanottīrṇasauvarṇasāraṁ*
*divyākāraṁ kimapi kalayan dṛptagopālabālaḥ |*
*āviṣkurvan kvacidavasare tattadāścaryalīlāṁ*
*sākṣādrādhāmadhuripuvapurbhāti gaurāṅgacandraḥ ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* dahana-uttirna-sauvarna-saram — like the essence of molten gold that has passed through fire; kim api varnam — a certain indescribable colour; bibhrat — assuming; dripta-gopala-balah [san] — (*dripta* — illumined, that is, manifested; *gopala-bala* — the pastime of Bala-Gopala; that is, displaying the pastime of Bala-Gopala); kim api divya-akaram — a certain indescribable spiritual form (*cinmaya-vigraha*); kalayan — assuming; kvacit avasare — sometimes; tat-tat-ashcarya-lilam — those various most wondrous pastimes (in the *anuvada*: the pastimes of youth, *kaishora*); avishkurvan — revealing; sakshat-radha-madhuripu-vapuh — the visible united body of Radha and Madhava; gauranga-candrah — Sri Gauranga; bhati — shines with supreme beauty; [as Srila Svarupa Gosvami also says, Radha and Krishna are a transformation of love, [a manifestation] of the *hladini* potency; in their essence They are one, but since the *tattva* of pastimes is eternal, They eternally abide in two forms; now these two have appeared in one form — as the *tattva* of Chaitanya].

**108.** Bearing an indescribable colour — the essence of gold refined in fire — now appearing as a frolicsome cowherd boy, now assuming an indescribable divine form, and at times revealing this or that wondrous pastime — Gauranga-chandra shines, manifestly the body of Radha and Madhuripu (Krishna).

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Sri Gaurahari — the united body [of Radha and Krishna].*

>> Assuming a certain indescribable colour like the essence of molten gold that has passed through fire, He displays the pastimes of Bala-Gopala, and sometimes, in a certain indescribable spiritual form, reveals the most wondrous pastimes of youth (*kaishora*) — Sri Gauranga shines, the visible united body of Radha and Madhava.

## The glory of His descent (avatara-mahima)

### Verse 109

अकस्मादेवाविर्भवति भगवन्नामलहरी
परीतानां पापैरपि पुरुभिरेषां तनुभृताम् ।
अहो वज्रप्रायं हृदपि नवनीतायितमभू-
न्नृणां लोके यस्मिन्नवतरति स गौरो मम गतिः ॥

*akasmādevāvirbhavati bhagavannāmalaharī*
*parītānāṁ pāpairapi purubhireṣāṁ tanubhṛtām |*
*aho vajraprāyaṁ hṛdapi navanītāyitamabhū-*
*nnṛṇāṁ loke yasminnavatarati sa gauro mama gatiḥ ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* nrinam loke — in the world of men, that is, in the *prapanca*; yasmin avatarati [sati] — when He descends; aho — ah!; purubhih papaih — by heaps of grievous sins; paritanam — surrounded; esham tanubhritam api — even for these embodied beings; bhagavat-nama-lahari — the wave of the name of Sri Krishna, that is, the sequence of the holy names "Hare Krishna, Hare Krishna" and so on; akasmat eva — suddenly; avirbhavati — appeared; vajra-prayam hrit api — even the heart grown hard as stone through *karma*, *jnana* and the like, or through various *anarthas*; navanitayitam abhut — became soft and melted like fresh butter; sah gaurah — that Gaurasundara; mama gatih [astu] — may He be my refuge.

**109.** Unexpectedly the wave of the Lord's Name comes to embodied beings, even those surrounded by grievous sins; oh! — even men's hearts, hard as diamond, have become like fresh butter — when He descends into the world. That Gaura is my refuge.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *The glory of the Gaura-avatara, who descended together with the name of Krishna, which melts […] hard [hearts].*

>> When He descended into the world of men, ah, even for embodied beings surrounded by heaps of grievous sins a wave of the name of Sri Krishna suddenly appeared, and even the heart grown hard as stone through offences became soft and melted like fresh butter — may that Gaurasundara be my only refuge.

### Verse 110

न योगो न ध्यानं न च जपतपस्त्यागनियमा
न वेदा नाचारः क्व नु बत निषिद्धाद्युपरतिः ।
अकस्माच्चैतन्येऽवतरति दयासारहृदये
पुमर्थानां मौलिं परमिह मुदा लुण्ठति जनः ॥

*na yogo na dhyānaṁ na ca japatapastyāganiyamā*
*na vedā nācāraḥ kva nu bata niṣiddhādyuparatiḥ |*
*akasmāccaitanye'vatarati dayāsārahṛdaye*
*pumarthānāṁ mauliṁ paramiha mudā luṇṭhati janaḥ ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* akasmat daya-sara-hridaye caitanye iha avatarati [sati] — when the supremely merciful Sri Chaitanyadeva suddenly descended into this world; [yasya — one who]; na yogah na dhyanam na ca japa-tapah-tyaga-niyamah [santi] — has neither yoga, nor meditation, nor *japa*, austerity, renunciation and vows; na vedah na acarah — has neither study of the Vedas nor good conduct; nishiddha-adi-uparatih bata kva nu — alas, where is the abstention from forbidden and other deeds! — that is, who has not even abstained from sinful deeds; [tadrishah] janah — such a man; puma-arthanam maulim param — the crown of the goals of man, the highest *prema*; muda — with a joyful heart; lunthati — plunders.

**110.** There is no yoga, no meditation, no *japa*, no austerity, no renunciation, no vows, no Vedas, no piety — what to speak of abstaining from the forbidden! — and yet, when Chaitanya, whose heart is the very essence of compassion, descends, people here joyfully and unexpectedly carry off the highest crown of the goals of man.
