>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *At the Gaura-avatara [even] those devoid of sadhana and given to forbidden conduct [obtain] […] and the bliss of prema.*

>> When the supremely merciful Sri Chaitanyadeva suddenly descended into this world, even a man who had neither yoga, nor meditation, nor *japa*, nor austerity, nor renunciation, nor vows, nor study of the Vedas, nor good conduct — alas, who had not even given up sinful and other deeds — even such a man in great joy plunders the highest *prema*, the crown of the goals of human life!

### Verse 111

महाकर्मस्रोतोनिपतितमपि स्थैर्यमयते
महापाषाणेभ्योऽप्यतिकठिनमेति द्रवदशाम् ।
नटत्यूर्ध्वं निःसाधनमपि महायोगमनसां
भुवि श्रीचैतन्येऽवतरति मनश्चित्रविभवे ॥

*mahākarmasrotonipatitamapi sthairyamayate*
*mahāpāṣāṇebhyo'pyatikaṭhinameti dravadaśām |*
*naṭatyūrdhvaṁ niḥsādhanamapi mahāyogamanasāṁ*
*bhuvi śrīcaitanye'vatarati manaścitravibhave ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* citra-vibhave — (*citra* — wondrous, *vibhava* — splendour; the locative in the sense of state; endowed with wondrous splendour); shri-caitanye bhuvi avatarati [sati] — when Sri Chaitanyadeva descended to earth; [karminam — of the people of *karma*]; manah — the mind; maha-karma-srotah-nipatitam api — though fallen into the stream of great *karma*; sthairyam ayate — attains steadiness, having obtained Gaurasundara's *prema*; [evam — and]; maha-pashanebhyah api ati-kathinam api — even if harder than huge rocks; [manah] drava-dasham eti — the mind comes to a state of fluidity through the *rasa* of *bhakti*; maha-yoga-manasam api — even of those whose mind, that is, consciousness, is directed to the *sadhana* of the great yoga and the like; [manah — the mind]; nihsadhanam — turning away from yoga and the like; urdhvam natati — dances high, that is, leaving the *sadhana* of yoga and the like, connected with the senses, tastes the bliss of *prema* in the kingdom of the spiritual pastimes of Adhokshaja.

**111.** When Sri Chaitanya, wondrous in His glory, descends on earth, the mind that has fallen into the torrent of great *karma* becomes steady; the mind harder than great rocks becomes melted; even the mind of great yogis, giving up its *sadhana*, dances on high.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *The glory of the Gaura-avatara, which melts even the hard hearts of the smartas and yogis immersed in karma.*

>> When Sri Chaitanyadeva, possessing wondrous splendour, descended to earth, the mind of the people of *karma*, though fallen into the stream of great *karma*, became steady, having obtained Gaura's [love], and even the mind harder than a huge rock melted in the *rasa* of *bhakti*. Even the mind of those whose consciousness was directed to the *sadhana* of the great yoga and the like, turning away from the *sadhana* of yoga and the like (connected with the senses), dances high, that is, tastes *prema* in the kingdom of the spiritual pastimes of Adhokshaja.

### Verse 112

स्त्रीपुत्रादिकथां जहुर्विषयिणः शास्त्रप्रवादं बुधा
योगीन्द्रा विजहुर्मरुन्नियमजक्लेशं तपस्तापसाः ।
ज्ञानाभ्यासविधिं जहुश्च यतयश्चैतन्यचन्द्रे परा-
माविष्कुर्वति भक्तियोगपदवीं नैवान्य आसीद्रसः ॥

*strīputrādikathāṁ jahurviṣayiṇaḥ śāstrapravādaṁ budhā*
*yogīndrā vijahurmarunniyamajakleśaṁ tapastāpasāḥ |*
*jñānābhyāsavidhiṁ jahuśca yatayaścaitanyacandre parā-*
*māviṣkurvati bhaktiyogapadavīṁ naivānya āsīdrasaḥ ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* caitanya-candre param bhakti-yoga-padavim avishkurvati [sati] — when Sri Chaitanya-chandra revealed the highest path of *bhakti-yoga*, that is, the path of *raganuga* worship; vishayinah — people immersed in the taste of material objects; stri-putra-adi-katham — worldly talk about the maintenance of wives, sons and the rest; jahuh — gave up; budhah — the *panditas*, that is, philosophers, experts in poetics (*alankarikas*), logicians (*naiyayikas*) and the rest; shastra-pravadam — disputes and quarrels about the *shastras*; [jahuh — gave up]; yogindrah — the best of yogis; marut-niyamaja-klesham — (*marut* — the air, *niyama* — subjugation, *ja-klesha* — the hardships born of it; that is, the hardships of *sadhana* in holding the inhalation, exhalation and the rest of the life-air by *pranayama*, *kumbhaka* and so on); vijahuh — entirely gave up; tapasah — the ascetics; tapah — austerity; [jahuh — gave up]; yatayah ca — and the *yatis*, that is, sannyasis, adherents of the knowledge of the undifferentiated; jnana-abhyasa-vidhim — the search for the undifferentiated Brahman; jahuh — gave up; [tada — then]; anyah rasah na eva asit — there was no *rasa* other than *bhakti-rasa* (that is, neither the worldly *rasa* of those attached to objects, nor the worldly *rasa* of the *shastras* of the *panditas* — the *alankarikas*, philosophers and the rest — nor the *rasa* of Paramatma of the yogis, nor the *rasa* of the impersonal Brahman of the seekers of undifferentiated knowledge — none seemed "*rasa*" beside the *rasa* of *advaya-jnana* (the non-dual Absolute Truth), for beside the wondrous *bhakti-rasa* produced by the union of the four ingredients — *rati*, that is, the inclination connected with Bhagavan, or the *sthayi-bhava*, together with the *vibhava*, *anubhava*, *sattvika[-bhavas]* and *vyabhichari[-bhavas]* — a worldly or partial *rasa* cannot be called "*rasa*" at all).

**112.** When Chaitanya-chandra revealed the supreme path of *bhakti-yoga*, the worldly gave up talk of wife, sons and so on, the learned their disputes about the shastras, the lords of yogis the torments of breath control, the ascetics their austerity, the *sannyasis* their study of knowledge. No other *rasa* remained.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *At the Gaura-avatara, people attached to worldly things, people of karma, jnanis and yogis, each leaving his own dharma, became intoxicated with the rasa of prema.*

>> When Sri Chaitanya-chandra revealed the path of the highest *bhakti-yoga*, people immersed in the taste of material objects gave up talk of wives, sons and the like; the *panditas* gave up disputes and quarrels about the *shastras*; the best of yogis entirely abandoned the torments of *sadhana* for the restraint of the life-air; the ascetics gave up their austerities; the [sannyasis] gave up the search for the undifferentiated Brahman; and then no "*rasa*" other than *bhakti-rasa* was to be seen in the world.

### Verse 113

अभूद्गेहे गेहे तुमुलहरिसङ्कीर्तनरवो
बभौ देहे देहे विपुलपुलकाश्रुव्यतिकरः ।
अपि स्नेहे स्नेहे परममधुरोत्कर्षपदवी
दवीयस्याम्नायादपि जगति गौरेऽवतरति ॥

*abhūdgehe gehe tumulaharisaṅkīrtanaravo*
*babhau dehe dehe vipulapulakāśruvyatikaraḥ |*
*api snehe snehe paramamadhurotkarṣapadavī*
*davīyasyāmnāyādapi jagati gaure'vatarati ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* jagati gaure avatarati [sati] — when Gaurasundara descended into the world; gehe gehe — in every house; tumula-hari-sankirtana-ravah — the noise of a tumultuous Hari-*sankirtana*; abhut — rose; dehe dehe — in every body; vipula-pulaka-ashru-vyatikarah — (*vipula* — abundant, that is, full; thrills, tears and the other *sattvika* transformations; *vyatikara* — multitude); babhau — shone; snehe snehe — (matured *prema* is *sneha*; with the growing intensification of the density of *prema-bhakti*); amnayat api — (*amnaya* — the *shrutis*, the Vedas; even from them); daviyasi — remote, that is, inaccessible; parama-madhura-utkarsha-padavi api — even the highest, sweet, best stage; [prakatabhuta — was revealed].

**113.** When Gaura descends into the world, in every house thunders the *sankirtana* of Hari, in every body shines a profusion of thrills and tears, and in every affection there is a state of supreme sweet excellence, far beyond even the Vedas.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *At the Gaura-avatara, the preaching of the elevated ujjvala-rasa, hidden in the Vedas, and the attainment of prema by all people.*

>> When Sri Gaurachandra descended into the world, in every house rose the noise of a tumultuous Hari-*sankirtana*, in every body shone abundant thrills, tears and the other [*sattvika-bhavas*], and with the growing intensification of the density of *prema-bhakti* even the highest, sweetest, best stage, inaccessible to the *shrutis*, was revealed.

### Verse 114

अकस्मादेवैतद्भुवनमभितः प्लावितमभू-
न्महाप्रेमाम्भोधेः किमपि रसवन्याभिरखिलम् ।
अकस्माच्चादृष्टाश्रुतचरविकारैरलमभू-
च्चमत्कारः कृष्णे कनकरुचिराङ्गेऽवतरति ॥

*akasmādevaitadbhuvanamabhitaḥ plāvitamabhū-*
*nmahāpremāmbhodheḥ kimapi rasavanyābhirakhilam |*
*akasmāccādṛṣṭāśrutacaravikārairalamabhū-*
*ccamatkāraḥ kṛṣṇe kanakarucirāṅge'vatarati ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* kanaka-rucira-ange krishne avatarati [sati] — when Bhagavan Sri Krishna Himself, the attractor of all hearts, descended, assuming a charming golden lustre; maha-prema-ambhodheh — of the ocean of great *prema*; kim api rasa-vanyabhih — by certain floods of *rasa*; akhilam etat bhuvanam — this whole world; abhitah — on all sides; akasmat eva plavitam abhut — was suddenly flooded; akasmat ca adrishta-ashruta-cara-vikaraih — and suddenly by transformations of *prema* never seen or heard of before; alam — extremely; camatkarah abhut — was astonished.

**114.** Unexpectedly this whole world was flooded on every side by indescribable floods of *rasa* from the ocean of great love; unexpectedly people were overcome with wonder at transformations never seen or heard of before — when Krishna descends in a body beautiful with golden radiance.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *The glory of the Chaitanya-avatara, which flooded the world with a deluge of the rasa of prema.*

>> When Bhagavan Sri Krishna Himself, the attractor of all hearts, descended, assuming a charming golden lustre, this whole world was suddenly and entirely flooded by the deluge of *rasa* from the ocean of great *prema* and came to the greatest astonishment at transformations of *prema* never seen or heard of before.

### Verse 115

उद्गृह्णन्ति समस्तशास्त्रमभितो दुर्वारगर्वायिताः
धन्यम्मन्यधियश्च कर्मतपसाद्युच्चावचेषु स्थिताः ।
द्वित्राण्येव जपन्ति केचन हरेर्नामानि वामाशयाः
पूर्वं सम्प्रति गौरचन्द्र उदिते प्रेमापि साधारणः ॥

*udgṛhṇanti samastaśāstramabhito durvāragarvāyitāḥ*
*dhanyammanyadhiyaśca karmatapasādyuccāvaceṣu sthitāḥ |*
*dvitrāṇyeva japanti kecana harernāmāni vāmāśayāḥ*
*pūrvaṁ samprati gauracandra udite premāpi sādhāraṇaḥ ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* [kecit janah — some people]; durvara-garvayitah — full of irrepressible pride; samasta-shastram — all the *shastras*; abhitah — from every side; udgrihnanti — collected, or else prided themselves: "We have studied all the *shastras*; who is above us?"; dhanyam-manya-dhiyah ca — and those who considered themselves blessed; karma-tapasa-adi-uccavaceshu sthitah — standing on the higher and lower rungs of the paths of the regular and occasional rites enjoined by the *smritis*, of austerity, Sankhya, yoga and the like; kecana — some [of them]; dvi-trani eva — only two or three times; hareh namani — the names of Hari; japanti — chanted; [tatrapi te — but even then they]; vama-ashayah — with crooked hearts, that is, devoted to the path of *karma*; [asan — were]; [evam — so]; purvam [asit] — it was before; samprati — now; gauracandre udite [sati] — when Gaurachandra has descended; prema api sadharanah [abhut] — *prema* too has become common, that is, all, from the highest to the lowest, have obtained it.

**115.** Formerly some, unrestrainably proud, seized upon all the shastras everywhere; others, thinking themselves fortunate, held to rituals, austerity and other rules high and low; and some, with perverse intent, chanted two or three names of Hari. But now that Gaurachandra has risen, even *prema* has become common.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *At the Gaura-avatara even those who think themselves learned, [the people of karma] and the ascetics obtain prema.*

>> Some people, full of irrepressible pride, collected [the knowledge of] all the *shastras* from every side, that is, thought: "I know all the *shastras*; there is no one better than I." Others considered themselves blessed. Of these people, who thought they had attained the goal and stood on the higher and lower rungs of the paths of the regular and occasional rites enjoined by the *smritis*, and of austerity, Sankhya, yoga and the like, some only two or three times chanted the names of Hari, yet their hearts remained full of duplicity. So it was before; but now, when Gaurachandra has descended, *prema* too has become common property, that is, all are endowed with it, from the highest to the lowest.

### Verse 116

देवे चैतन्यनामन्यवतरति सुरप्रार्थ्यपादाब्जसेवे
विष्वद्रीचीः प्रविस्तारयति सुमधुरप्रेमपीयूषवीचीः ।
को बालः कश्च वृद्धः क इह जडमतिः का वधूः को वराकः
सर्वेषामैकरस्यं किमपि हरिपदे भक्तिभाजां बभूव ॥

*deve caitanyanāmanyavatarati suraprārthyapādābjaseve*
*viṣvadrīcīḥ pravistārayati sumadhurapremapīyūṣavīcīḥ |*
*ko bālaḥ kaśca vṛddhaḥ ka iha jaḍamatiḥ kā vadhūḥ ko varākaḥ*
*sarveṣāmaikarasyaṁ kimapi haripade bhaktibhājāṁ babhūva ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* sura-prarthya-pada-abja-seve — He service to whose lotus feet the gods long for; caitanya-namani — bearing the name Chaitanya; deve — (the Purusha of pastimes, or He who, accepting Radhika's mood, wishes to taste His own sweetness); avatarati [sati] — when He descended; vishvadricih — all-pervading; sumadhura-prema-piyusha-vicih — the sweetest waves of the nectar of *prema*; pravistarayati [sati] — when He spread far and wide; kah balah kah ca vriddhah — whether child or old man; kah jadamatih ka vadhuh — whether dullard or woman; kah varakah — whether a wretched low person; iha — in this world (*samsara*); sarvesham bhakti-bhajam — in all those endowed with *bhakti*; hari-pade — for the feet of Sri Hari; kim api aikarasyam babhuva — a certain unprecedented *rasa* arose, that is, in all, for the feet of Sri Hari, one unprecedented *rasa* of *advaya-jnana* (the non-dual Absolute Truth) awoke.

**116.** When the Lord named Chaitanya descended, service to whose lotus feet the gods pray for, and spread through the universe the sweetest waves of the nectar of love — who is a child, who an old man, who a dullard, who a woman, who a wretch? In all who are devoted to Hari's feet there arose an indescribable oneness of taste.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *At the Gaura-avatara, children, the old, […], the blind, the deaf and others become intoxicated with the rasa of prema.*

>> When Sri Chaitanyadeva, the Purusha of pastimes, service to whose lotus feet the demigods long for, descended into this world and spread far and wide the universal, sweetest wave of the nectar of *prema* — whether child or old man, woman or dull-witted, or a wretched low person — all in this world gained the capacity to obtain *bhakti*, and in each awoke for the feet of Sri Hari a certain unprecedented, wondrous *rasa* of *advaya-jnana* (the non-dual Absolute Truth).

### Verse 117

सर्वे शङ्करनारदादय इहायाताः स्वयं श्रीरपि
प्राप्ता देवहलायुधोऽपि मिलितो जाताश्च ते वृष्णयः ।
भूयः किं व्रजवासिनोऽपि प्रकटा गोपालगोप्यादयः
पूर्णे प्रेमरसेश्वरेऽवतरति श्रीगौरचन्द्रे भुवि ॥

*sarve śaṅkaranāradādaya ihāyātāḥ svayaṁ śrīrapi*
*prāptā devahalāyudho'pi milito jātāśca te vṛṣṇayaḥ |*
*bhūyaḥ kiṁ vrajavāsino'pi prakaṭā gopālagopyādayaḥ*
*pūrṇe premaraseśvare'vatarati śrīgauracandre bhuvi ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* purne prema-rasa-ishvare shri-gauracandre — when Bhagavan Gaurachandra Himself, the crown of the connoisseurs of the *rasa* of *prema*; bhuvi — to earth; avatarati [sati] — descended; sarve shankara-narada-adayah — all — Shankara, Narada and the others (in the form of Advaita, Srivasa and others); iha — into this world (*prapanca*); ayatah — came; svayam shrih api — Lakshmi Herself (in the form of Sri Lakshmipriya and Vishnupriya); prapta — appeared; deva-halayudhah api — and Baladeva, His manifest form, non-different from Bhagavan Himself; militah — was present (in the form of Nityananda); te vrishnayah — the Yadavas (in the form of Shachi, Jagannatha Mishra and others); jatah — descended; kim bhuyah — what more is to be said; vraja-vasinah gopala-gopi-adayah api — and the residents of Vraja — Nanda and the rest, the servants Raktaka, Citraka and the rest, the friends headed by Subala, the *gopis* of Vraja (the words "and the rest" also mean Yogamaya and the other *shaktis*) — all; prakatah — appeared, that is, all the eternally perfect devotees of Krishna's pastimes appeared in Gaura's pastimes.

**117.** All have come here — Shankara, Narada and the others; Sri (Lakshmi) herself has appeared; the god Halayudha (Balarama) has joined Him; those very Vrishnis have been born; and more — the dwellers of Vraja too have appeared: the cowherd boys, the gopis and the others — when Sri Gaurachandra, the Lord of the *rasa* of complete love, descended to earth.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *At the Gaura-avatara, the appearance of […] the demigods and the eternal associates of Bhagavan.*

>> When Bhagavan Gaurachandra Himself, the crown of the connoisseurs of the *rasa* of *prema*, descended to earth, all came — Shankara, Narada and the others (in the form of the devotees Advaita, Srivasa and the rest). Lakshmi Herself appeared (as Sri Lakshmipriya and Sri Vishnupriya). Baladeva — His manifest form, non-different from Bhagavan Himself — was present (in the form of Nityananda Raya, the crusher of the godless). The Yadavas too appeared (in Shachi, Jagannatha and others); what more is there to say — Nanda and the other residents of Vraja, the servants Raktaka, Citraka and the rest, the friends headed by Subala and the *shaktis* headed by the *gopis* — all the eternally perfect associates of Krishna's pastimes descended into Gaura's pastimes.

### Verse 118

भृत्याः स्निग्धा अतिसुमधुरप्रोज्ज्वलोदारभाज-
स्तत्पादाब्जद्वितयसविधे सर्व एवावतीर्णाः ।
प्रापुः पूर्वाधिकतरमहाप्रेमपीयूषलक्ष्मीं
स्वप्रेमाणं वितरति जगत्यद्भुतं हेमगौरे ॥

*bhṛtyāḥ snigdhā atisumadhuraprojjvalodārabhāja-*
*statpādābjadvitayasavidhe sarva evāvatīrṇāḥ |*
*prāpuḥ pūrvādhikataramahāpremapīyūṣalakṣmīṁ*
*svapremāṇaṁ vitarati jagatyadbhutaṁ hemagaure ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* hema-gaure — Sri Gaurasundara, shining like molten gold; adbhutam — unearthly; sva-premanam — His own *prema*; jagati — on earth; vitarati [sati] — when He was distributing; bhrityah — the servants; snigdhah — the friends; ati-sumadhura-projjvala-udara-bhajah — (*ati-sumadhura* — sweetness free from the idea of *aishvarya*; *projjvala* — the elevated *ujjvala*; *udara* — charming; that is, also the beloveds who serve exclusively the sweet, elevated, charming *ujjvala-rasa*); sarve eva — all; tat-pada-abja-dvitaya-savidhe — near His two lotus feet; avatirnah [santah] — having descended; purva-adhikatara-maha-prema-piyusha-lakshmim — a wealth of the nectar of great *prema* greater than [the tasting of *prema*] in Krishna's pastimes; prapuh — obtained.

**118.** The servants, the friends, and those who partake of the sweetest, radiant and generous [*rasa*] — all of them descended beside His two lotus feet. When the golden Gaura distributes His wondrous love to the world, they obtained a wealth of the nectar of great love greater than before.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *At the Gaura-avatara the eternal associates obtain a greater bliss of prema than before.*

>> When Sri Gaurasundara, shining like molten gold, was distributing His unearthly *prema* on earth, the servants, the friends and the beloveds — the eternally perfect maidservants of *madhura-rasa* alone — all of them, having descended to His lotus feet, obtained a wealth of the nectar of great *prema* surpassing their former (in Krishna's pastimes) tasting of *prema*.

### Verse 119

हसन्त्युच्चैरुच्चैरहह कुलवध्वोऽपि परितो
द्रवीभावं गच्छन्त्यपि कुविषयग्रावघटिताः ।
तिरस्कुर्वन्त्यज्ञा अपि सकलशास्त्रज्ञसमितिं
क्षितौ श्रीचैतन्येऽद्भुतमहिमसारेऽवतरति ॥

*hasantyuccairuccairahaha kulavadhvo'pi parito*
*dravībhāvaṁ gacchantyapi kuviṣayagrāvaghaṭitāḥ |*
*tiraskurvantyajñā api sakalaśāstrajñasamitiṁ*
*kṣitau śrīcaitanye'dbhutamahimasāre'vatarati ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* adbhuta-mahima-sare — supremely unearthly, endowed with the highest greatness; shri-caitanye — Sri Chaitanya; kshitau — to earth; avatarati [sati] — when He descended; ahaha — ah!; kula-vadhvah api — even noble married women; uccaih uccaih hasanti — laugh very loudly; ku-vishaya-grava-ghatitah api — (*ku-vishaya* — evil objects; *grava* — stone; *ghatita* — hearts made of it; that is, hard hearts made of the stones of evil objects of enjoyment); paritah — wholly; dravibhavam gacchanti — melt; ajnah api — even the ignorant; sakala-shastrajna-samitim — the society of the knowers of all the *shastras*; tiraskurvanti — put to shame.

**119.** When Sri Chaitanya, the essence of wondrous glory, descended to earth — ah! — even noble housewives all around laugh loudly, loudly; even hearts made of the stones of base sense attachments melt; even the ignorant put to shame the assembly of those who know all the shastras.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Description of the essence of the glory of the Gaura-avatara through the attainment of prema even by the hard-hearted, those given to evil sense objects, and the ignorant.*

>> When the supremely unearthly Sri Chaitanya, endowed with the greatest glory, descended to earth, even noble married women (leaving shame, in love for Krishna) laugh aloud; even hearts hard as the stone of evil objects serving sense gratification melt completely; even the ignorant, devoid of knowledge of the truth (having obtained knowledge of the truth by Chaitanya's mercy), put to shame the whole society of the knowers of the *shastras* (that is, censure the *shastric* knowledge of those who think themselves *panditas*, skilled in the lower knowledge).

### Verse 120

प्रायश्चैतन्यमासीदपि सकलविदां नेह पूर्वं यदेषां
खर्वा सर्वार्थसारेऽप्यकृत नहि पदं कुण्ठिता बुद्धिवृत्तिः ।
गम्भीरोदारभावोज्ज्वलरसमधुरप्रेमभक्तिप्रवेशः
केषां नासीदिदानीं जगति करुणया गौरचन्द्रेऽवतीर्णे ॥

*prāyaścaitanyamāsīdapi sakalavidāṁ neha pūrvaṁ yadeṣāṁ*
*kharvā sarvārthasāre'pyakṛta nahi padaṁ kuṇṭhitā buddhivṛttiḥ |*
*gambhīrodārabhāvojjvalarasamadhurapremabhaktipraveśaḥ*
*keṣāṁ nāsīdidānīṁ jagati karuṇayā gauracandre'vatīrṇe ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* iha — in this world (*prapanca*); purvam — before Chaitanya's appearance; sakala-vidam api — even in the *panditas*, the knowers of all the *shastras*; prayah caitanyam — (the function of consciousness, service to Krishna, is "*caitanya*"); na asit — was almost absent; yat — since; esham — their; kharva kunthita [ca] — extremely petty and inclined to doubt; buddhi-vrittih — the function of the intelligence; sarva-artha-sare api — (*sarva* — all, *artha* — the goals of man; the essence of the four goals is the fifth goal, *krishna-prema*) even in it; padam — (an object, that is, an inclination); na hi akrita — did not have; [kintu] idanim — but now; gauracandre — Gaurachandra; karunaya — out of mercy; jagati — into the world; avatirne [sati] — having descended; kesham — in whom; gambhira-udara-bhava-ujjvala-rasa-madhura-prema-bhakti-praveshah — (*gambhira* — inscrutable; *udara* — extremely wondrous; *bhava* — *vibhava* and *anubhava*; *ujjvala-rasa* — *shringara-rasa*; *madhura-prema-bhakti*; entry into it; that is, entry into the inscrutable *prema-bhakti*, full of the elevated *ujjvala-madhura-rasa* and nourished by the most wondrous ingredients — *vibhava*, *anubhava* and the rest); na asit — was there not?

**120.** Formerly even the all-knowing here had hardly any consciousness (*chaitanya*): their meagre, dull reasoning never set foot on the essence of all goals. But now, when out of mercy Gaurachandra has descended into the world — who has not entered into the *bhakti* of sweet love, the radiant *rasa* of deep and generous feelings?

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Before the Chaitanya-avatara people in general did not obtain the elevated ujjvala-rasa.*

>> Before Chaitanya's appearance in this world, even in the knowers of all the *shastras* who thought themselves *panditas*, the function of consciousness — service to Krishna — was almost covered. They did not aspire to *krishna-prema*, the crown of all the goals of human life, since their intelligence was extremely petty and inclined to doubt. But now that Sri Gaurachandra has mercifully descended into the world, who has not entered the inconceivable *prema-bhakti*, full of the elevated *ujjvala-madhura-rasa* and nourished by the most wondrous ingredients — *vibhava*, *anubhava* and the rest!

### Verse 121

श्रीमद्भागवतस्य यत्र परमं तात्पर्यमुट्टङ्कितं
श्रीवैयासकिना दुरन्वयतया रासप्रसङ्गेऽपि यत् ।
यद्राधारतिकेलिनागररसास्वादैकसद्भाजनं
तद्वस्तुप्रथनाय गौरवपुषा लोकेऽवतीर्णो हरिः ॥

*śrīmadbhāgavatasya yatra paramaṁ tātparyamuṭṭaṅkitaṁ*
*śrīvaiyāsakinā duranvayatayā rāsaprasaṅge'pi yat |*
*yadrādhāratikelināgararasāsvādaikasadbhājanaṁ*
*tadvastuprathanāya gauravapuṣā loke'vatīrṇo hariḥ ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* shri-vaiyasakina api — even by the son of Vyasa, Shukadeva; yatra rasa-prasange — in the account of the *rasa-lila*; shrimat-bhagavatasya — of the *Srimad-Bhagavatam*; yat paramam tatparyam — that highest meaning (*prema*, the highest meaning of the *rasa* of the pastimes of Sri Radha and Krishna); duranvayataya — because of the difficulty of comprehension, that is, because there was no vessel able to taste the *rasa* of these pastimes and know their truth; uttankitam — was only indicated, not described in detail; yat [ca] — and that which; radha-rati-keli-nagara-rasa-asvada-eka-sat-bhajanam — (the *rati-keli* of Srimati Radhika — the love-sports in the *nikunja*; *nagara* — Sri Krishna, the crown of the supreme connoisseurs of *rasa*; the one best vessel for tasting the sweetness of His *rasa* and other pastimes); tat-vastu-prathanaya — to spread these two things; harih — Sri Krishna; gaura-vapusha — in the body of Gaura; loke — into this world; avatirnah — descended.

**121.** The supreme purport of the *Srimad Bhagavatam*, which Sri Vaiyasaki (Shuka), even in the account of the *rasa* dance, only indicated without revealing it, because it is hard to grasp; that which is the one true vessel for tasting the *rasa* of the hero (*nagara*) of Radha's love-sports — to spread that treasure, Hari descended into the world in the form of Gaura.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *The Gaura-avatara for the sake of revealing the meaning of the Srimad-Bhagavatam and the rasa of Radha.*

>> Even the son of Vyasa, Srila Shukadeva, in the account of the *rasa-lila* only touched on the secret meaning of the *Srimad-Bhagavatam* without describing it in detail, for it cannot be grasped by the study of the *shastras* alone. To reveal two things — this meaning of the *Srimad-Bhagavatam* and the one best vessel for tasting the sweetness of the *rasa-lila* and the other pastimes of Sri Krishna, the crown of the supreme connoisseurs of *rasa*, in the love-sports of the *nikunja* — Sri Krishna descended into this world in the body of Gaura.

### Verse 122

केचिद्दास्यमवापुरुद्धवमुखाः श्लाघ्यं परे लेभिरे
श्रीदामादिपदं व्रजाम्बुजदृशां भावञ्च भेजुः परे ।
अन्ये धन्यतमा धयन्ति सुधियो राधापदाम्भोरुहं
श्रीचैतन्यमहाप्रभोः करुणया लोकस्य काः सम्पदः ॥

*keciddāsyamavāpuruddhavamukhāḥ ślāghyaṁ pare lebhire*
*śrīdāmādipadaṁ vrajāmbujadṛśāṁ bhāvañca bhejuḥ pare |*
*anye dhanyatamā dhayanti sudhiyo rādhāpadāmbhoruhaṁ*
*śrīcaitanyamahāprabhoḥ karuṇayā lokasya kāḥ sampadaḥ ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* shri-caitanya-mahaprabhoh — of Sri Krishna-Chaitanya Mahaprabhu; karunaya — by the mercy; lokasya kah sampadah — what riches did people [not obtain]?; uddhava-mukhah kecit dasyam avapuh — of the devotees headed by Uddhava, some obtained the mood of service, like that of Raktaka, Patraka and others; pare — others; shlaghyam shridama-adi-padam lebhire — obtained the praiseworthy position of the friends Shridama and the rest; pare ca vraja-ambuja-drisham bhavam bhejuh — and others obtained the mood of the *gopis* of Vraja; anye dhanyatamah sudhiyah — still others, the most blessed and wise; radha-pada-ambhoruham — the sweetness of the lotus feet of Sri Radha; dhayanti — taste.

**122.** Some — Uddhava and the others — attained service; others attained the praiseworthy state of Shridama and the other friends; still others attained the mood of the lotus-eyed maidens of Vraja; and others, most fortunate and wise, drink [the nectar of] Radha's lotus feet. What riches has the world not received by the mercy of Sri Chaitanya Mahaprabhu!

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *At the Gaura-avatara all obtain the wealth of prema in dasya, sakhya and the other [rasas], and [the most chosen] service to Radha.*

>> What riches did people not obtain by the mercy of Sri Chaitanya Mahaprabhu? Of the devotees headed by Uddhava (all the devotees of Krishna's pastimes headed by Uddhava descended into Gaura's pastimes), some obtained the Vraja mood of servants, like that of Raktaka, Patraka and others; others the praiseworthy position of confidential friends, like that of Shridama and others; others again obtained the mood of the *gopis* of Vraja; and still others, the most blessed and wise, taste [the sweetness of] the lotus feet of Sri Radha.

### Verse 123

सर्वज्ञैर्मुनिपुङ्गवैः प्रवितते तत्तन्मते युक्तिभिः
पूर्वं नैकतरत्र कोऽपि सुदृढं विश्वस्त आसीज्जनः ।
सम्प्रत्यप्रतिमप्रभाव उदिते गौराङ्गचन्द्रे पुनः
श्रुत्यर्थो हरिभक्तिरेव परमः कैर्वा न निर्धार्यते ॥

*sarvajñairmunipuṅgavaiḥ pravitate tattanmate yuktibhiḥ*
*pūrvaṁ naikataratra ko'pi sudṛḍhaṁ viśvasta āsījjanaḥ |*
*sampratyapratimaprabhāva udite gaurāṅgacandre punaḥ*
*śrutyartho haribhaktireva paramaḥ kairvā na nirdhāryate ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* sarvajnaih — by the omniscient; muni-pungavaih — best of sages; tat-tat-mate — their own doctrines; yuktibhih — by arguments and reasonings; pravitate [sati] — when they were widely spread; purvam — formerly; kah janah api — no person at all; ekataratra — in a one-sided doctrine; sudridham vishvastah — firmly convinced; na asit — was; samprati — now; apratima-prabhave gauranga-candre — when Sri Gaurachandra, incomparable in power; udite [sati] — has risen; punah — again; paramah shruti-arthah — the highest goal established by all the *shrutis*; hari-bhaktih eva — *hari-bhakti* alone; kaih va — by whom; na nirdharyate — has it not been determined?; [that is, although the mutually contradictory doctrines of the sages, born of speculation, were established by casuistic arguments and the like, their very authors doubted their doctrines; but the eternal religion of *bhakti* preached by the Eternal Purusha, Bhagavan Sri Gaurasundara Himself, is the real truth established by the *shrutis*, free from all deceit].

**123.** Formerly the all-knowing best of sages spread their various doctrines by arguments, and not one person was firmly convinced of any of them. But now, when Gauranga-chandra of matchless power has risen — who has not again become certain that the highest meaning of the *shruti* is devotion to Hari alone?

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *The difference between the doctrines born of speculation and preached by the sages, and the religion of bhakti preached [by Gaura] — the real truth; the prema-bhakti preached by Gaura is the highest goal established by the Vedas.*

>> Although the omniscient best of sages expounded their doctrines [on the basis of] arguments, formerly no one was firmly convinced by these partial arguments. But now that Sri Gaurachandra, incomparable in power, has risen, who has not again become convinced that *hari-bhakti* alone is the highest goal established by the Vedas?

### Verse 124

विश्वं महाप्रणयसाधुसुधारसैक-
पाथोनिधौ सकलमेव निमज्जयन्तम् ।
गौराङ्गचन्द्रनखचन्द्रमणिच्छटायाः
कञ्चिद्विचित्रमनुभावमहं स्मरामि ॥

*viśvaṁ mahāpraṇayasādhusudhārasaika-*
*pāthonidhau sakalameva nimajjayantam |*
*gaurāṅgacandranakhacandramaṇicchaṭāyāḥ*
*kañcidvicitramanubhāvamahaṁ smarāmi ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* gauranga-candra-nakha-candra-mani-cchatayah — of the radiance of the moonstones, the toenails of Gaurangachandra; maha-pranaya-sadhu-sudha-rasa-eka-pathonidhau — (as *prema* matures, *sneha*, *mana* and *pranaya* arise; with the growth of *pranaya*, *raga*, *anuraga*, *bhava* and [*mahabhava*] gradually arise; therefore "*mahapranaya*" is *prema* in the form of *mahabhava*; *sadhu* — excellent; *sudha-rasa* — the nectar *rasa*; *eka* — principal; *pathonidhi* — ocean; that is, in the excellent nectar ocean of *mahapranaya*); sakalam eva vishvam — the whole world; nimajjayantam — immersing; kancit — indescribable; vicitram — wondrous; anubhavam — power; aham smarami — I remember.

**124.** I remember a certain wondrous power of the radiance of the moonstones — the toenails of Gauranga-chandra — which immerses the whole world in the matchless ocean of the excellent nectar *rasa* of great love.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *The glory of Gaurahari, the ocean of the nectar of the rasa of prema, who immerses the whole world [in the ocean of prema].*

>> I remember the indescribable, wondrous power of the radiance of the moonstones of Gaurachandra's toenails, which immerses the whole world in the supreme nectar ocean of *mahapranaya*.

### Verse 125

अतिपुण्यैरतिसुकृतैः कृतार्थीकृतः कोऽपि पूर्वैः ।
एवं कैरपि न कृतं यत्प्रेमाब्धौ निमज्जितं विश्वम् ॥

*atipuṇyairatisukṛtaiḥ kṛtārthīkṛtaḥ ko'pi pūrvaiḥ |*
*evaṁ kairapi na kṛtaṁ yatpremābdhau nimajjitaṁ viśvam ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* ati-punyaih — by those extremely pious, that is, strictly following good conduct; ati-sukritaih — possessing special *sukriti*, that is, the highest righteousness; purvaih — by such ancient great personalities; kah api [janah] — some people; kritarthi-kritah — were brought to the goal, that is, reached Vaikuntha and other worlds; [caitanya-candrena — by Chaitanya-chandra]; yat vishvam — in the way the world; prema-abdhau — in the ocean of *prema*; nimajjitam — has been immersed; evam — so; [prak — before]; kaih api na kritam — was done by no one.

**125.** The [great ones] of old, full of the highest merit and piety, brought perhaps one person or another to fulfilment. But to immerse the whole world in the ocean of love — this no one did.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Sri Gaurahari is the only one who has flooded the world with a deluge of prema.*

>> Through the ancient great personalities, distinguished by good conduct and the highest righteousness, some people, reaching Vaikuntha and other worlds, obtained the fulfilment of their goal; but as Sri Chaitanya-chandra has immersed the whole world in the ocean of *prema*, no one did before.

### Verse 126

धर्मे निष्ठां दधदनुपमां विष्णुभक्तिं गरिष्ठां
संविभ्राणो दधदिह हि हृत्तिष्ठतीवाश्मसारम् ।
नीचो गोघ्नादपि जगदहो प्लावयत्यश्रुपूरैः
को वा जानात्यहह गहनं हेमगौराङ्गरङ्गम् ॥

*dharme niṣṭhāṁ dadhadanupamāṁ viṣṇubhaktiṁ gariṣṭhāṁ*
*saṁvibhrāṇo dadhadiha hi hṛttiṣṭhatīvāśmasāram |*
*nīco goghnādapi jagadaho plāvayatyaśrupūraiḥ*
*ko vā jānātyahaha gahanaṁ hemagaurāṅgaraṅgam ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* dharme — with regard to *dharma*; anupamam — incomparable; nishtham — firmness or faith; dadhat [api] — even having; garishtham — the highest; vishnu-bhaktim — *bhakti* to Sri Vishnu, or the inclination to serve; samvibhranah [api] — even having wholly taken shelter; ashma-saram iva — hard as iron; hrit — heart; dadhat — having; iha — in this world; tishthati — [a man] abides; [atha ca — and meanwhile, by the mercy of Sri Gaurasundara]; goghnat api nicah [janah] — a man lower even than a cow-killer, that is, a sinner; aho — ah!; ashru-puraih — with streams of tears; jagat plavayati — floods the world; ahaha — ah!; kah va — who indeed; gahanam hi hema-gauranga-rangam — the inscrutable play of the golden Sri Gaurasundara; janati — can comprehend.

**126.** Even one who has matchless fidelity to *dharma* and the weightiest devotion to Vishnu sometimes lives here with a heart as hard as iron; while one lower than a cow-killer — oh! — floods the world with streams of tears. Ah, who can fathom the deep play of the golden Gauranga?

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *The glory of the mercy of Gaurahari, who gives prema to the unworthy without waiting to be asked.*

>> Even possessing incomparable firmness in *dharma* and having wholly taken shelter of the highest *bhakti* to Vishnu, people live on earth with hearts hard as iron; (but by the mercy of Sri Gaurahari) ah! even a man more sinful than a cow-killer (wholly turning away from sinful tendencies) floods the world with streams of tears. Ah! Who can comprehend the inscrutable play of the golden Sri Gaurangasundara!

### Verse 127

क्वचित्कृष्णावेशान्नटति बहुभङ्गीमभिनयन्
क्वचिद्राधाविष्टो हरि हरि हरीत्यार्तिरुदितः ।
क्वचिद्रिङ्गन् बालः क्वचिदपि च गोपालचरितो
जगद्गौरो विस्मापयति बहुगम्भीरमहिमा ॥

*kvacitkṛṣṇāveśānnaṭati bahubhaṅgīmabhinayan*
*kvacidrādhāviṣṭo hari hari harītyārtiruditaḥ |*
*kvacidriṅgan bālaḥ kvacidapi ca gopālacarito*
*jagadgauro vismāpayati bahugambhīramahimā ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* bahu-gambhira-mahima — (*bahu* — vast; *gambhira* — unfathomable by logic or hard to penetrate; He who has such greatness, that is, power); [sah — that]; gaurah — Gaurasundara; jagat vismapayati — astonishes the world; krishna-aveshat — because of His absorption in Sri Krishna; kvacit — sometimes; balah [san] — becoming a child; ringan — crawls on His knees; kvacit api ca — and sometimes; gopala-caritah [san] — displaying the nature of a cowherd boy; kvacit — sometimes; bahu-bhangim — many poses; abhinayan — enacting; natati — dances; kvacit — sometimes; radha-avishtah [san] — seized by the mood of Sri Radha in separation from Krishna; hari hari hari iti arti-ruditah — saying "Hari! Hari! Hari!", weeps with the pain born of the suffering of separation.

**127.** Sometimes, absorbed in Krishna, He dances, enacting many postures; sometimes, absorbed in Radha, He cries out in anguish: "Hari! Hari! Hari!"; sometimes He crawls like an infant; sometimes He behaves like a cowherd boy. So the great Gaura of unfathomable glory astonishes the world.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Sri Gaurahari, who astonishes the world by the pastimes of childhood, pauganda and the rest and by manifesting the mood of Sri Radhika, immersed in the rasa of separation.*

>> Sri Gaurasundara, possessing vast and inscrutable power, astonishes the universe. Because of His absorption in Sri Krishna, He now, manifesting a child's [mood], crawls on His knees, now, displaying the nature of a cowherd boy, now, enacting many poses, dances, and now, seized by the mood of Sri Radha in separation from Krishna, weeps with the pain born of the suffering of separation: "Hari! Hari!! Hari!!!"

### Verse 128

वेलायां लवणाम्बुधेर्मधुरिमप्राग्भावसारस्फुर-
ल्लीलायां नववल्लवीरसनिधेरावेशयन्ती जगत् ।
खेलायामपि शैशवे निजरुचा विश्वैकसम्मोहिनी
मूर्तिः काचन काञ्चनद्रवमयी चित्ताय मे रोचते ॥

*velāyāṁ lavaṇāmbudhermadhurimaprāgbhāvasārasphura-*
*llīlāyāṁ navavallavīrasanidherāveśayantī jagat |*
*khelāyāmapi śaiśave nijarucā viśvaikasammohinī*
*mūrtiḥ kācana kāñcanadravamayī cittāya me rocate ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* lavana-ambudheh — of the salt ocean; velayam — on the shore; shaishave api khelayam — even in childhood games; nija-ruca — by its own radiance; vishva-eka-sammohini — the only enchantress of the universe; nava-ballavi-rasa-nidheh — ("*nava-ballavi*" — the young Sri Radhika, the best of the *gopis*; Her *rasa-nidhi*, that is, the receptacle of Her *rasa* — Sri Krishna, the crown of the connoisseurs of *rasa*); madhurima-prak-bhava-sara-sphurat-lilayam — (*madhurima* — in sweetness; *prag-bhava* — the former *bhava*, that is, that supreme wondrous *prema* which the *ashraya-vigraha* Srimati Radhika bears for the *vishaya-vigraha* Krishna; the pastime manifesting through its essence, that is, the pastime of the divine madness of the *mahabhava* of Krishna, the king of *rasa*, in the mood of Radha — in it); jagat — the world [that has taken shelter of Him]; aveshayanti — immersing; kacana kancana-drava-mayi — a certain indescribable [form] of molten gold; murtih — form; me cittaya rocate — is pleasing to my heart.

**128.** On the shore of the salt ocean immersing the world in pastimes in which shines the essence of the feeling that the young cowherd girl (Radha) has for the sweetness of Krishna, the treasury of her *rasa*; already in His childhood games, by His radiance the one enchanter of the universe — an indescribable form, as if made of molten gold, delights my heart.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Devotion to Gaurahari, who immerses the world in the elevated ujjvala-rasa.*

>> That form of Gaurahari which by the power of its own radiance, even in childhood games, is the only enchanter of the universe that has taken shelter of Him, and which immerses this world [that has taken] shelter in the pastime manifesting through the essence of the former *bhava* of the sweetness of Sri Krishna, the crown of the connoisseurs of *rasa*, the receptacle of the *rasa* of Sri Varshabhanavi, the best of the young [cowherd girls] (that is, through the essence of the supreme wondrous *prema* of Srimati Radhika, the *ashraya-vigraha*, for Krishna, the *vishaya-vigraha*) — that is, in the pastime of the divine madness of Krishna, the king of *rasa*, full of *mahabhava*, in the mood of Radha — that wondrous form of molten gold on the shore of the salt ocean is pleasing to my heart.

### Verse 129

प्रेमा नामाद्भुतार्थः श्रवणपथगतः कस्य नाम्नां महिम्नः
को वेत्ता कस्य वृन्दावनविपिनमहामाधुरीषु प्रवेशः ।
को वा जानाति राधां परमरसचमत्कारमाधुर्यसीमा-
मेकश्चैतन्यचन्द्रः परमकरुणया सर्वमाविश्चकार ॥

*premā nāmādbhutārthaḥ śravaṇapathagataḥ kasya nāmnāṁ mahimnaḥ*
*ko vettā kasya vṛndāvanavipinamahāmādhurīṣu praveśaḥ |*
*ko vā jānāti rādhāṁ paramarasacamatkāramādhuryasīmā-*
*mekaścaitanyacandraḥ paramakaruṇayā sarvamāviścakāra ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* prema nama — called *prema*; adbhuta-arthah — the highest goal of man; [prak — formerly]; kasya — whose; shravana-patha-gatah — hearing did it reach?; kah [va] namnam mahimnah — of the glory of the names (the genitive of the object); vetta [syat] — was a knower?; vrindavana-vipina-maha-madhurishu — (into the great sweetnesses of the forests of Vrindavana — "*vipina*," that is, thickets hard to penetrate); kasya praveshah — who had entry?; ko va — who indeed; parama-rasa-camatkara-madhurya-simam radham — (Radha, in whom is the limit of the wondrous sweetness of *adhirudha-mahabhava*, that is, Srimati Radhika, in whom alone the highest stage […] of the divine madness full of unearthly service to Krishna is manifest in its fullness — as the object of worship); janati — knew?; ekah caitanya-candrah — Sri Chaitanya-chandra alone (the word "*candra*" indicates the destruction of the darkness of duplicity and […]); parama-karunaya — out of supreme generosity; sarvam avishcakara — revealed all this; [Srila Bhaktivinoda Thakura, quoting this verse in the conclusion of the fifth chapter of the book *Sri Mahaprabhu-shiksha*, wrote: "The nectarean *prema* was brought into this world by Sri Chaitanyadeva alone; no one brought it before; to show this Srila Prabodhananda Sarasvati has presented this verse"].

**129.** Who had ever before heard of the wondrous thing called *prema*? Who knew the glory of the Names? Who entered the great sweetness of the forests of Vrindavana? Who knew Radha — the limit of the sweetness and wonder of the highest *rasa*? Chaitanya-chandra alone, out of supreme mercy, revealed all this.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Sri Gaurahari is the only one who revealed ujjvala-rasa, the sweetness of Vrindavana and the glory of Radha.*

>> Who had ever heard of the highest goal of human life called "*prema*"? Who knew the glory of the holy name? Who had penetrated the impenetrable mass of the great sweetnesses of the forest of Vrindavana? Who knew (as the object of worship) Sri Varshabhanavi, the highest limit of the sweetness of the most wondrous *adhirudha-mahabhava*? Chaitanya-chandra alone, displaying the pastime of supreme generosity, revealed all this.

## The beauty and dance of Gaura

### Verse 130

पूर्णप्रेमरसामृताब्धिलहरीलोलाङ्गगौरच्छटा-
कोट्याच्छादितविश्वमीश्वरविधिव्यासादिभिः संस्तुतम् ।
दुर्लक्ष्यां श्रुतिकोटिभिः प्रकटयन्मूर्तिं जगन्मोहिनी-
माश्चर्यं लवणोदरोधसि परं ब्रह्म स्वयं नृत्यति ॥

*pūrṇapremarasāmṛtābdhilaharīlolāṅgagauracchaṭā-*
*koṭyācchāditaviśvamīśvaravidhivyāsādibhiḥ saṁstutam |*
*durlakṣyāṁ śrutikoṭibhiḥ prakaṭayanmūrtiṁ jaganmohinī-*
*māścaryaṁ lavaṇodarodhasi paraṁ brahma svayaṁ nṛtyati ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* purna-prema-rasa-amrita-abdhi-lahari-lola-anga-gaura-cchata-koti-acchadita-vishvam — (that Purusha by whom the universe is covered with millions of golden rays of His body, set trembling by the waves of the ocean of the nectar of the *rasa* of perfect *prema*); ishvara-vidhi-vyasa-adibhih samstutam — ("Ishvara" — Maheshvara, the god of gods; "Vidhi" — Brahma; and fully praised by the sages headed by Vyasa); shruti-kotibhih durlakshyam — hidden from multitudes of *shrutis*; jagat-mohinim murtim — a world-enchanting form; prakatayat — manifesting; ashcaryam param brahma — the world-astonishing Supreme Brahman, Sri Bhagavan; lavanoda-rodhasi — on the shore of the salt ocean; svayam nrityati — Himself dances.

**130.** He covers the universe with millions of reflections of the golden lustre of His body, swaying on the waves of the ocean of the nectar of the *rasa* of complete love; He is praised by the Lord (Shiva), the Creator (Brahma), Vyasa and others; revealing a form that enchants the world, barely discernible by millions of *shrutis* — oh wonder! — on the shore of the salt ocean the Supreme Brahman Himself dances.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *[…], beyond the reach of logic, […], the wondrously dancing Supreme Brahman, Sri Gaurahari.*

>> He who has covered the universe with millions of rays of His golden lustre, set trembling by the waves of the ocean of the nectar of the *rasa* of perfect *prema*, and whom the wise — Shiva, Brahma, Vyasa and the others — incessantly praise — that wondrous Supreme Brahman, Sri Gaurasundara, manifesting a world-enchanting form hidden from millions of *shrutis*, Himself dances on the shore of the salt ocean.

### Verse 131

कोऽयं पट्टधटीविराजितकटीदेशः करे कङ्कणं
हारं वक्षसि कुण्डलं श्रवणयोर्बिभ्रत्पदे नूपुरम् ।
ऊर्ध्वीकृत्य निबद्धकुन्तलभरप्रोत्फुल्लमल्लीस्रगा-
पीडः क्रीडति गौरनागरवरो नृत्यन्निजैर्नामभिः ॥

*ko'yaṁ paṭṭadhaṭīvirājitakaṭīdeśaḥ kare kaṅkaṇaṁ*
*hāraṁ vakṣasi kuṇḍalaṁ śravaṇayorbibhratpade nūpuram |*
*ūrdhvīkṛtya nibaddhakuntalabharaprotphullamallīsragā-*
*pīḍaḥ krīḍati gauranāgaravaro nṛtyannijairnāmabhiḥ ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* patta-dhati-virajita-kati-deshah — He on whose waist shines a silk cloth; kare kankanam — on the arm a bracelet; vakshasi haram — on the chest a necklace; shravanayoh kundalam — in the ears earrings; pade nupuram — on the feet ankle-bells; bibhrat — wearing; urdhvi-kritya nibaddha-kuntala-bhara-protphulla-malli-srak-apidah — He on whose high-bound curling hair is a crown adorned with a garland of full-blown *mallika*; kah ayam gaura-nagara-varah — who is this indescribable ("Gaura" — golden in colour; "*nagara-vara*" — the crown of the supreme connoisseurs of *rasa*; that is, Gaura is Krishna Himself, the crown of the connoisseurs of *rasa*, enacting the pastime on the side of the *ashraya*); nijaih namabhih — with the *kirtana* of His own names; nrityan — dancing; kridati — plays.

**131.** Who is this Gaura, the foremost *nagara*, the crown of the knowers of *rasa*?[^04-2] His hips are adorned with silk cloth; on His arm is a bracelet, on His breast a necklace, in His ears earrings, on His feet ankle bells; He has gathered up and tied His abundant curls, crowning them with a garland of blossoming jasmine — He sports, dancing to His own names.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Sri Gaura-Krishna, dancing to the sankirtana of His own names.*

>> Who is this golden Purusha, the supreme *nagara*, the crown of the supreme connoisseurs of *rasa*, who, clothed in silk at the waist, with bracelets on His arms, a necklace on His chest, earrings in His ears, ankle-bells on His feet and a crown of full-blown garlands of jasmine (*mallika*) on His high-bound curling hair, plays, dancing to the *kirtana* of His names?

### Verse 132

देवा दुन्दुभिवादनं विदधिरे गन्धर्वमुख्या जगुः
सिद्धाः सन्ततपुष्पवृष्टिभिरिमां पृथ्वीं समाच्छादयन् ।
दिव्यस्तोत्रपरा महर्षिनिबहाः प्रीत्योपतस्थुर्निज-
प्रेमोन्मादिनि ताण्डवं रचयति श्रीगौरचन्द्रे भुवि ॥

*devā dundubhivādanaṁ vidadhire gandharvamukhyā jaguḥ*
*siddhāḥ santatapuṣpavṛṣṭibhirimāṁ pṛthvīṁ samācchādayan |*
*divyastotraparā maharṣinibahāḥ prītyopatasthurnija-*
*premonmādini tāṇḍavaṁ racayati śrīgauracandre bhuvi ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* nija-prema-unmadini shri-gauracandre — when Sri Gaurachandra, maddened with His own *prema*; bhuvi — on earth; tandavam — the dance; racayati [sati] — performed, that is, manifested; devah — the demigods; dundubhi-vadanam — the playing of the *dundubhis*; vidadhire — performed; gandharva-mukhyah — the chief Gandharvas; jaguh — sang; siddhah — the Siddhas; santata-pushpa-vrishtibhih — with an incessant rain of flowers; imam prithvim — this earth; samacchadayan — wholly covered; divya-stotra-parah — skilled in reciting charming hymns; maharshi-nivahah — hosts of great *rishis*; pritya — lovingly; upatasthuh — praised.

**132.** When Sri Gaurachandra, maddened with His own love, performed a vigorous dance on earth, the gods beat the kettledrums, the best of the Gandharvas sang, the Siddhas covered this earth with an unceasing rain of flowers, and hosts of great *rishis* lovingly drew near, offering wondrous hymns.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Gaurahari's dance gives joy and wonder to the gods, the Gandharvas and the Siddhas.*

>> When Sri Gaurachandra, maddened with His own *prema*, began His dance on earth, the demigods began to beat the *dundubhis*, the chief Gandharvas began *sankirtana*, the Siddhas covered the earth with an incessant rain of flowers, and hosts of great *rishis*, skilled in reciting charming hymns, began lovingly to offer praise.

### Verse 133

क्षणं हसति रोदिति क्षणमथ क्षणं मूर्च्छति
क्षणं लुठति धावति क्षणमथ क्षणं नृत्यति ।
क्षणं श्वसिति मुञ्चति क्षणमुदारहाहारुतिं
महाप्रणयसीधुना विहरतीह गौरो हरिः ॥

*kṣaṇaṁ hasati roditi kṣaṇamatha kṣaṇaṁ mūrcchati*
*kṣaṇaṁ luṭhati dhāvati kṣaṇamatha kṣaṇaṁ nṛtyati |*
*kṣaṇaṁ śvasiti muñcati kṣaṇamudārahāhārutiṁ*
*mahāpraṇayasīdhunā viharatīha gauro hariḥ ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* maha-pranaya-sidhuna gaurah harih — (*mahapranaya* — *mahabhava*; *sidhu* — the nectar *rasa*; marked by it, that is, Gaurahari full of *mahabhava*; the instrumental of attribute); iha — in this world; viharati — sports; kshanam hasati — now laughs; kshanam roditi — now weeps; atha kshanam murcchati — now loses consciousness; kshanam luthati — now rolls on the ground; kshanam dhavati — now runs; atha kshanam nrityati — now dances; kshanam shvasiti — now heaves long sighs; kshanam udara-haha-rutim muncati — now utters loud (great) cries of "ha! ha!"

**133.** Now He laughs, now weeps, now faints, now rolls on the ground, now runs, now dances, now sighs, now utters loud cries of "Ha! Ha!" — intoxicated with the wine of great love, Gaura-Hari sports here.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Sri Gaurahari, maddened by mahabhava.*

>> Immersed in the nectar *rasa* of *mahabhava*, Sri Gaurahari now laughs, now weeps, now loses consciousness, now rolls on the ground, now runs swiftly and now dances again, now heaves long sighs and now utters loud cries of "ha! ha!" — (thus, in various states) He sports in this world.

### Verse 134

अश्रूणां किमपि प्रवाहनिवहैः क्षौणीं पुरः पङ्किलां
कुर्वन् पाणितले निधाय बदरापाण्डुं कपोलस्थलीम् ।
आश्चर्यं लवणोदरोधसि वसन् शोणं दधानोंऽशुकं
गौरीभूय हरिः स्वयं वितनुते राधापदाब्जे रतिम् ॥

*aśrūṇāṁ kimapi pravāhanivahaiḥ kṣauṇīṁ puraḥ paṅkilāṁ*
*kurvan pāṇitale nidhāya badarāpāṇḍuṁ kapolasthalīm |*
*āścaryaṁ lavaṇodarodhasi vasan śoṇaṁ dadhānoṁ'śukaṁ*
*gaurībhūya hariḥ svayaṁ vitanute rādhāpadābje ratim ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* lavanoda-rodhasi — on the shore of the salt ocean; vasan — sitting; ashcaryam — wondrous; shonam amshukam — ("*shona*" — red, "*amshuka*" — garment); dadhanah — wearing (in the *shastras* it is said that Srimati Radhika too wears garments of a red colour — the colour of *anuraga* for Krishna); pani-tale — on the palm; badara-pandum — slightly pale like a *badari* fruit; kapola-sthalim — the cheek; nidhaya — placing; ashrunam — of tears; kim api pravaha-nivahaih — with certain wondrous streams; purah kshaunim — the ground before Him; pankilam — muddy; kurvan — making; svayam harih — Bhagavan Sri Krishna Himself; gauri-bhuya — having become Gauranga; radha-pada-abje — for Radha's lotus feet; ratim vitanute — spreads *rati*.

**134.** With indescribable floods of tears turning the ground before Him to mud, resting on His palm a cheek pale as a *badari* fruit, dwelling — how wondrous! — on the shore of the salt ocean and wearing a red garment, Hari Himself, having become golden, spreads love for the lotus feet of Radha.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Sri Gaurahari, who gives rise to […] attachment to the service of Radha.*

>> Sri Krishna Himself, having accepted the *bhava* on the side of the *ashraya*, in the form of Gauranga sits on the shore of the salt ocean, clothed in saffron garments, and, resting on His palm His cheek, slightly pale like a *badari* fruit (which indicates *vaivarnya*, change of colour), turning the ground before Him into mud with streams of tears, spreads *rati* for Radha's lotus feet.

### Verse 135

पादाघातरवैर्दिशो मुखरयन्नेत्राम्भसां बिन्दुभिः
क्षौणीं पङ्किलयन्नहो विशदयन्नट्टाट्टहासैर्नभः ।
चन्द्रज्योतिरुदारसुन्दरकटिव्यालोलशोणाम्बरः
को देवो लवणोदकूलकुसुमोद्याने मुदा नृत्यति ॥

*pādāghātaravairdiśo mukharayannetrāmbhasāṁ bindubhiḥ*
*kṣauṇīṁ paṅkilayannaho viśadayannaṭṭāṭṭahāsairnabhaḥ |*
*candrajyotirudārasundarakaṭivyālolaśoṇāmbaraḥ*
*ko devo lavaṇodakūlakusumodyāne mudā nṛtyati ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* aho — ah!; pada-aghata-ravaih — with the sounds of the strokes of His feet, that is, with stamping in the dance; dishah — the ten directions; mukharayan — making resound; netra-ambhasam — of tears; bindubhih — with drops; kshaunim — the earth; pankilayan — making muddy; atta-atta-hasaih — with peals of loud laughter; nabhah — the sky; vishadayan — making white; candra-jyotih — with a golden lustre like that of the moon; udara-sundara-kati-vyalola-shona-ambarah — wearing charming saffron garments hanging from His beautiful waist; lavanoda-kula-kusuma-udyane — in the flowering garden on the shore of the salt ocean; kah devah — what Purusha of pastimes; muda — with joy (with bliss); nrityati — dances?

**135.** Making the directions resound with the stamping of His feet, turning the ground to mud with drops of tears, — oh! — making the sky bright with peals of laughter, shining like the moon, His red garment swaying on His beautiful, graceful hips — what Lord is this who dances joyfully in the flower garden on the shore of the salt ocean?

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Sri Gaurahari, dancing the uddanda and displaying […] and many other anubhavas.*

>> Ah! What Purusha of pastimes is this, shining golden like the moon, in charming saffron garments hanging from His beautiful waist, who dances in the flowering garden on the shore of the salt ocean, making the ten directions resound with the sound of the stamping of His feet, turning the ground into mud with drops of tears and making the sky white with peals of loud laughter?

## Lament (shochaka)

### Verse 136

सर्वैराम्नायचूडामणिभिरपि न संलक्ष्यते यत्स्वरूपं
श्रीशब्रह्माद्यगम्या सुमधुरपदवी कापि यस्यातिरम्या ।
येनाकस्माज्जगच्छ्रीहरिरसमदिरामत्तमेतद्व्यधायि
श्रीमच्चैतन्यचन्द्रः स किमु मम गिरां गोचरश्चेतसो वा ॥

*sarvairāmnāyacūḍāmaṇibhirapi na saṁlakṣyate yatsvarūpaṁ*
*śrīśabrahmādyagamyā sumadhurapadavī kāpi yasyātiramyā |*
*yenākasmājjagacchrīharirasamadirāmattametadvyadhāyi*
*śrīmaccaitanyacandraḥ sa kimu mama girāṁ gocaraścetaso vā ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* sarvaih amnaya-cudamanibhih api — even by all the jewelled crowns of the *shrutis*, that is, by the Upanishads, the summit of the Vedas; yat-svarupam — whose essence; na samlakshyate — cannot be fully known; yasya — whose; kapi — never given before; sumadhura-padavi — supremely relishable path, that is, path of instruction; shri-isha-brahma-adi-agamya — ("Sri" — Lakshmi, the original guru of the [Sri-]*sampradaya*, worshipped by Sri Ramanujacharya, the teacher of *vishishta-advaita*; "Isha" — Rudra, worshipped by Sri Vishnusvami, the teacher of *shuddha-advaita*; "Brahma" — the original guru of the Brahma-*sampradaya*, worshipped by Sriman Madhvacharya, the teacher of *shuddha-dvaita*; "and the rest" — Sanaka, Sanandana and the others, the original gurus of the Catuhsana-*sampradaya*, worshipped by Nimbaditya; inaccessible even to them, that is, a path on which even they could not set foot); [atha ca — and yet]; ati-ramya — easy to travel, that is, very accessible to Gaura's devotees; yena — by that Gaurasundara; akasmat — unexpectedly; etat jagat — this world; shri-hari-rasa-madira-mattam vyadhayi — ("Sri" — Srimati Radhika, the embodiment of all the Lakshmis, "Hari" — Krishna; was intoxicated with the wine of the *rasa* of the *prema* of Sri Sri Radha-Krishna); sah shrimat caitanya-candrah — that Sri Chaitanya-chandra; mama giram cetasah va — of my words and mind; gocarah kimu [syat] — will He become accessible?

**136.** Whose nature even the crest-jewels of all the Vedas cannot discern; whose sweetest path, inaccessible even to Lakshmi, Shiva, Brahma and the others, is yet indescribably beautiful and easily accessible to those on whom His merciful glance has fallen; who suddenly intoxicated this world with the wine of the *rasa* of Sri Radha and Hari — can that Sri Chaitanya-chandra be within the reach of my words or my mind?

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *The prema which the Upanishads and the rest seek and which is hard to comprehend even for the four sampradayas — of Sri, Brahma, Rudra and [Sanaka] — is easily accessible to Gaura's devotees.*

>> He whose essence even the Upanishads — the jewelled necklace on the crown of all the *shrutis* — cannot fully indicate; He whose never-before-given, supremely relishable path is hard to comprehend even for Sri, Brahma, Rudra and the others — that is, the path of the elevated *ujjvala-prema* is unknown not only to the founders of the *sampradayas* of Sri, Brahma, Rudra and Sanaka but even to their original gurus — but which is very easy [to walk] for those who have received His merciful glance, that is, which is very accessible to Gaura's devotees; He who suddenly intoxicated this world with the wine of the *rasa* of the *prema* of Sri Radha and Krishna — will that Chaitanya-chandra, radiating supreme beauty, become accessible to my words and mind?

### Verse 137

जाड्यं कर्मसु कुत्रचिज्जपतपोयोगादिकं कुत्रचिद्
गोविन्दार्चनविक्रियः क्वचिदपि ज्ञानाभिमानः क्वचित् ।
श्रीभक्तिः क्वचिदुज्ज्वलापि च हरेर्वाङ्मात्र एव स्थिता
हा चैतन्य कुतो गतोऽसि पदवी कुत्रापि ते नेक्ष्यते ॥

*jāḍyaṁ karmasu kutracijjapatapoyogādikaṁ kutracid*
*govindārcanavikriyaḥ kvacidapi jñānābhimānaḥ kvacit |*
*śrībhaktiḥ kvacidujjvalāpi ca harervāṅmātra eva sthitā*
*hā caitanya kuto gato'si padavī kutrāpi te nekṣyate ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* ha caitanya — O Sri Chaitanya!; kutah gatah asi — where have You gone?; te padavi — Your (pure, secret path of the *bhakti* of the elevated *ujjvala-rasa*); kutrapi — in any *sampradaya*; na ikshyate — is not seen; kutracit — in one *sampradaya*; karmasu jadyam — the inertness of *karma*; kutracit japa-tapo-yoga-adikam — in another *sampradaya* austerity, *japa*, yoga and the like; kvacit api govinda-arcana-vikriyah — in another the rules of the worship of Govinda on the path of *arcana*; kvacit jnana-abhimanah — in another *bhakti* mixed with knowledge; kvacit ca — and somewhere; hareh — of Sri Hari; ujjvala api shri-bhaktih — even *ujjvala-bhakti*; vak-matre eva sthita — abides only in words, that is, no one practises *bhakti* in deed.

**137.** Somewhere there is dullness in rituals, somewhere *japa*, austerity, yoga and so on, somewhere ritualistic worship of Govinda, somewhere pride of knowledge; and the sacred *bhakti* of Hari, though radiant, somewhere remains only in words. Ah, Chaitanya, where have You gone? Your path is seen nowhere!

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *With the concealment of Gaurahari's pastimes, the path of bhakti again fell into disorder.*

>> O Sri Chaitanya! Where have You gone! Your pure, secret path of the *bhakti* of the elevated *ujjvala-rasa* is no longer seen in any *sampradaya*. In one *sampradaya* there is the inertness of *karma*, in another austerity, *japa*, yoga and the like, in a third the rules of the worship of Govinda on the path of *arcana*, in a fourth *bhakti* mixed with knowledge, and somewhere even *ujjvala-bhakti* abides only in words, without practice.

### Verse 138

अभिव्यक्तो यत्र द्रुतकनकगौरो हरिरभू-
न्महिम्ना तस्यैव प्रणयरसमग्नं जगदभूत् ।
अभूदुच्चैरुच्चैस्तुमुलहरिसङ्कीर्तनविधिः
स कालः किं भूयोऽप्यहह परिवर्तेत मधुरः ॥

*abhivyakto yatra drutakanakagauro harirabhū-*
*nmahimnā tasyaiva praṇayarasamagnaṁ jagadabhūt |*
*abhūduccairuccaistumulaharisaṅkīrtanavidhiḥ*
*sa kālaḥ kiṁ bhūyo'pyahaha parivarteta madhuraḥ ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* yatra — at the time when; druta-kanaka-gaurah harih — Sri Gaurahari, golden like molten gold; abhivyaktah abhut — became visible in this world; [tada — then]; tasya eva mahimna — by His very greatness; jagat — the earth; pranaya-rasa-magnam abhut — was immersed in the *rasa* of *prema*; [ca] uccaih uccaih tumula-hari-sankirtana-vidhih — the loudly resounding, tumultuous Krishna-*sankirtana*; abhut — arose; ahaha — alas!; sah madhurah kalah — that sweet time; api kim bhuyah parivarteta — will it ever return?

**138.** When Hari appeared, golden as molten gold, by His glory alone the world was immersed in the *rasa* of love, and everywhere the *sankirtana* of Hari thundered loudly, loudly — ah, will that sweet time ever return?

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *The heartache of the devotees at the concealment of Gaurahari's pastimes.*

>> At the time when Sri Gaurahari, shining like molten gold, became visible in this world, by His power the earth was immersed in the *rasa* of *prema*, and a tumultuous Krishna-*sankirtana* resounded loudly. Alas! Will that sweet time ever return?

### Verse 139

सैवेयं भुवि धन्यगौडनगरी वेलापि सैवाम्बुधेः
सोऽयं श्रीपुरुषोत्तमो मधुपतेस्तान्येव नामानि तु ।
नो कुत्रापि निरीक्ष्यते हरि हरि प्रेमोत्सवस्तादृशो
हा चैतन्य कृपानिधान तव किं वीक्ष्ये पुनर्वैभवम् ॥

*saiveyaṁ bhuvi dhanyagauḍanagarī velāpi saivāmbudheḥ*
*so'yaṁ śrīpuruṣottamo madhupatestānyeva nāmāni tu |*
*no kutrāpi nirīkṣyate hari hari premotsavastādṛśo*
*hā caitanya kṛpānidhāna tava kiṁ vīkṣye punarvaibhavam ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* bhuvi — on earth; sa eva iyam dhanya-gauda-nagari — the same blessed city of Gauda; sa eva ambudheh vela api — the same shore of the ocean with its gardens and groves; sah ayam shri-purushottamah — the same Sri Purushottama, Sri Jagannathadeva; madhupateh — of Sri Krishna; tani eva namani tu — the same names "Hare Krishna" and the rest; [vartate — exist]; hari hari — Hari! Hari! (a cry of grief); kutrapi — nowhere; tadrishah prema-utsavah — that festival of the bliss of *prema*; no nirikshyate — is seen; ha caitanya [ha] kripa-nidhana — O Chaitanya! O treasury of mercy!; tava vaibhavam — Your splendour; punah kim vikshye — shall I see again?

**139.** This is the same blessed capital of Gauda, the same shore of the ocean, the same Sri Purushottama (Jagannatha), the same names of Madhupati — but, Hari, Hari! — such a festival of love is seen nowhere. Ah, Chaitanya, treasury of mercy, shall I ever see Your glory again?

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Separation from Gaura at the sight of […], Sri Navadvipa, the holy name of Sri Hari and the other things that awaken the mood of Gaurahari.*

>> On earth there is the same blessed city of Gauda, the same shore of the ocean with its gardens and groves, the same Sri Purushottama, and the same names of Sri Krishna exist — "Hare Krishna" and the rest. Hari! Hari! But nowhere is that festival of the bliss of *prema* to be seen. O Chaitanya, O treasury of mercy, will my eyes behold Your splendour again?

### Verse 140

यदि निगदितमीनाद्यंशवद्गौरचन्द्रो
न तदपि स हि कश्चिच्छक्तिलीलाविकाशः ।
अतुलसकलशक्त्याश्चर्यलीलाप्रकाशै-
रनधिगतमहत्त्वः पूर्ण एवावतीर्णः ॥

*yadi nigaditamīnādyaṁśavadgauracandro*
*na tadapi sa hi kaścicchaktilīlāvikāśaḥ |*
*atulasakalaśaktyāścaryalīlāprakāśai-*
*ranadhigatamahattvaḥ pūrṇa evāvatīrṇaḥ ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* gauracandrah nigadita-mina-adi-amshavat yadi api [syat] — if you say that Gaurachandra is like the partial *avataras* — the Fish, the Boar and the others — of whom the *shrutis* speak; tat na hi — He certainly is not so; sah — they (the partial *avataras*, Matsya and the rest); kashcit shakti-lila-vikashah — the manifestation of some particular potency and pastime; [ayam gauracandrah — this Gaurachandra]; anadhigata-mahattvah — He whose greatness is unfathomed; atula-sakala-shakti-ashcarya-lila-prakashaih — (*atula* — incomparable; by the manifestation of wondrous pastimes endowed with all potencies); purnah eva — certainly in fullness; avatirnah — has descended.

**140.** If one says that Gaurachandra is like the partial incarnations — the Fish and the rest — He is not so: for they are only the manifestation of some potency and pastime. But He, whose greatness is unfathomable, revealing wondrous pastimes with the matchless fullness of all potencies, descended as the complete [Lord].

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Gaurahari is not a partial avatara but the complete Bhagavan Himself, the son of the lord of Vraja.*

>> If you say that Gaurachandra is like the partial *avataras* — the Fish and the others of whom the *shrutis* speak — in fact He is not so, for the partial *avataras*, Matsya and the others, are only the manifestation of some particular potency and pastime. But this Gaurachandra, whose greatness is unfathomed, displaying a wondrous pastime endowed with the incomparable fullness of all potencies, has certainly descended in fullness.

### Verse 141

ब्रह्मेशादिमहाश्चर्यमहिमापि महाप्रभुः ।
मुग्धबालोदितं श्रुत्वा स्निग्धोऽवश्यं भविष्यति ॥

*brahmeśādimahāścaryamahimāpi mahāprabhuḥ |*
*mugdhabāloditaṁ śrutvā snigdho'vaśyaṁ bhaviṣyati ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* brahma-isha-adi-maha-ashcarya-mahima api — He whose might, having neither equal nor superior, is more wondrous even than that of Sri Narayana, the original [source] of Brahma and Maheshvara; mahaprabhuh — Sriman Mahaprabhu; mugdha-bala-uditam — words befitting a foolish child; shrutva — having heard; avashyam — surely; snigdhah bhavishyati — will become affectionate, that is, will mercifully bestow tender love on me.

**141.** Though Mahaprabhu has a glory more wondrous even than that of the Original One, the source of Brahma and Shiva, on hearing the prattle of an innocent child He will surely be tender.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *Bhagavan Gaurahari Himself is more primeval even than Shiva, Brahma and the rest, the possessor of inconceivable power.*

>> Sriman Mahaprabhu, whose might, having neither equal nor superior, is more wondrous even than Sri Narayana — the original cause of Shiva and Brahma — on hearing these words befitting a foolish child, will surely become affectionate, that is, will mercifully bestow His tender love on me.

### Verse 142

दृष्टं न शास्त्रं गुरवो न दृष्टा
विवेचितं नापि बुधैः सुबुद्ध्या ।
यथा तथा जल्पतु बालभावा-
त्तथैव मे गौरहरिः प्रसीदतु ॥

*dṛṣṭaṁ na śāstraṁ guravo na dṛṣṭā*
*vivecitaṁ nāpi budhaiḥ subuddhyā |*
*yathā tathā jalpatu bālabhāvā-*
*ttathaiva me gaurahariḥ prasīdatu ||*

*Word for word (Bhaktisiddhanta's anvaya):* [maya — by me]; shastram na drishtam — the *shastras* have not been seen (not studied); guravah — the teachers of many *sampradayas*; na drishtah — have not been seen, that is, I have not received instruction from them; [su]buddhya — with […] intelligence; budhaih api — even with *panditas* learned in the *shastras*; na vivecitam — has there been discussion; [tathapi shri-gaurahari-vishayartha mama vani — and yet my words about Gaurahari]; bala-bhavat — out of childishness (in the *anuvada*: out of foolishness); yatha tatha [va] jalpatu — let them say whatever they may (however they may describe); gaura-harih — Gaurasundara; tatha eva — by that very means; me prasidatu — may He be pleased with me.

**142.** I have not seen the shastras, I have not seen gurus, the wise have not examined [my words] with their keen intellect — so however I may prattle like a child, may Gaura-Hari be pleased with me.

>> **Gaudiya-bhashya.** *The only thing worth praying for is Gaurahari's mercy.*

>> I have not seen [the *shastras*], I have not received instruction from many teachers, I have not discussed the *shastras* with *panditas* learned in them; and yet, however my words, spoken in foolishness, may describe Gaurahari, may Sri Gaurahari be pleased with me.

*Thus ends the Sri Sri Chaitanya-chandramrita in twelve sections, composed by Shripada Srimat Prabodhananda Sarasvati — the jewel in the crown of the family of the tridandi-gosvamis, the best of wandering teachers, an eminent associate of Gaura.*

>> Thus ends the *Sri Sri Chaitanya-chandramrita* in twelve sections, composed by Srimat Prabodhananda Sarasvati-pada, the crest-jewel of the line of *tridandi-gosvamis*, the best of wandering acharyas, the foremost of Gaura's associates.

>> Thus ends the *Gaudiya-bhashya* on the *Sri Chaitanya-chandramrita*.

[^04-1]: "Knowers of non-duality" (*advaita*) — the experts in non-dualism (Advaita Vedanta); this is also how Bhaktisiddhanta explains the word.

[^04-2]: *Nagara-vara* — according to Bhaktisiddhanta, "the crown of the highest knowers of *rasa*": Gaura is Krishna Himself, enacting His pastime on the side of the *ashraya* (tasting the feelings of Radha). It does not mean a worldly "gallant lover," nor does it support the doctrine of *gaura-nagari*, which Bhaktisiddhanta rejected.
